P·L EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM JOANNEM 114

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 114 ·

EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM JOANNEM

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 114, coll. 355–426  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

PROLOGUS.

  • 355B

Omnibus divinae Scripturae paginis, Evangelium excellit, quia quod lex et prophetae futurum praedixerunt, hoc completum dicit Evangelium. Inter ipsos autem evangeliorum scriptores Joannes eminet in divinorum mysteriorum profunditate, qui a tempore Dominicae Ascensionis per annos sexaginta quinque verbum Dei absque adminiculo scribendi usque ad ultima Domitiani praedicavit tempora. Sed occiso Domitiano, cum permittente Nerva de exsilio redisset Ephesum, compulsus ab episcopis Asiae de coaeterna Patri divinitate Christi scripsit adversus haereticos, qui eo absente irruperant in ejus ecclesias, qui Christum ante Mariam fuisse negabant. Unde merito in figura quatuor animalium aquilae volanti comparatur, 355Cquae volat altius caeteris animalibus, et solis radios irreverberatis aspicit luminibus. Caeteri quippe evangelistae, qui temporalem Christi nativitatem et temporalia ejus facta, quae gessit in homine sufficienter exponunt, et de divinitate pauca dixerunt, quasi animalia gressibilia, cum Domino ambulant in terra. Hic autem pauca de temporalibus ejus gestis edisserens, sed divinitatis potentiam sublimius contemplans, cum Domino ad coelum volat. Qui enim supra pectus Domini in coena recubuit, coelestis haustum sapientiae caeteris excellentius de ipso Dominici pectoris fonte potavit. Legerat siquidem evangelia trium evangelistarum, et approbaverat fidem eorum et veritatem in quibus deesse vidit aliqua gestarum rerum historiae, et ea maxime quae Dominus 355Dgessit primo praedicationis suae tempore, scilicet, antequam Joannes Baptista clauderetur in carcere. Haec ergo quasi dimissa ab illis, scribit Joannes quae fecit Jesus antequam Joannes traderetur, sed maxime divinitatem Christi et Trinitatis mysterium commendare curavit. Tres siquidem alii evangelistae, dicta et facta Domini temporalia, quae ad informandos mores vitae praesentis maxime valent, copiosius consecuti, circa activam vitam sunt versati. In qua laboratur, ut cor mundetur ad videndum Deum. Joannes vero pauca Domini facta dicit, verba vero 356BDomini quae Trinitatis unitatem, et vitae aeternae felicitatem insinuant, diligentius conscribit, et sic in contemplativa vita virtute commendanda, intentionem suam et praedicationem tenuit. In qua contemplativa vacatur, ut Deus videatur. Iste siquidem est Joannes quem Dominus de fluctivaga nuptiarum tempestate vocavit, et cui matrem virginem virgini commendavit.

PROLOGUE.

  • 355B

The Gospel excels all the pages of Holy Scripture, because what the law and the prophets foretold as to come, this the Gospel declares to be fulfilled. But among the writers of the gospels themselves John stands out in the profundity of the divine mysteries: he who, from the time of the Lord's Ascension, for sixty-five years preached the word of God without the aid of writing, down to the last times of Domitian. But when Domitian had been slain, and he had returned from exile to Ephesus by Nerva's permission, being compelled by the bishops of Asia he wrote of the divinity of Christ, coeternal with the Father, against the heretics who in his absence had broken in upon his churches, and who denied that Christ had existed before Mary. Whence he is deservedly compared, in the figure of the four living creatures, to the flying eagle, 355Cwhich flies higher than the other creatures and looks upon the rays of the sun with eyes unstruck by them. For the other evangelists, who sufficiently set forth the temporal nativity of Christ and his temporal deeds, which he wrought in his manhood, and said few things of his divinity, walk with the Lord upon the earth like creatures that go on foot. But this one, expounding few things of his temporal deeds, yet contemplating more loftily the power of his divinity, flies with the Lord to heaven. For he who reclined upon the Lord's breast at the supper drank the draught of heavenly wisdom more excellently than the rest from that very fount of the Lord's breast. He had read, indeed, the gospels of the three evangelists, and had approved their faith and truth, in which he saw that some things of the history of the deeds done were wanting, and chiefly those which the Lord 355Dwrought in the first season of his preaching, that is, before John the Baptist was shut up in prison. These things, therefore, as left aside by them, John writes: what Jesus did before John was delivered up; but above all he took care to commend the divinity of Christ and the mystery of the Trinity. For the three other evangelists, having followed out more copiously the temporal sayings and deeds of the Lord, which avail most for the shaping of the manners of the present life, were occupied about the active life. In which there is labour, that the heart may be cleansed for the seeing of God. But John tells few deeds of the Lord, while the words 356Bof the Lord which intimate the unity of the Trinity and the felicity of eternal life he writes down more diligently; and thus, in commending the virtue of the contemplative life, he held to his own purpose and preaching. In which contemplative life there is leisure, that God may be seen. For this is that John whom the Lord called out of the wave-tossed storm of wedlock, and to whom, a virgin, he commended his virgin mother.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- In principio erat Verbum. Contra eos qui propter temporalem Christi nativitatem dicebant Christum non semper fuisse, incipit de aeternitate Verbi dicens: In principio erat Verbum.

VERS. 3.-- Omnia per ipsum facta sunt. (AUG.) Non est creatura per quem omnis creatura facta est, etc., 356Cusque ad quae concordant cuncti potentiae Divinitatis.

Et sine ipso factum est nihil. (ORIG.) Ita distinguit: Et sine ipso factum est nihil. Ordo: Nihil est quod factum est sine ipso.

VERS. 4.-- Quod factum est, erat vita in ipso. (AUG.) Joannes Chrysostomus: Et sine ipso factum est nihil. Quod factum est. Hilarius: Sine ipso factum est nihil, quod factum est in ipso.

VERS. 6.-- Fuit homo missus. Postquam sublimi volatu ultra omnem naturam in altitudinem theologiae ascendit, quia Verbum apud Patrem semper fuisse contemplatus est, mox ad humilitatem humanae naturae, et specialiter praecursoris Verbi descendit contra illos qui dicebant humanam naturam in 356Dprimo parente ex toto perditam et ad nihilum redactam. Homo missus. Homo, non angelus, ut haeretici volunt, missus a Deo. Ideo potuit dicere de Deo.

VERS. 9.-- Erat lux vera. Omnis homo dicitur illa natura in homine, quae ad imaginem et ad similitudinem Dei facta est. Alia omnia quae sunt in homine inferiores partes hominis sunt, et quodammodo extra hominem sunt. In illa scilicet, anima velut in quodam mundo superiori vera lux lucet, et in illum mundum venientes, et per gratiam renascentes illuminat, quia 357Aquorum vita et conversatio in coelis est, quasi in alio mundo sunt.

VERS. 11.-- Et sui eum non receperunt. Ab initio mundi nullum tempus erat in quo receptores divini verbi non essent, unde addit: Quotquot autem receperunt.

VERS. 12.-- Filios Dei fieri. Mirabilis potestas ut qui filii diaboli erant, et filii Dei et fratres Christi per eum liberati dicantur, quo autem ordine filii Dei fiant, et quomodo haec generatio distat a carnali, subditur: Qui non ex sanguinibus.

VERS. 14.-- Et Verbum caro factum est. Ut autem secure credamus homines nasci ex Deo, subdit Deum nasci ex homine, ex hac susceptione infirmitatis sanatur nostra infirmitas, ut possimus filii Dei fieri. 357BEt hoc est: Et Verbum caro factum est. Quod superius erat, inferius descendit. Quid mirum: si quod erat inferius, in id quod superius est ascendit? Nec mirum, si homines nascuntur ex Deo, quia Deus ex homine est natus. Plenum gratiae et veritatis. Homo Christus plenus fuit gratia, quia nullis praecedentibus meritis ex quo conceptus est, fuit Deitas in eo implens hominem illum omni plenitudine redundante, in omnes sanctos, unde et mater ejus non tantum dicitur mater hominis, sed et mater Dei. Nec mirum, si dicitur, homo etiam Deus, cum Deus dicatur etiam homo, haec autem gratia dicitur et veritas, quia sic olim promissum est, et modo exhibitum, scilicet, ut Deus sit in homine implens eum omni bono, et per eum suos.

357CVERS. 15.-- Hic erat quem dixi. Per demonstrativum pronomen innuitur Christum affuisse in illo loco. Saepe enim ipse Dominus adhuc ignotus antequam baptizaretur et praedicaret, ad Joannem solitus erat venire. Qui post me venturus est. Qui post me natus, post me praedicaturus, baptizaturus, moriturus. Non quod jam non esset natus, quando haec dixit, quia etiam baptizatus: sed venturus in praedicatione, in morte, sed non in resurrectione. Ante me factus est, quia prior me erat. Ex Graeco, coram me factus est, id est, apparuit mihi quidem, scilicet, quia prior me erat.

VERS. 16.-- Et de plenitudine. Exposito testimonio praecursoris, redit evangelista ad testimonium 357Dsuae assertionis. Quasi dicat: Verbum caro factum est: vidimus gloriam ejus plenam gratiae et veritatis, et de plenitudine ejus nos omnes accepimus. Gratiam, ut ipsum credamus, et veritatem qua illum intelligemus. Accepimus. (AUG.) Non solum gratiam, quae plane gratis datur, non pro merito ut fidem quae per dilectionem operatur, ex qua justus vivit, sed pro ista, gratiam, id est beatitudinem aeternam, quae et merces justitiae dicitur, et etiam gratiam, quia meritum ex gratia. Gratiam pro gratia. Gratia in conceptione boni per gratiam datur. Gratia simpliciter quae non est meriti, si postea cum illa operamur, in bonis consuescimus, et tunc datur gratia pro gratia priori.

358AVERS. 17.-- Per Moysen. Per Moysen servum praenuntiatio, per Dominum Jesum salutis impletio.

VERS. 18.-- Deum nemo vidit unquam. Id est, nullus purus homo vivens in hoc corpore vidit Deum ut est, unde dictum est Moysi: Non videbit me homo et vivet (Exod. XXXIII), id est, nemo vivens in corpore videbit divinitatem meam. Unde ista adjecit Deus Moysi: Sta in foramine petrae, et cum pertransiero videbis posteriora mea (Ibid.). Petra, id est, Christus, foramen, crucis patibulum, pertransiero, id est, transiero de mundo ad Patrem. Posteriora, ortus ejus, passio, resurrectio, ascensio, per quae credita ad ejus beatitudinem et visionem pervenimus. Quod legitur Deum apparuisse hominibus sive visibiliter 358Bcarnalibus oculis, sive invisibiliter propheticis visionibus per subjectam creaturam, intelligendum est, visiones enim prophetarum quadam spirituali creatura factae sunt, quibus Deum vidisse perhibentur.

Unigenitus Filius, etc. Quae sit summa gratiae et veritatis, breviter subdit, scilicet cognitio ipsius Trinitatis, unde: Haec est vita aeterna, ut cognoscant te unum verum Deum, et quem misisti Jesum Christum. Quae in hac vita non plene est, quia nemo in carne vidit Deum ut est. Unde nec ipse Moyses nedum per eum data sit gratia, sicut etiam ipse post omnia visa in typo Dei dixit: Ostende mihi teipsum (Exod. XXXIII), sed Filius qui est in secreto Patris narravit, quia nemo novit Patrem nisi Filius, et cui voluerit Filius 358Crevelare (Luc. X). Ipse narrat suis quid de Trinitate deitatis sentiendum sit, quomodo ad eam perveniendum, et ad ipsam introducit.

VERS. 19.-- Et hoc. Et haec et caetera sequentia testimonia Joannis, exhibita sunt post baptizatum Christum, cum jam Jesus veniret invitare discipulos, quos Joannes suis testimoniis mittit ad eum. Testimonium contra hoc, quod occultus Christus: occultus quia humilis: humilis ut possit sperni et occidi, ut redimeret mundum. Tu quis es. Putabant Judaei Joannem esse Christum, qui in lege promittebatur. Alii putabant eum esse Eliam, propter nimiam abstinentiam et castitatem, et solitariam vitam, et asperrimam delictorum reprehensionem, et durissimum futurae vindictae terrorem. Alii dicebant eum esse unum de 358Dprophetis resuscitatum, propter prophetiae gratiam.

VERS. 20.-- Confessus est. Confessus est, ut postea dicit, non quod erat Christus. Non negavit quod erat, id est, quod esset praecursor, ut post interrogatus dicit: Ego vox clamantis. Ordo verborum, et confessus est et non negavit quando miserunt.

VERS. 21.-- Elias es tu? Cum omnes scirent nomen Christi, sciebant etiam quod Elias praecessurus sit eum. Sed ipse Elias praecessurus, in potestate judicaturum, Joannes figuraliter Elias eumdem judicandum, ut sint duo praecones sicut duo adventus ejusdem, unde: Non sum Elias, id est, non praeco: potentiam indicans; sed in quam offenditis ostendo 359Ahumilitatem: quasi dicat: Servite humili antequam excelsus judicet. Ecce nec ego Christus, nec a parte potentiae judex.

VERS. 23.-- Ego vox clamantis in deserto. Non dicit, Ego sum homo, vel Joannes, vel filius Zachariae. Non considerat humanam subsistentiam vel generationem, sed ultra haec omnia exaltatus praecursor Verbi, deserit omnia quae intra mundum continentur. Ascendit in altum factus vox Verbi, nullam in se substantiam fatetur praeter abundantiam gratiae, qua excedit omnem creaturam, ut sit vox Verbi. Vox est interpres animi, animus autem, id est intellectus omnium est Filius Dei. Joannes ergo vox est et interpres hujus animi, quia primo mundo eum demonstravit: Ecce agnus Dei. Isaias, Hoc de 359BJoanne prophetavit: Ego vox clamantis in deserto, in qua verbum sonat, id est prophetia, et ostensio et lucerna verbi praesentis.

VERS. 24.-- Ex Pharisaeis. Id est, ex principibus Judaeorum, qui doctrinam non quaerunt, sed invident. Qui tamen ad Joannem ideo veniunt, quia audiunt eum praedicasse communem omnium resurrectionem, quam et ipsi credebant, unde et ipsi in multis Christo consentiunt. Paulum quoque apostolum in multis audierunt.

VERS. 25.-- Quid ergo baptizas. Audiebant Pharisaei in prophetis Christum venturum et baptizaturum, scientes Jordanem figuram baptismi gessisse, eumque Eliam et Elisaeum siccis pedibus transisse, figuramque baptismi in Elia et Elisaeo non dubitabant 359Cpraecessisse, unde et nunc eos surrexisse putabant et baptizasse, unde nec interrogant, Es tu unus prophetarum? sed solummodo: Es tu propheta? ille videlicet qui praefiguravit baptismum.

VERS. 26.-- Ego baptizo in aqua. Non imputetur audaciae quod ago, quia corpora abluo aqua, non aufero peccata, ut sicut nascendo et praedicando praecursor sum, sic et baptizando, sed iste medius inter vos est qui peccata tollit.

VERS. 27.-- Cujus ego non sum dignus, etc. Non ait non solvam. Solvit enim, quoniam ipsum manifestavit et de divinitate et humanitate ipsius multa aperuit; sed, Non sum dignus, quia indignum ad hoc agendum se reputat, in quo simpliciter indicatur humilitas.

359DVERS. 28.-- Haec in Bethania. Duae sunt Bethaniae. Una trans Jordanem, altera circa non longe ab Hierusalem, ubi Lazarus suscitatus est. Illa quae trans Jordanem est significat humanam naturam sub lege naturali ante fluenta gratiae, quae gratia post incarnationem Christi abundans fuit, quae natura ad hoc creata erat; ut legi naturae obediret in qua Joannes baptizat, quia a peccato non liberat. Baptizat ultra fluenta divinorum donorum, quae nondum in Christo inchoaverant in humanam naturam descendere. Illa quae est citra Jordanem significat eamdem humanam naturam obedientem sub lege gratiae, quae est proxima Hierusalem, id est visioni pacis, per charitatem, 360Ain qua Christus mortuum suscitat quia a peccato liberat.

VERS. 29.-- Altera die vidit Joannes Jesum. Typice. Joannes, id est populus gratiae, altera die, id est post legem, cognoscit agnum quo redimitur. Venientem ad se, et ait. Non solum gressibus corporis, sed etiam interioris suae contemplationis accessibus, quia dignatus est a Joanne cognosci secundum divinitatem et humanitatem. Ecce agnus Dei, etc. Tria ministrat agnus possidentibus se, lac simplicis doctrinae quo parvuli nutriuntur, lanam, id est incrementa virtutum, esum carnis suae. Peccata mundi. Peccatum mundi dicitur originale peccatum quod est commune totius mundi quo tota humana natura simul et semel condita leges divinas per inobedientiam 360Btransgressa est in paradiso, quod originale, singulaque superaddita gratia relaxat.

VERS. 30.-- Post me venit vir. Christus est vir in quo nobis congruit et pro nobis solvit, et Filius Dei in quo Patri congruit et peccata dimittendo reconciliat nos ei.

VERS. 31.-- Ut manifestetur. Hic est agnus qui tollit peccata, quod non ego quem etiam melius modo novi, sed propterea baptizo ut manifestetur Israeli. Ne enim propter insolentiam minus baptizarentur a Christo in spiritu, usu baptizandi ei viam paravit, unde postquam ad ipsum, qui via est, ventum est, baptismus ejus cessat. In baptismo etiam Joannis manifestatur humilitas Christi, quae est impletio totius justitiae; sicut enim opus est Christum 360Cnasci et mori, sic et baptizari. Si enim ipse qui non eget a servo baptizatur, non dedignentur quantumcunque sapientes et sancti a Domino baptizari, sine quo permanent in peccatis. Baptizans. Christus a Joanne baptizatur, ut indicetur omnis humilitas quae est omnis justitia. Sed non solus, ne baptisma Joannis sanctius et majus, in quo Deus homo baptizatus est, videretur baptismo Christi, in quo homines baptizantur. Baptizantur autem alii a Joanne, ut usus baptizandi praeparet viam baptismo Christi.

VERS. 32.-- Et testimonium. Sicut dixit se venisse ut manifestaret Christum, ita perhibuit testimonium, scilicet vidit spiritum. In quo didicit, quod prius nescivit: venerat etiam praedicare poenitentiam, id 360Dest, facere praeparationem praedicationis Christi, sed tantum de praeparatione baptismi ponit singulariter, quia baptismus Christi est summum sacramentum inter alia. Spiritum descendentem. Non quod modo primum descenderit, sed ut per hoc descendere ostendatur, et manere in baptizatis aqua et non aliter, et ut Joannes per eum de Christo excellentius intelligeret.

VERS. 33.-- Et ego nesciebam. (AUG.) Visa columba, dicit se per eam didicisse, quod prius nesciebat, etc., usque ad unde Apostolus: In labore et mente sunt differentes, in operatione indifferentes.

(HIER.) Ostendit Joannem non scire per baptismum Christi, id est mundum esse redemptum, nam sciebat 361Aeum Filium Dei esse, et natum in carne. Aug. dicit Joannem ignorasse hoc tantum utrum sibi retineat vim baptizandi, an alicui suorum tribuat. Et manentem. (AUG.) Spiritualiter dicitur spiritum manere in Christo, a quo nunquam per peccatum recessit, in quo semper mirabiliter vixit et mirabiliter operatus est; in sanctos venit, et per peccata recedit. Manet tamen semper in eis ad aliquid, ut Christus eis promisit, scilicet ut bonis semper insistant. Ad aliquid non manet, scilicet ut ex eo prophetent, vel miracula faciant, et ideo hoc quod dicit est primum signum agnoscendi Christum.

Qui baptizat. Baptizat Christus in spiritu, non solum remittendo peccata in aqua, sed et post purgando, et amorem accendendo, et donis implendo.

361BHic est Filius Dei. Hic testatur Filium Dei quem superius virum vocabat: ut ab eo habeatur testimonium utriusque naturae.

VERS. 35.-- Altera die iterum stabat Joannes. Typice. Stat Joannes, cessat lex, tamen testimonium perhibens Christo. Ambulat Jesus, id est gratia hinc inde colligens.

VERS. 37.-- Secuti sunt Jesum. Secuti sunt, ut eum jam potius quam Joannem audirent, non tamen adhuc ei penitus adhaerentes, sed ut videant ubi habitat, ut assidue ad eum veniant, et ab eo erudiantur, et postea perfecte sunt secuti, quando eos de navi vocavit. Duo sequuntur, quia qui sequuntur gemina charitate debent accendi.

VERS. 38.-- Conversus. Quasi a tergo viderant, sed 361Cquasi faciem praebuit illis dum de majestate descendit, ut posset videri, dum pro umbra legis Evangelii lucem exhibuit. Quid quaeritis? Interrogat non ignorans, sed ut mercedem habeant respondentes, qui rem, non personam, quid, non quem, ne se videatur ostendere, illi personam respondent. Ubi habitas? Mystice. Volunt ostendi sibi in quibus Christus habitet, ut se illis assimilent. Vel, qui incarnationem vident, pie quaerunt ostendi sibi aeternam mansionem quomodo sit apud Patrem.

VERS. 39.-- Venite. Quasi sermone non potest explicari, sed opere demonstratur, venite credendo et operando, et intelligetis; venite testimonio Joannis et a lege recedite, et gratiam recipietis. Die illo. Ostendit Novum Testamentum, sive praesentem vitam. Hora 361Dautem erat quasi decima. Quia ex lege venerant, decalogus ad Christum misit, hora decima quaerunt ab eo doceri, quia non alius docet legem, nisi qui dedit eam. Docet autem cum misericordia et implentur quae implere non poterant, vel hora decima vesperam mundi indicat.

VERS. 40.-- Frater Simonis Petri, unus ex duobus qui audierant a Joanne, et secuti fuerant. Frater junior natu prior credit, non enim est ordo fidei inanis. Forte ad dignitatem Andreas dicitur esse frater Petri, super quem, id est super cujus fidem fundanda erat Ecclesia.

VERS. 41.-- Invenit hic primum. Vera pietas statim inventum thesaurum nuntiat fratri, ut sicut est 362Asanguine, sit et germanus in fide. Invenimus Messiam, etc. Christus Graece, Messias Hebraice, unctus Latine, vere enim unctus est a Spiritu sancto specialiter, a qua unctione Christiani appellantur. Quod Jacob erexit lapidem post visionem in titulum quem olei superfusio unxit, jam divinitus per illam petram, futuram petram, scilicet Christum Spiritu sancto ungendum figuraliter intellexit, in qua petra sic fundari Ecclesiam praefigurabat, etc.

VERS. 42.-- Tu es Simon filius. Prius dicit nomen ut habet a parentibus, ut significetur mutatio, dum de Simone Petrum dicit. In mutatione autem notatur vivacitas mysterii, sed nec prius nomen caret virtute. Est enim Simon obediens filius gratiae vel columbae, dum venit ad Jesum, sequens fratrem, 362Bunde a petra Christo dicitur Petrus, infirmus in illo, in figura Ecclesiae quae in petra fundatur.

Tu es Simon. (CHRYS.) Ideo nomen mutavit ut ostendat quia ipse est qui Vetus Testamentum dedit, et nomina transmutavit, quia Abram Abraham vocavit, et Sarai Saram, et Jacob Israelem. Igitur multis quidem et a nativitate nomina imposuit, ut Isaac et Samson, aliis autem post eam quae a progenitoribus est nuncupationem, ut Petro et filiis Zebedaei. Nam quibus quidam a prima aetate debebat virtus clarescere, ex tunc nomina susceperunt: quibus autem postea debebat augeri, postea nuncupatio posita est.

Filius Joanna, etc. Jona dicitur et Joanna indifferenter. Jona columba, Joanna gratia interpretatur.

362CVERS. 43.-- In Galilaeam et invenit Philippum. Galilaea dicitur transmigratio, vel revelatio. Transmigrat enim homo de vitiis ad virtutes, et post de virtute in virtutem, et sic fit ei revelatio, ut videatur Deus deorum in Sion. Vocaturus ergo discipulum ad sequendum, id est ad imitandum exit in Galilaeam, ipso loco innuens, ut sicut ipse proficiebat sapientia, et sicut per passiones intravit in gloriam, sic et sequaces.

VERS. 44.-- A Bethsaida civitate. Non frustra nominatur civitas, nec frustra dicitur Philippi, Petri et Andreae. Sonat enim domus venatorum, in quo notatur animus et officium istorum: sunt enim venatores in capiendis animabus, unde Philippus, antequam fiat apostolus, sponte vocat Nathanaelem.

362DVERS. 45.-- Invenit Philippus, etc. Nathanael peritissimus Scripturarum intelligit quod non alii, scilicet a Nazareth exire singularem Nazarenum: quia autem doctissimus, non est in apostolum electus: omnes enim apostoli primum de idiotis, ut confundantur sapientes. Quem scripsit Moyses. Ut cautus venator multis rationibus veritatem fratri astruit, scilicet quod Jesus est qui praenuntiatur in lege et prophetis. Filius Joseph, qui est de domo David, ut per eum (non quod de eo natus) cum Maria matre ejus (quam sciebat ex prophetis virginem peperisse) notetur esse ex David.

VERS. 46.-- Et dixit ei Nathanael. Vel dubitando interrogat vel affirmat ut legisperitus, a civitate 363Atanti nominis posse esse aliquem bonum vel eximium doctorem, vel summum Salvatorem. Dicitur enim Nazareth, flos vel germen munditiae, vel sanctitas. Utrique autem pronuntiationi congrue subditur: Veni et vide, etc.

VERS. 47.-- Ecce vere Israelita. Dolus, id est simulatio. Si enim habet peccata, confitetur. Non ergo negat peccatorem, sed in eo laudat confessionem. Pharisaei autem sunt dolosi qui se bonos praedicant cum sint mali.

VERS. 48.-- Unde me nosti? Cognoscens Dominum loqui de sua conscientia, non ex indignatione quaerit, sed admirando, unde et ex qua virtute. Sub ficu vidi te. (AUG.) Nathanael, id est donum Dei, est verus populus Israel donum Dei, qui erat 363Bsub ficu abditus, id est subditus sub consuetudine peccandi et umbra mortis, ut primi parentes sub fici foliis se texerunt, ubi prius visus esset a Domino et quaesitus, quam Dominum videret et quaereret, et ante etiam quam per apostolos vocaret.

VERS. 49.-- Rabbi, tu es Filius Dei. Quia cognovit Nathanael Christum absentem vidisse quae ipse in alio loco gesserat, id est, quomodo et ubi vocatus sit a Philippo, quod est indicium Deitatis, fatetur non solum magistrum, sed et Dei Filium, et regem Israel, id est Christum in quo probatur esse Israelita, ut dicitur, id est videns Deum.

VERS. 51.-- Videbitis coelum apertum. Nathanael Israelita, dicitur visurus apertum coelum, et angelos Dei descendentes et ascendentes, ut olim patriarcha 363CJacob vidit scalam, et angelos ascendentes et descendentes, qui per benedictionem vocatus est Israel. Angelos Dei. Saepe hoc miraculum apparuit quod angeli ascendebant et descendebant super Christum. Super Filium hominis. Christus dicit se Filium hominis, affirmans quod minus est, scilicet humanitatem, Nathanael et Petrus Filium Dei, quod majus est, affirmantes divinitatem. Ecce confirmatio utriusque naturae in Christo. Isti confitentes deitatem, merentur ascendere, ille asserens humanitatem, testatur se ad redimendum venisse. Super Filium hominis. Confirmat se esse hominem ne aliqui errarent, credentes ipsum tantum esse Deum ut etiam Petrus cum dixit: Absit a te, Domine.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- In the beginning was the Word. Against those who, on account of Christ's temporal nativity, said that Christ had not always been, he begins with the eternity of the Word, saying: In the beginning was the Word.

VERS. 3.-- All things were made by him. (AUG.) He is not a creature, through whom every creature was made, etc., 356Cas far as which all agree unto the power of the Divinity.

And without him was made nothing. (ORIG.) Thus he divides it: And without him was made nothing. The order is: There is nothing which was made without him.

VERS. 4.-- What was made, was life in him. (AUG.) John Chrysostom: And without him was made nothing. What was made. Hilary: Without him was made nothing, which was made in him.

VERS. 6.-- There was a man sent. After he had ascended by a lofty flight beyond every nature into the height of theology, because he had contemplated that the Word had always been with the Father, he presently descends to the lowliness of human nature, and particularly of the Word's forerunner, against those who said that human nature had been in 356Dthe first parent wholly lost and brought to nothing. A man sent. A man, not an angel, as the heretics will have it, sent from God. Therefore he was able to speak of God.

VERS. 9.-- He was the true light. Every man is said to be that nature in man which was made to the image and to the likeness of God. All the other things which are in man are the lower parts of man, and are in a manner outside the man. In that, namely, the soul, as in a certain higher world, the true light shines, and it enlightens those coming into that world and being born again through grace, because 357Athey whose life and conversation is in the heavens are as it were in another world.

VERS. 11.-- And his own received him not. From the beginning of the world there was no time in which there were not receivers of the divine word; whence he adds: But as many as received him.

VERS. 12.-- To be made the sons of God. A wondrous power, that they who were sons of the devil should, set free through him, be called both sons of God and brethren of Christ. But by what order they are made sons of God, and how this generation differs from the carnal, is subjoined: Who not of bloods.

VERS. 14.-- And the Word was made flesh. But that we may securely believe that men are born of God, he subjoins that God is born of man: by this taking on of infirmity our infirmity is healed, that we may be able to be made sons of God. 357BAnd this is it: And the Word was made flesh. That which was above descended below. What wonder: if that which was below ascended into that which is above? Nor is it a wonder if men are born of God, since God was born of man. Full of grace and truth. The man Christ was full of grace, because, with no merits going before, from the time he was conceived the Godhead was in him, filling that man with all fullness, redounding into all the saints; whence his mother also is called not only the mother of a man, but also the mother of God. Nor is it a wonder if a man is also called God, since God is also called man. And this is called grace and truth, because it was so promised of old and now exhibited: namely, that God should be in the man, filling him with every good, and through him his own.

357CVERS. 15.-- This was he of whom I said. By the demonstrative pronoun it is intimated that Christ was present in that place. For often the Lord himself, still unknown, before he was baptized and preached, was wont to come to John. Who is to come after me. Who is born after me, who is to preach after me, to baptize, to die. Not that he was not already born when he said these things, since he had even been baptized: but he was to come in preaching, in death, but not in resurrection. He was made before me, because he was prior to me. From the Greek, he was made in my presence, that is, he appeared to me indeed, namely, because he was prior to me.

VERS. 16.-- And of the fullness. The forerunner's testimony having been set forth, the evangelist returns to the testimony 357Dof his own assertion. As if he should say: The Word was made flesh: we saw his glory, full of grace and truth, and of his fullness we all have received. Grace, that we may believe in him, and truth, by which we shall understand him. We have received. (AUG.) Not only the grace which is plainly given gratis, not for merit, as the faith which works through love, by which the just man lives, but in return for that one, grace, that is, eternal blessedness, which is also called the wage of justice, and grace also, because the merit is from grace. Grace for grace. Grace is given in the conceiving of the good through grace. Grace simply, which is not of merit; if afterwards we work with it, we grow accustomed to good things, and then grace is given for the former grace.

358AVERS. 17.-- By Moses. By Moses the servant, the foretelling; by the Lord Jesus, the fulfilling of salvation.

VERS. 18.-- No man hath seen God at any time. That is, no mere man living in this body has seen God as he is; whence it was said to Moses: Man shall not see me and live (Exod. XXXIII), that is, no one living in the body shall see my divinity. Whence God added these things to Moses: Stand in the hole of the rock, and when I shall have passed through, thou shalt see my back parts (Ibid.). [Vulg. Ex 33:21 stabis supra petram; 33:22 ponam te in foramine petrae … donec transeam.] The rock, that is, Christ; the hole, the gibbet of the cross; I shall have passed through, that is, I shall pass over from the world to the Father. The back parts: his birth, his passion, his resurrection, his ascension, through which, being believed, we come to his blessedness and to the sight of him. That which is read, that God appeared to men either visibly 358Bto the eyes of the flesh, or invisibly in prophetic visions, is to be understood as done through a creature made subject to him; for the visions of the prophets were wrought by a certain spiritual creature, in which they are declared to have seen God.

The only-begotten Son, etc. What the sum of grace and truth may be he briefly subjoins, namely, the knowledge of the Trinity itself; whence: This is life eternal, that they may know thee the one true God, and Jesus Christ whom thou hast sent. [Vulg. Jo 17:3 Haec est autem vita aeterna: ut cognoscant te, solum Deum verum, "the only true God."] Which in this life is not full, because no one in the flesh has seen God as he is. Whence neither Moses himself, much less was grace given through him, as he himself also, after all the things seen in the figure of God, said: Show me thyself (Exod. XXXIII), [Vulg. Ex 33:13 ostende mihi faciem tuam, "show me thy face"; 33:18 Ostende mihi gloriam tuam, "show me thy glory."] but the Son, who is in the secret of the Father, has declared him, because no one knows the Father but the Son, and he to whom the Son shall have willed to 358Creveal him (Luc. X). [Vulg. Lc 10:22 et nemo scit quis sit Filius, nisi Pater: et quis sit Pater, nisi Filius; cf. Mt 11:27 neque Patrem quis novit, nisi Filius.] He himself declares to his own what is to be held concerning the Trinity of the Godhead, and how it is to be come to, and he leads them into it.

VERS. 19.-- And this. Both this and the other testimonies of John that follow were given after Christ had been baptized, when Jesus was now coming to invite the disciples, whom John by his testimonies sends to him. A testimony against this, that Christ was hidden: hidden, because lowly; lowly, that he might be able to be despised and killed, that he might redeem the world. Who art thou. The Jews thought John to be the Christ who was promised in the law. Others thought him to be Elias, because of his exceeding abstinence and chastity, and his solitary life, and his most sharp rebuking of offences, and the most severe terror of the vengeance to come. Others said that he was one of 358Dthe prophets raised up again, because of the grace of prophecy.

VERS. 20.-- He confessed. He confessed, as he says afterwards, not that he was the Christ. He did not deny what he was, that is, that he was the forerunner, as he says when questioned afterwards: I am the voice of one crying. The order of the words is: and he confessed and did not deny, when they sent.

VERS. 21.-- Art thou Elias? Since all knew the name of Christ, they knew also that Elias was to go before him. But Elias himself is to go before him as about to judge in power; John is figuratively Elias, going before the same one as about to be judged: so that there are two heralds, as there are two advents of the same one. Whence: I am not Elias, that is, not the herald pointing to his power; but I show the 359Alowliness at which you take offence. As if he should say: Serve the lowly one before the exalted one judges. Behold, neither am I the Christ, nor a judge on the side of power.

VERS. 23.-- I am the voice of one crying in the wilderness. He does not say, I am a man, or John, or the son of Zacharias. He does not regard his human subsistence or his generation, but, exalted beyond all these things as the forerunner of the Word, he forsakes all things that are contained within the world. He ascends on high, made the voice of the Word, and confesses in himself no substance beyond the abundance of grace by which he surpasses every creature, that he may be the voice of the Word. The voice is the interpreter of the mind; but the mind, that is, the understanding of all things, is the Son of God. John therefore is the voice and interpreter of this mind, because he first showed him to the world: Behold the Lamb of God. Isaias, prophesied this of 359BJohn: I am the voice of one crying in the wilderness, in which the word sounds, that is, prophecy, and the showing forth and the lamp of the Word present.

VERS. 24.-- Of the Pharisees. That is, of the chief men of the Jews, who do not seek doctrine, but envy it. Who nevertheless come to John for this reason, that they hear that he has preached the common resurrection of all, which they themselves also believed; whence they themselves also agree with Christ in many things. They gave hearing to the Apostle Paul also in many things.

VERS. 25.-- Why then dost thou baptize. The Pharisees heard in the prophets that Christ would come and would baptize, knowing that the Jordan had borne the figure of baptism, and that Elias and Eliseus had crossed it with dry feet; and they did not doubt that the figure of baptism had gone before 359Cin Elias and Eliseus, whence they now thought that these had risen again and had baptized. Whence they do not ask, Art thou one of the prophets? but only: Art thou the prophet? that one, namely, who prefigured baptism.

VERS. 26.-- I baptize in water. Let not what I do be imputed to boldness, because I wash bodies with water, I do not take away sins; so that, as I am forerunner in being born and in preaching, so also in baptizing: but he is in the midst of you who takes away sins.

VERS. 27.-- Whose I am not worthy, etc. He does not say, I will not loose. For he does loose, since he made him manifest and disclosed many things concerning his divinity and his humanity; but, I am not worthy, because he reckons himself unworthy for the doing of this, in which humility is simply shown.

359DVERS. 28.-- These things in Bethania. There are two Bethanias. One beyond the Jordan, the other round about, not far from Jerusalem, where Lazarus was raised. That which is beyond the Jordan signifies human nature under the natural law before the streams of grace, which grace was abundant after the incarnation of Christ; which nature had been created to this end, that it should obey the law of nature, in which John baptizes, because he does not free from sin. He baptizes beyond the streams of the divine gifts, which had not yet begun in Christ to descend into human nature. That which is on this side of the Jordan signifies the same human nature obedient under the law of grace, which is nearest to Jerusalem, that is, to the vision of peace, through charity, 360Ain which Christ raises the dead man, because he frees from sin.

VERS. 29.-- The next day John saw Jesus. Typically. John, that is, the people of grace, on the next day, that is, after the law, knows the lamb by whom it is redeemed. Coming to him, and he says. Not only by the steps of the body, but also by the approaches of his own inward contemplation, because he deigned to be known by John according to his divinity and his humanity. Behold the Lamb of God, etc. Three things the lamb supplies to those who possess him: the milk of simple doctrine, by which little ones are nourished; the wool, that is, the increases of the virtues; the eating of his flesh. The sins of the world. [Vulg. Jo 1:29 peccatum mundi, singular.] The sin of the world is called original sin, which is common to the whole world, by which the whole of human nature, founded at once and together, transgressed the divine laws through disobedience 360Bin paradise; and that original sin, and the several sins added on top of it, grace looses.

VERS. 30.-- After me there comes a man. Christ is man, in which he agrees with us and pays on our behalf; and Son of God, in which he agrees with the Father, and by forgiving sins reconciles us to him.

VERS. 31.-- That he may be made manifest. This is the lamb who takes away sins, which I am not, whom also I now know better; but for this reason do I baptize, that he may be made manifest to Israel. For lest, on account of the strangeness of it, they should be the less baptized by Christ in the Spirit, he prepared the way for him by the practice of baptizing; whence, after men had come to him who is the way, his baptism ceases. In John's baptism also the humility of Christ is made manifest, which is the fulfilling of all justice; for as it was needful that Christ 360Cshould be born and die, so also that he should be baptized. For if he himself, who has no need, is baptized by a servant, let not men, however wise and holy, disdain to be baptized by the Lord, without whom they remain in their sins. Baptizing. Christ is baptized by John, that all humility, which is all justice, may be shown. But not he alone, lest the baptism of John, in which God-and-man was baptized, should seem holier and greater than the baptism of Christ, in which men are baptized. But others are baptized by John, so that the practice of baptizing may prepare the way for the baptism of Christ.

VERS. 32.-- And testimony. As he said that he had come to make Christ manifest, so he bore testimony, namely: he saw the Spirit. In which he learned what he did not know before. He had come also to preach penance, that 360Dis, to make the preparation for Christ's preaching; but he sets down only the preparation of baptism by itself, because the baptism of Christ is the highest sacrament among the rest. The Spirit descending. Not that he then first descended, but that by this he might be shown to descend and to remain in those baptized in water and not otherwise, and that John through him might understand more excellently concerning Christ.

VERS. 33.-- And I knew him not. (AUG.) The dove having been seen, he says that through it he learned what he did not know before, etc., as far as whence the Apostle: In labour and in mind they are different, in working undifferentiated.

(HIER.) He shows that John did not know, by the baptism of Christ, that is, that the world was redeemed; for he knew 361Ahim to be the Son of God and born in the flesh. Aug. says that John was ignorant of this only: whether he would keep to himself the power of baptizing, or would grant it to some one of his own. And remaining. (AUG.) Spiritually the Spirit is said to remain in Christ, from whom he never withdrew through sin, in whom he always lived wonderfully and wonderfully wrought; into the saints he comes, and through sins he withdraws. Yet he always remains in them for something, as Christ promised them, namely, that they may always press on in good things. For something he does not remain, namely, that they should prophesy from him or work miracles; and therefore this which he says is the first sign of recognizing Christ.

Who baptizes. Christ baptizes in the Spirit, not only by remitting sins in the water, but also by cleansing afterwards, and by kindling love, and by filling with gifts.

361BThis is the Son of God. Here he testifies him to be the Son of God whom above he was calling a man: so that from him there may be had a testimony of either nature.

VERS. 35.-- The next day John was standing again. Typically. John stands, the law ceases, yet bearing testimony to Christ. Jesus walks, that is, grace, gathering from this side and from that.

VERS. 37.-- They followed Jesus. They followed, that they might now hear him rather than John, not yet however cleaving to him wholly, but that they might see where he dwells, that they might come to him constantly and be taught by him; and afterwards they followed perfectly, when he called them from the boat. Two follow, because they who follow ought to be kindled with a twofold charity.

VERS. 38.-- Turning. As it were from behind they had seen him, but 361Cas it were he offered them his face when he came down from his majesty, that he might be able to be seen, when in place of the shadow of the law he showed forth the light of the Gospel. What seek ye? He asks, not as one ignorant, but that they who answer may have a reward: he asks the thing, not the person, what, not whom, lest he should seem to show himself; they answer the person. Where dwellest thou? Mystically. They wish it to be shown them in whom Christ dwells, that they may make themselves like to them. Or: they who see the incarnation devoutly ask that it be shown them how the eternal dwelling is with the Father.

VERS. 39.-- Come. As if to say: it cannot be unfolded in speech, but is shown in work; come by believing and by working, and you shall understand; come by the testimony of John, and withdraw from the law, and you shall receive grace. That day. He shows the New Testament, or the present life. And it was 361Dabout the tenth hour. Because they had come from the law, the decalogue sent them to Christ; at the tenth hour they seek to be taught by him, because none other teaches the law but he who gave it. And he teaches with mercy, and those things are fulfilled which they could not fulfil; or the tenth hour indicates the evening of the world.

VERS. 40.-- The brother of Simon Peter, one of the two who had heard from John, and had followed. The brother younger in birth believes first, for the order of faith is not an empty thing. Perhaps for dignity Andrew is said to be the brother of Peter, upon whom, that is, upon whose faith, the Church was to be founded.

VERS. 41.-- He findeth first. True piety at once announces the treasure it has found to its brother, that as he is a brother by 362Ablood, so he may be a brother also in faith. We have found the Messias, etc. Christus in Greek, Messias in Hebrew, anointed in Latin: for he was truly and in a special way anointed by the Holy Spirit, and from that anointing Christians are named. That Jacob, after the vision, set up a stone for a pillar which the pouring on of oil anointed: already by divine gift he figuratively understood, through that rock, the rock to come, namely Christ, who was to be anointed with the Holy Spirit; and he prefigured that the Church was so to be founded upon that rock, etc.

VERS. 42.-- Thou art Simon the son. First he says the name as he has it from his parents, that the change may be signified when from Simon he calls him Peter. But in the change is marked the liveliness of the mystery; yet neither is the former name without power. For Simon is obedient, son of grace or of the dove, when he comes to Jesus, following his brother; 362Bwhence from the rock, that is Christ, he is called Peter, weak in him, in the figure of the Church which is founded upon the rock.

Thou art Simon. (CHRYS.) He changed the name for this reason, that he might show that it is he himself who gave the Old Testament and changed names: because he called Abram Abraham, and Sarai Sara, and Jacob Israel. Therefore upon many he laid names even from their birth, as upon Isaac and Samson; but upon others after the naming which comes from their forebears, as upon Peter and the sons of Zebedee. For those in whom virtue was to shine out from their first age received their names from that time; but those in whom it was afterwards to be increased, upon them the naming was set afterwards.

Son of Joanna, etc. Jona and Joanna are said indifferently. Jona is interpreted dove, Joanna grace.

362CVERS. 43.-- Into Galilee, and he found Philip. Galilee is interpreted transmigration, or revelation. For man passes over from the vices to the virtues, and afterwards from virtue to virtue, and so revelation is made to him, that the God of gods may be seen in Sion. About to call a disciple therefore to follow him, that is, to imitate him, he goes out into Galilee, intimating by the place itself that, as he himself advanced in wisdom, and as through sufferings he entered into glory, so also do his followers.

VERS. 44.-- From Bethsaida the city. Not in vain is the city named, nor in vain is it said to be the city of Philip, of Peter and of Andrew. For it sounds house of hunters, in which is marked the disposition and the office of these men: for they are hunters in the taking of souls; whence Philip, before he becomes an apostle, of his own accord calls Nathanael.

362DVERS. 45.-- Philip findeth, etc. Nathanael, most skilled in the Scriptures, understands what others do not, namely, that from Nazareth there comes forth a singular Nazarene; but because he was most learned, he was not chosen into an apostle: for all the apostles were first from the unlearned, that the wise might be confounded. Whom Moses wrote of. Like a wary hunter he builds up the truth for his brother by many reasons, namely, that Jesus is he who is foretold in the law and the prophets. The son of Joseph, who is of the house of David: so that through him (not that he was born of him), together with Mary his mother (whom he knew from the prophets to have brought forth as a virgin), he may be marked as being of David.

VERS. 46.-- And Nathanael said to him. Either he asks in doubt, or he affirms, as one skilled in the law, that from a city 363Aof so great a name there can be some good man, or an outstanding teacher, or the supreme Saviour. For Nazareth is interpreted flower, or bud of cleanness, or holiness. And to either utterance there is fittingly subjoined: Come and see, etc.

VERS. 47.-- Behold an Israelite indeed. Guile, that is, dissimulation. For if he has sins, he confesses them. He does not therefore deny him to be a sinner, but praises the confession in him. But the Pharisees are guileful, who proclaim themselves good when they are evil.

VERS. 48.-- Whence knowest thou me? Knowing that the Lord speaks of his conscience, he asks not out of indignation, but in wonder: whence, and from what power. I saw thee under the fig tree. (AUG.) Nathanael, that is, the gift of God, is the true people of Israel, the gift of God, which was 363Bhidden under the fig tree, that is, made subject under the habit of sinning and the shadow of death, as the first parents covered themselves under the leaves of the fig tree; where he had been seen and sought by the Lord before he saw and sought the Lord, and even before he called him through the apostles.

VERS. 49.-- Rabbi, thou art the Son of God. Because Nathanael knew that Christ, though absent, had seen what he himself had done in another place, that is, how and where he was called by Philip, which is a token of Deity, he confesses him not only master, but also the Son of God, and king of Israel, that is, Christ: in which he is proved to be an Israelite, as it is said, that is, one seeing God.

VERS. 51.-- You shall see heaven opened. Nathanael the Israelite is said to be about to see heaven opened, and the angels of God descending and ascending, as of old the patriarch 363CJacob saw the ladder, and the angels ascending and descending, who through the blessing was called Israel. The angels of God. Often this miracle appeared, that angels ascended and descended upon Christ. Upon the Son of man. Christ calls himself the Son of man, affirming what is less, namely his humanity; Nathanael and Peter call him the Son of God, which is greater, affirming his divinity. Behold the confirmation of either nature in Christ. These, confessing his deity, deserve to ascend; he, asserting his humanity, testifies that he came to redeem. Upon the Son of man. He confirms that he is man, lest any should err, believing him to be only God, as even Peter did when he said: Far be it from thee, Lord.

CAPUT II.

  • 363D

VERS. 1.-- Et die tertia. (AUG.) Post ostensionem Christi per testimonia Joannis ipse se miraculo ostendit.

Et die. Significat tertia die, id est tempore gratiae, post tempus, id est ante legem et sub lege Christum ad nuptias Ecclesiae per carnem venisse.

VERS. 2.-- Vocatus est autem. In quo ostendit non despiciendas nuptias quas fecit, et sacramentum nuptiarum insinuat.

VERS. 3.-- Deficiente. Vinum deficit, ut mirabiliter vino de aqua facto, virtus latentis Deitatis appareat.

(AUG.) Major virtus est in creatione et gubernatione 364Acoeli et terrae, etc., usque ad per quae virtus quae in majoribus etiam operatur, ad memoriam reducitur.

VERS. 4.-- Quid mihi. (ID.) In his non negat matrem, ut haeretici dicunt, etc., usque ad intrationem ad Deum intelligit, vinum bibit.

VERS. 6.-- Binas vel. (ID.) Aestimantis est hoc dicere, ut ipsas diceret binas quas et ternas, etc., usque ad tribus filiis Noe disseminatae.

VERS. 7.-- Et impleverunt. Quod jussu ejus impletae sunt hydriae, significat quod etiam illa Scriptura a Domino est, sed dum in ea sub velamine, id est adopertione litterae, latet Christus, sapit aquam insipidam. Cum autem aufertur velamen, est vinum inebrians. Potuit quidem vacuas implere 364Bvino, qui cuncta creat ex nihilo, sed maluit de aqua vinum facere, ut doceret se non solvere legem, sed implere, nec in Evangelio alia facere vel docere quam quae prophetia praedixit.

VERS. 9.-- Ut autem gustavit. Triclinium, id est tres ordines discumbentium, altitudine distantes inter se, id est doctores, continentes, conjugati. Architriclinus aliquis legisperitus, qui bibit spiritualem sensum legis. Unde spiritualem sensum in Scripturis aperire discipulis est aquam in vinum convertere, ut quando post resurrectionem aperuit illis sensum, intelligerent Scripturas. Aquam vinum factam. Per aquam baptismus Joannis, per vinum passio Christi, unde et vinum prius fertur architriclino, id est Joanni qui ante bibit passionem quam Christus.

364CVERS. 11.-- Hoc fecit. (HIER.) In initio signorum quae mortalis mortalibus erat daturus, aquam vertit in vinum, sicut jam immortalis discipulis aperuit sensum, ut in lege et prophetis intelligerent Christum, ita carnales mentes prius sapore sapientiae imbuit, postea resuscitatos majori gloria completurus.

VERS. 12.-- Post haec, etc. Post initium signorum de vino, subdit aliud indicium divinae majestatis in ejectione vendentium de templo. Typice. Filius Dei ut humilis, descendit Capharnaum, id est in mundum, secundum quod habet matrem et fratres.

VERS. 13.-- Et prope erat Pascha. Pascha agimus, dum a vitiis ad virtutes transimus. Ad hoc Jesus venit dum Ecclesiam quotidie visitat, et actus 364Dcujusque considerat, et eos ejicit qui inter sanctos, vel ficte bona, vel aperte mala faciunt. Per boves qui arant, praedicatores doctrinae coelestis significantur, hos vendunt qui non amore Dei, sed pro quaestu temporali praedicant. Oves innocentes sua vellera vestiendis praebent, per has signantur opera munditiae et pietatis, quae venduntur, dum pro humana laude geruntur. Spiritus ut columba apparuit, unde per columbam spiritus accipitur, quam vendunt Simoniaci. Nummos dant mutuo in Ecclesia, qui non simulate coelestibus, sed aperte terrenis serviunt. Hi omnes ejiciuntur de parte sortis sanctorum, qui vel ficte bona, vel aperte mala faciunt, et funiculis peccatorum modo flagellantur ad correctionem; 365Aqui, si incorrecti permanserint, in fine ligabuntur. Oves quoque et boves ejicit, quia talium vitam et doctrinam ostendit reprobam. Et mensas subvertit, quia in fine ipsae res quas dilexerunt, destruuntur. Et ascendit. Si ipse qui dedit decreta Dei, custodit, docet quanta cura hominibus debet esse vacare orationibus et Dei solemnitatibus. Bis legitur ad Pascha ascendisse. Semel primo anno praedicationis, nondum incarcerato Joanne, unde hic agitur. Secundo cum ad passionem iret, et in utroque vendentes ejecit de templo.

VERS. 14.-- Et invenit in templo. Si in figurali templo quod est domus orationis, prohibet negotiationem quae honesta putatur, cum sit de his quae in templo offeruntur, quanto magis prohibet 365Bpotationes et caetera graviora?

VERS. 15.-- De funiculis. Funiculi quibus ejiciuntur sunt incrementa actionum malarum, unde Isaias: Vae qui trahitis iniquitatem in funiculis vanitatis (Isa. V). Et Salomon: Funiculis peccatorum suorum quisque contringitur (Prov. V).

VERS. 16.-- Qui columbas. Non solum vendentes columbas, sed etiam alii domum Dei faciunt domum negotiationis, id est omnes qui quaerunt quae sua sunt, non quae Jesu Christi.

VERS. 17.-- Zelus domus tuae. (AUG.) Bonus zelus est fervor animi, quo mens, abjecto humano timore, pro defensione veritatis accenditur. Ab eo comeditur, qui quaelibet prava quae viderit, corrigere satagit; si nequit, tolerat et gemit.

365CVERS. 18.-- Quod signum ostendis nobis, quia haec facis? Respondit. Quia signum quaerunt quare solita commercia de templo ejicit? et ait: Solvite. Quasi dicat: Quia hoc templum significat templum corporis in quo nulla est macula et merito hoc figurale purgo, qui illud ab hominibus morte solutum divina potentia suscitare queo.

VERS. 20.-- Dixerunt ergo. Carnales carnaliter sapiebant, ille spiritualiter loquebatur. Quod Evangelista nobis aperit: Ille autem dicebat de templo corporis sui.

Quadraginta et sex. (AUG.) Anatole Graece oriens, dysme occidens, arctos septentrio, etc., usque ad qui senarium numerum quadragies sexies habent.

Aedificatum. (AUG.) Aedificatio Salomonis septem 365Dannis perfecta est, etc., usque ad unde dicitur electos esse congregandos a quatuor ventis.

VERS. 23.-- Cum autem. Conventus adibat, ut verbis et signis plures converteret. Et quia aliqui solis signis moti credebant in eum, vere quidem, sed tamen ita, ut possent postea in passione ejus scandalizari, et quia nondum erant renati ex aqua et spiritu, non erant digni quibus se crederet Jesus, et familiariter cohabitaret. Sciebat enim quid esset in illis, melius quam ipse homo de se. Haec fides convenit catechumenis, qui credunt in eum, sed quia non sunt renati, non creditur eis communio corporis et sanguinis Christi. De talibus erat Nicodemus, qui credens dicit eum a Deo venisse, propter 366Asigna quae viderat, sed nondum renatus erat, et ideo nocte venit, non die, quia nondum illuminatus coelesti lumine.

CHAPTER II.

  • 363D

VERS. 1.-- And on the third day. (AUG.) After the showing forth of Christ through the testimonies of John, he himself shows himself by a miracle.

And on the day. It signifies that on the third day, that is, in the time of grace, after the time, that is, before the law and under the law, Christ came through the flesh to the nuptials of the Church.

VERS. 2.-- And there was called. In which he shows that the nuptials which he made are not to be despised, and he intimates the sacrament of marriage.

VERS. 3.-- Failing. The wine fails, that, wine being wonderfully made from water, the power of the hidden Godhead may appear.

(AUG.) Greater is the power in the creation and governance 364Aof heaven and earth, etc., as far as through which the power that works also in the greater things is brought back to memory.

VERS. 4.-- What is it to me. (ID.) In these words he does not deny his mother, as the heretics say, etc., as far as he understands the entrance to God, he drinks the wine.

VERS. 6.-- Two or. (ID.) It belongs to one making an estimate to speak thus, so that he should call the same vessels two-measure which he also calls three-measure, etc., as far as sown abroad by the three sons of Noe.

VERS. 7.-- And they filled them. That the waterpots were filled at his command signifies that that Scripture also is from the Lord; but while Christ lies hidden in it under the veil, that is, under the covering of the letter, it tastes of insipid water. But when the veil is taken away, it is wine that inebriates. He could indeed have filled them, empty as they were, 364Bwith wine, he who creates all things out of nothing; but he chose rather to make wine out of water, that he might teach that he does not destroy the law but fulfil it, nor in the Gospel does he do or teach other things than those which prophecy foretold.

VERS. 9.-- And when he had tasted. The triclinium, that is, three ranks of those reclining, standing apart from one another in height: that is, teachers, the continent, the married. The chief steward is some man skilled in the law, who drinks the spiritual sense of the law. Whence to open the spiritual sense in the Scriptures to disciples is to turn water into wine, as when after the resurrection he opened their understanding, that they might understand the Scriptures. The water made wine. By the water, the baptism of John; by the wine, the passion of Christ: whence the wine also is carried first to the chief steward, that is, to John, who drank the passion before Christ did.

364CVERS. 11.-- This he did. (HIER.) In the beginning of the signs which, being mortal, he was to give to mortals, he turned water into wine; and as, being now immortal, he opened the understanding of the disciples, that in the law and the prophets they might understand Christ, so he first steeped carnal minds with the savour of wisdom, being about afterwards to fill them, when raised again, with a greater glory.

VERS. 12.-- After this, etc. After the beginning of the signs, concerning the wine, he subjoins another token of the divine majesty in the casting out of the sellers from the temple. Typically. The Son of God, as lowly, goes down to Capharnaum, that is, into the world, according to that whereby he has a mother and brethren.

VERS. 13.-- And the Pasch was at hand. We keep the Pasch when we pass over from the vices to the virtues. To this Jesus comes when he daily visits the Church, and considers the deeds 364Dof each man, and casts out those who, among the saints, either do good things feignedly or evil things openly. By the oxen that plough are signified the preachers of heavenly doctrine; and these they sell who preach not from the love of God, but for temporal gain. Innocent sheep offer their own fleeces for the clothing of others; by these are signified the works of cleanness and of piety, which are sold when they are performed for human praise. The Spirit appeared as a dove, whence by the dove the Spirit is understood, which the Simoniacs sell. They give coins in loan in the Church who serve, not the heavenly things feignedly, but the earthly things openly. All these are cast out from the portion of the lot of the saints, who either do good things feignedly or evil things openly; and they are now scourged with the cords of their sins unto correction; 365Aand if they remain uncorrected, at the end they shall be bound. He casts out the sheep also and the oxen, because he shows the life and doctrine of such men to be reprobate. And he overturns the tables, because at the end the very things which they loved are destroyed. And he went up. If he himself who gave the decrees of God keeps them, he teaches with how great care men ought to be free for prayers and for the solemnities of God. He is read to have gone up to the Pasch twice: once in the first year of his preaching, John not yet imprisoned, which is what is treated of here; a second time when he was going to his passion; and in both he cast the sellers out of the temple.

VERS. 14.-- And he found in the temple. If in the figurative temple, which is a house of prayer, he forbids the trading that is thought honourable, since it is of those things which are offered in the temple, how much more does he forbid 365Bdrinking-bouts and the other graver things?

VERS. 15.-- Of little cords. The cords by which they are cast out are the increases of evil actions; whence Isaias: Woe to you that draw iniquity with cords of vanity (Isa. V). And Solomon: Each man is bound fast with the cords of his own sins (Prov. V). [Vulg. Pr 5:22 et funibus peccatorum suorum constringitur, with no quisque; Migne prints funiculis and quisque, and sets contringitur, read at the plate.]

VERS. 16.-- Who sold doves. Not only they who sell doves, but others also make the house of God a house of trade: that is, all who seek the things that are their own, not the things that are Jesus Christ's.

VERS. 17.-- The zeal of thy house. (AUG.) Good zeal is a fervour of the mind by which the mind, human fear being cast away, is kindled for the defence of the truth. He is eaten by it who busies himself to correct whatever crooked things he has seen; and if he cannot, he bears with them and groans.

365CVERS. 18.-- What sign dost thou show us, seeing thou doest these things? He answered. Because they seek a sign, why does he cast the accustomed traffic out of the temple? And he says: Destroy. As if he should say: Because this temple signifies the temple of the body, in which there is no stain, and deservedly do I cleanse this figurative one, I who am able by divine power to raise up that one when it has been dissolved by men in death.

VERS. 20.-- They said therefore. Carnal men were minded carnally, while he was speaking spiritually. Which the Evangelist opens to us: But he spoke of the temple of his body.

Forty and six. (AUG.) Anatole in Greek is the east, dysme the west, arctos the north, etc., as far as which have the number of six forty-six times.

Built. (AUG.) The building of Solomon was completed in seven 365Dyears, etc., as far as whence it is said that the elect are to be gathered from the four winds.

VERS. 23.-- And when. He went to the assemblies, that by words and by signs he might convert the more. And because some, moved by the signs alone, believed in him, truly indeed, yet in such a way that they could afterwards be scandalized in his passion; and because they were not yet born again of water and of the Spirit, they were not worthy men to whom Jesus should entrust himself and with whom he should dwell familiarly. For he knew what was in them, better than the man himself of himself. This faith befits catechumens, who believe in him; but because they are not born again, the communion of the body and blood of Christ is not entrusted to them. Of such was Nicodemus, who, believing, says that he came from God, because of 366Athe signs which he had seen; but he was not yet born again, and therefore he came by night, not by day, because he was not yet enlightened by the heavenly light.

CAPUT III.

VERS. 1.-- Erat autem homo, etc. Hic erat unus ex his, qui per signa crediderant. Nicodemus nomine. Nicodemus, victoria populi, vel victor populi gestans figuram omnium qui ex Judaico populo in Christum credentes per fidem vincunt mundum.

VERS. 2.-- Hic venit, etc. Nox significat litteram legis, vel ignorantiam cordis, vel timorem. Et nocte ideo forsitan venit, quia magister in Israel palam dicere erubesceret, vel pro metu Judaeorum. Hic quia prudenter aperta signa notavit, plenius mysteria 366Bfidei requirit, et ideo meruit doceri de deitate Christi et ipsius utraque nativitate, de passione, de resurrectione et ascensione, de secunda generatione, et ingressu regni coelorum, et de aliis pluribus.

VERS. 3.-- Amen, amen dico. Non interroganti secundam nativitatem exponit, ne credat Nicodemus solam fidem, quapropter visa signa crediderat in Jesum sufficere ad salutem absque spiritualis regenerationis virtute, et absque sacramentis. Etiam quia hominem purum intelligebat eum Nicodemus ut Joannem Baptistam, vel quemlibet ex prophetis, ideo incoepit eum instruere Jesus de secunda regeneratione, qua intelligit eum Filium Dei, et se posse Filium Dei fieri.

(AUG.) Secunda nativitas de qua docet Jesus spiritualis 366Cest, quae est ex Deo et Ecclesia ad vitam, sed ille solam carnalem sapit quae est ex Adam et Eva ad mortem. Sed sicut carnalem dicit iste non posse iterari, sic et spiritualis a quocunque facta non potest iterari. Ex semine enim veri Abrahae sunt nati, sive per liberam sive per ancillam.

Nisi quis. Necessarium est visibile sacramentum aquae ad ablutionem visibilis corporis, sicut est necessaria doctrina invisibilis fidei, et ad sanctificationem animae invisibilis.

Renatus fuerit. (CHRYS.) Hoc est, si tu non natus fueris desuper, et dogmatum susceperis certitudinem alicubi, extra erras et longe es a regno coelorum, seipsum hic ostendens et indicans, quoniam non est hoc tantum quod videtur, sed aliis oculis 366Dopus est ad videndum Deum.

VERS. 5.-- Ex aqua. Ex aqua visibili sacramento, et spiritu, invisibili intellectu, ut symbolum baptismi visibiliter accipiat, et spiritualem intellectum ipsius symboli perficiat. Vel ex aqua visibili et Spiritu sancto.

VERS. 6.-- Quod natum. Hanc spiritualem nativitatem distinguit a carnali, dicens: Quod natum est. Ex carne. Id est, ex primo homine per carnis successionem carnale est. Nomine carnis, non solum carnem, sed totum carnalem hominem significat. Sic econtra totus homo spiritualis anima et corpore spiritus dicitur. Et quod, etc. Verbo et sacramento spiritus invisibiliter adest quo natus 367Aex Adam ad mortem nascitur, Filius Dei ad vitam, qui non tantum spiritualis dicitur, sed spiritus, quia sicut substantia spiritus est invisibilis, ita invisibiliter per visibile sacramentum efficitur Dei Filius. Spiritus est. Non autem mireris, si sic natum dico spiritum, quia sicut spiritus ubi vult spirat, et nescis unde venit, aut quo vadat, sic est omnis qui natus est ex spiritu.

VERS. 8.-- Spiritus ubi vult. Qua in potestate habet cujus cor illustret. Vocem ejus audis in Scripturis, vel dum loquitur aliquis plenus Spiritu sancto, quae et si audis, nescis unde venit, id est, quomodo cor illius intravit, vel quomodo redierit, quia natura ejus est invisibilis. Sic est omnis qui natus est ex Spiritu sancto, quia ipse invisibiliter 367Bagente Spiritu incipit esse quod non erat, ita ut infidelis nesciat unde veniat, et quo vadat, id est, qua gratia venit in adoptionem Filii, et vadit in regnum Dei. Quae quia incognita sunt carnali, iterum quaerit? Quomodo possunt haec fieri, non habens spiritualem intellectum in littera legis; impossibile putat quod dicitur de nativitate per spiritum, unde redarguitur a Domino: Tu es magister, etc. Et vocem ejus, etc. Vox spiritus est vox Christi, quam audivit Nicodemus, sed quia nondum est natus de spiritu, nescit unde veniat, aut quo vadat, id est, qua occasione velit nasci hominem in spiritu, aut quo vadat, id est, ad quam perfectionem dicat ipsum nascentem ex se. Nam qui ex eo nascitur, unus cum ipso spiritus per gratiam efficitur, 367Csicut etiam ipse spiritus unus est per naturam.

VERS. 10.-- Tu es magister, etc. Non insultat ei, ut superior eo habeatur qui omnibus praeest, sed quia vult eum nasci ex spiritu, quod nequit nisi humilis, tumidum ex magisterio humiliat. Quasi dicat: Superbus princeps, nil nosti, humiliare ut ille qui seipsum exinanivit, et natus est ex spiritu, scies quod modo nescis.

VERS. 11.-- Quod scimus loquimur. Id est quia ipse est scientia, locutio et sermo Patris. Et quod vidimus testamur. Quia ipse visio, et verum testimonium et veritas Patris. Quod pluraliter mysterium Trinitatis innuit.

VERS. 12.-- Si terrena dixi vobis. (AUG.) Et quia non accipitis terrena, quomodo potestis capere coelestia, 367Did est coelestem spiritus nativitatem? cum non solum non credatis, sed nec intelligatis terrena, quod de templo corporis mei (quod de terra accepi) dixi a vobis solvendo, a me suscitando.

VERS. 13.-- Nisi qui descendit. Hoc pertinet ad secundam nativitatem in spiritu, quia nemo in Christo ad Patrem ascendit, nisi qui ex spiritu nascitur. Nemo salvatur per mortem Christi, nisi qui ex spiritu nascitur. Nemo nascitur ex spiritu, nisi in morte Christi. Et cum dicit Filius hominis, dat nobis certitudinem ascendendi, cum filii hominum simus.

VERS. 14.-- Et sicut Moyses exaltavit serpentem in deserto. Supponit de morte sua quae est origo 368Aet causa habendi praedicta, in cujus fide eum instruit. Et quia carnalis erat, et notus in lege, ideo quamdam legis partem adducit, quam ostendit esse figuram et mortis suae significativam, ut etiam sic pateat de aliis partibus legis, quod sint in figura. Et quia mors intelligitur per serpentem, et Christus mortuus serpens dicitur, quem qui fide intuetur, liberatur, sicut intuitu illius in figura ab ignitis serpentibus liberantur qui significant incentiva vitiorum. Ita exaltari oportet Filium hominis, etc. Magistrum legis ad significationem legis invitat. Et quia dixerat de Filio hominis, ne hoc solum putet Nicodemus, subdit eumdem etiam esse Filium Dei. Sic enim dilexit Deus. Aperit causam humanae salutis, scilicet dilectionem Dei Patris, ex quo omnis 368Brestauratio est per Filium suum in Spiritu sancto.

VERS. 16.-- Mundum. Cosmos Graece, quod Latine mundus interpretatur, ornatus dicitur. Per mundum igitur humana natura significatur, non quod secundum corpus ex quatuor elementis constet, sed quia secundum animam a Deo est ornata, et ad imaginem Dei est facta, qua ad hoc diligit Deus ut eam aeternam faciat.

Ut Filium suum unigenitum daret, ut omnis qui credit, etc. Ecce idem dicit de Filio Dei quod supra de Filio hominis, ut per quem in deitate conditi sumus, per eumdem in homine restauremur.

VERS. 19.-- Hoc est autem judicium. Cur qui non credit judicatur? quia lux venit quae excitat 368Chomines et admonet cognoscere sua mala, in quibus omnes sunt, sed alii oderunt admonentem lucem, et fugiunt, ne arguantur eorum mala quae diligunt. Alii admoniti per lucem accusant sua mala, quod est facere veritatem et sic illuminantur. Erant enim eorum. Qui est in tenebris, et diligit lucem, non est malus, sed ille dicitur malus, et opera ejus mala, qui postquam lux venit, odit lucem et diligit mala Et hoc est quod dicit: Erant enim opera eorum mala. Opera, id est impietas, et incredulitas, et odium aeternae lucis, et nolle eam aspicere, sed velle in tenebris peccatorum remanere.

VERS. 21.-- Qui autem facit. Ostenso qua justitia damnetur qui non credit, ostendit qua justitia 368Dsalvetur qui credit. Qui autem facit. Id est, cui displicent mala sua et accusat, non sibi ignoscit, non sibi parcit, sed venit ad lucem. Et incipit agere cum Deo qui mala accusat, quia initium boni est accusatio mali, ergo opera ejus, haec scilicet, quod se accusat sicut Deus accusat, manifestat, quia in Deo sunt facta, nisi enim Dei lux sibi luceret, sibi non displiceret.

VERS. 22.-- Post haec. Non continuo post disputationem cum Nicodemo quae facta est in Hierosolymis, sed peracto spatio temporis de Galilaea in Judaeam rediit. In terram Judam, et illic demorabatur, etc. Moraliter. Ubi est confessio peccatorum vel divinarum laudum, illuc venit Jesus, et discipuli 369Aejus, id est doctrina et illuminatio ejus, ubi moratur purgando a delictis, unde sequitur, et ibi morabatur et baptizabat.

Erat autem. Christo jam baptizante adhuc baptizat Joannes, quia adhuc permanet umbra. Nec debet praecursor cessare donec manifestetur veritas.

VERS. 24.-- Nondum enim missus fuerat Joannes in carcerem. Ecce aperte notat facta Christi ante Joannem incarceratum quae alii praeterierunt, incipientes ab his quae facta sunt post Joannem missum in carcerem, quando, cessante baptismo praecursoris, aperte incoepit baptismus Christi.

VERS. 25.-- Facta est autem quaestio ex discipulis Joannis, etc. Dum Joannes praedicat, et schola ejus durat, Christus latenter praedicat et operatur, 369Bsed palam baptizat, ut oriatur quaestio de baptismo Joannis, et de baptismo Christi, et illa solvatur testimoniis Joannis.

VERS. 26.-- Et venerunt ad Joannem et dixerunt ei: Rabbi, etc. Volentes accusare Christum quem paulo ante viderant a Joanne baptizatum.

VERS. 27.-- Respondit Joannes, et dixit, etc. Quia ultra consuetudinem humanae naturae sum natus ex parentibus provectae aetatis, putatis me esse ultra homines. Sed scitote quia purus sum homo, ille Deus, a quo habeo quidquid habeo. Ex me infirmitas, ex illo gratiae sublimitas. Putatis ut aliquid dicam contra meum testimonium? Ego non baptizo in remissionem peccatorum, sed ille. Non potest. Quasi dicat: Ego purus homo, nil habeo 369Cnisi quod datum est a Deo, id est baptizare in poenitentiam non in remissionem, quia non ego Christus, sed ille ut scitis me testatum fuisse vobis, ideo ad illum currite.

VERS. 28.-- Non sum ego Christus. Non sum unctus a Patre plenitudine spiritus, sed de plenitudine illius accepi, ut illum praecurrerem.

VERS. 29.-- Hoc ergo gaudium. In hoc gaudium meum est plenum, quod factus sum amicus sponsi, et quia sto et gaudeo.

VERS. 30.-- Illum oportet crescere. Gaudeo ad vocem sponsi. Ille sponsus crescet, id est invenietur esse Christus, qui putabatur propheta. Ego minuar, quia ejus propheta inveniar, qui Christus putabar. Illum oportet crescere. Id est exaltari, id est dum 369Dpeccata dimittit, gratiam dat, in quo gloria ejus quae in se semper est perfecta, crescit in hominem, dum eam homo plus videt et habet. Homo minuitur qui ante in se gloriabatur, dummodo peccata confitetur, et accipit quod ille dat. Cui rei attestatur passio utriusque: Christus in cruce exaltatur, Joannes capite minuitur. Attestatur et creatura. Joannes natus est decrescentibus diebus, Christus vero crescentibus.

VERS. 31.-- Me autem minui. Joannes figura legis est, Christus, veritas, lex minuitur, apparente veritate.

Qui desursum venit, super omnes est, etc. (ALC.) Id est, de altitudine humanae naturae ante peccatum 370Aprimi parentis, etc., usque ad secundo juxta altitudinem Patris, cui est aequalis.

De terra. Id est, qui est de terrenis parentibus natus, a quibus peccatum et poenam peccati contraxit, ex se nihil est nisi terra.

Et quod vidit. Cum ipse Filius Dei sit verbum Patris, non aliud verbum Patris audit vel hominibus testatur, sed se verbum quod Pater locutus est, id est, edidit et genuit ex se, scit et indicat hominibus esse ex Patre. Hoc Evangelista dicit, ut potuit, quod plene dici vel intelligi nequit.

VERS. 33, 34.-- Signavit quia Deus. Hoc est, signum posuit in corde suo quasi speciale aliquid, scilicet hunc esse verum Deum, qui missus est ad salutem hominum. Verax est. Quem enim misit Deus. 370BOmnis homo ex se mendax; non enim vera dicere potest, nisi illuminatus a Deo. Quem enim misit Deus Pater, verba Dei loquitur, quod est? Quem misit Deus, Deus est: ideo verba Dei loquitur, ergo verba Filii de se testimonium perhibentis quod Deus sit, verba Dei sunt. Verba Dei loquitur. In hoc se distinguit a Christo, sed quia missus et verba Dei loquitur, apertius addit: Non enim ad mensuram dat Deus spiritum. Pater diligit Filium et omnia dedit in manu ejus. Hominibus dat ad mensuram, filio non dat ad mensuram, sed sicut totum ex seipso toto genuit Filium suum, ita incarnato Filio suo totum spiritum suum dedit, non particulatim, non per subdivisiones, sed generaliter et universaliter, et non ob aliud dat nisi quia Pater diligit Filium.

370CVERS. 36.-- Qui credit in Filium habet vitam aeternam: qui autem, etc. Ac si diceret, qui credit in Filium habet ipsum Filium, et per hoc habet vitam. Sed ira Dei manet super eum. Ira cum qua natus est, permanet, et ideo non ait, venit, sed manet, quia per Filium missum, qui est vita, non tollitur effectus illius irae, scilicet mors, corruptio, etc., quae omnia manent.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1.-- And there was a man, etc. This man was one of those who had believed through the signs. Nicodemus by name. Nicodemus, the victory of the people, or the victor of the people, bearing the figure of all those who, from the Jewish people believing in Christ, overcome the world through faith.

VERS. 2.-- He came, etc. Night signifies the letter of the law, or the ignorance of the heart, or fear. And perhaps he came by night because, as a master in Israel, he would blush to speak openly, or from dread of the Jews. Because he had prudently noted the manifest signs, he asks more fully after the mysteries 366Bof faith; and therefore he deserved to be taught concerning the deity of Christ and his twofold nativity, concerning the passion, the resurrection and the ascension, concerning the second generation and the entry into the kingdom of heaven, and concerning many other things.

VERS. 3.-- Amen, amen I say. To one who does not ask he expounds the second nativity, lest Nicodemus should believe that faith alone, on account of which, the signs being seen, he had believed in Jesus, suffices for salvation without the power of spiritual regeneration and without the sacraments.
