Also, because Nicodemus understood him to be a mere man, like John the Baptist or any one of the prophets, therefore Jesus began to instruct him concerning the second regeneration, by which he understands him to be the Son of God, and himself able to be made a son of God.

(AUG.) The second nativity of which Jesus teaches is spiritual, 366Cwhich is of God and the Church unto life; but that man is minded only of the carnal, which is of Adam and Eve unto death. But as he says that the carnal cannot be repeated, so neither can the spiritual, by whomsoever it is performed, be repeated. For they are born of the seed of the true Abraham, whether through the free woman or through the handmaid.

Unless a man. The visible sacrament of water is necessary for the washing of the visible body, as the doctrine of invisible faith is necessary also for the sanctification of the invisible soul.

Shall have been born again. (CHRYS.) That is, if thou hast not been born from above, and hast not received the certainty of the doctrines, thou wanderest somewhere outside and art far from the kingdom of heaven; showing and pointing to himself here, since it is not only this that is seen, but there is need of other eyes 366Dfor the seeing of God.

VERS. 5.-- Of water. Of water, the visible sacrament, and of the Spirit, the invisible understanding: so that a man may visibly receive the symbol of baptism, and may bring to completion the spiritual understanding of that same symbol. Or: of visible water and of the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 6.-- That which is born. He distinguishes this spiritual nativity from the carnal, saying: That which is born. Of the flesh. That is, that which is from the first man by the succession of the flesh is carnal. By the name of flesh he signifies not only the flesh, but the whole carnal man. So, on the contrary, the whole spiritual man, in soul and body, is called spirit. And that which, etc. By the word and by the sacrament the Spirit is invisibly present, whereby he who is born 367Aof Adam unto death is born a son of God unto life: who is called not only spiritual, but spirit, because, as the substance of a spirit is invisible, so invisibly, through the visible sacrament, he is made a son of God. Is spirit. But do not wonder if I so call what is born spirit, because, as the spirit breathes where it will, and thou knowest not whence it comes or whither it goes, so is everyone that is born of the Spirit.

VERS. 8.-- The Spirit where he will. By which he has in his power whose heart he shall enlighten. Thou hearest his voice in the Scriptures, or when someone full of the Holy Spirit speaks; and even if thou hearest it, thou knowest not whence it comes, that is, how it entered that man's heart, or how it returned, because its nature is invisible. So is everyone that is born of the Holy Spirit, because, by the invisible 367Bworking of the Spirit, he begins to be what he was not, so that the unbeliever knows not whence he comes and whither he goes, that is, by what grace he comes into the adoption of a son, and goes into the kingdom of God. And because these things are unknown to the carnal man, he asks again? How can these things be done, not having the spiritual understanding within the letter of the law; he thinks impossible what is said of the nativity through the Spirit, whence he is reproved by the Lord: Thou art a master, etc. And his voice, etc. The voice of the Spirit is the voice of Christ, which Nicodemus heard; but because he is not yet born of the Spirit, he knows not whence it comes or whither it goes, that is, by what occasion he wills a man to be born in the Spirit, or whither it goes, that is, to what perfection he says that man is born of him. For he who is born of him is made one spirit with him by grace, 367Cas he himself also is one spirit by nature.

VERS. 10.-- Thou art a master, etc. He does not insult him, so that he who is set over all should be held superior to him; but because he wills him to be born of the Spirit, which he cannot be unless he is humble, he humbles one swollen with his mastership. As if he should say: Proud prince, thou knowest nothing; humble thyself as did he who emptied himself, and, born of the Spirit, thou shalt know what now thou knowest not.

VERS. 11.-- We speak what we know. That is, because he himself is the knowledge, the speech and the word of the Father. And what we have seen we testify. Because he himself is the vision, and the true testimony and truth of the Father. Which, being in the plural, intimates the mystery of the Trinity.

VERS. 12.-- If I have spoken to you earthly things. (AUG.) And because you do not receive earthly things, how can you take in the heavenly, 367Dthat is, the heavenly nativity of the Spirit? since you not only do not believe, but do not even understand earthly things, namely what I said of the temple of my body (which I took from the earth), to be destroyed by you, to be raised up by me.

VERS. 13.-- But he that descended. This pertains to the second nativity in the Spirit, because no one ascends in Christ to the Father but he who is born of the Spirit. No one is saved through the death of Christ but he who is born of the Spirit. No one is born of the Spirit but in the death of Christ. And when he says the Son of man, he gives us the assurance of ascending, since we are sons of men.

VERS. 14.-- And as Moses lifted up the serpent in the desert. He puts forward his own death, which is the origin 368Aand the cause of having the things aforesaid, and in faith of it he instructs him. And because he was carnal, and versed in the law, therefore he brings forward a certain part of the law, which he shows to be a figure and significative of his own death, so that in this way it may be plain of the other parts of the law also that they are in figure. And because death is understood by the serpent, and Christ dead is called a serpent, whom he who beholds with faith is set free; as by the beholding of that other one in the figure they are set free from the fiery serpents, which signify the incitements of the vices. So must the Son of man be lifted up, etc. He invites the master of the law to the signification of the law. And because he had spoken of the Son of man, lest Nicodemus should think this only, he subjoins that the same is also the Son of God. For God so loved. He opens the cause of human salvation, namely, the love of God the Father, from whom all 368Brestoration is through his Son in the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 16.-- The world. Cosmos in Greek, which in Latin is rendered mundus, is called adornment. By the world, therefore, human nature is signified, not because according to the body it consists of the four elements, but because according to the soul it has been adorned by God and made to the image of God; wherefore God loves it to this end, that he may make it eternal.

That he would give his only-begotten Son, that everyone who believes, etc. Behold, he says the same of the Son of God which he said above of the Son of man, so that by him through whom we were founded in the deity, through the same we may be restored in the man.

VERS. 19.-- And this is the judgment. Why is he who does not believe judged? Because the light came which rouses 368Cmen and admonishes them to know their own evils, in which all men are; but some hate the light that admonishes, and flee, lest their evils which they love should be reproved. Others, admonished by the light, accuse their own evils, which is to do the truth, and so they are enlightened. For theirs were. He who is in darkness and loves the light is not evil; but he is called evil, and his works evil, who, after the light has come, hates the light and loves evil things And this is what he says: For their works were evil. Works, that is, impiety, and unbelief, and hatred of the eternal light, and the unwillingness to look upon it, but the will to remain in the darkness of sins.

VERS. 21.-- But he that doeth. Having shown by what justice he is condemned who does not believe, he shows by what justice 368Dhe is saved who believes. But he that doeth. That is, he to whom his own evils are displeasing, and who accuses them, does not pardon himself, does not spare himself, but comes to the light. And he begins to act with God who accuses his own evils, because the beginning of good is the accusation of evil. Therefore his works, these namely, that he accuses himself as God accuses, he manifests, because they are done in God; for unless the light of God shone upon him, he would not be displeasing to himself.

VERS. 22.-- After these things. Not immediately after the disputation with Nicodemus, which took place in Jerusalem, but when a space of time had passed he returned from Galilee into Judea. Into the land of Juda, and there he abode, etc. [Vulg. Jo 3:22 in terram Judaeam: et illic demorabatur cum eis.] Morally. Where there is confession of sins or of the divine praises, thither comes Jesus, and his disciples, 369Athat is, his doctrine and his enlightening, where he abides, cleansing from offences; whence there follows, and there he stayed and baptized. [Vulg. Jo 3:22 et illic demorabatur cum eis, et baptizabat; the same clause stands above, in the lemma, as illic demorabatur.]

And there was. Christ now baptizing, John still baptizes, because the shadow still remains. Nor ought the forerunner to cease until the truth be made manifest.

VERS. 24.-- For John was not yet cast into prison. Behold, he plainly notes the deeds of Christ before John was imprisoned, which the others passed over, beginning from those things which were done after John was sent into prison, when, the baptism of the forerunner ceasing, the baptism of Christ openly began.

VERS. 25.-- And there arose a question from among John's disciples, etc. While John preaches and his school endures, Christ preaches and works in hiding, 369Bbut baptizes openly, so that a question may arise concerning the baptism of John and the baptism of Christ, and that it may be resolved by the testimonies of John.

VERS. 26.-- And they came to John and said to him: Rabbi, etc. Wishing to accuse Christ, whom a little before they had seen baptized by John.

VERS. 27.-- John answered, and said, etc. Because beyond the custom of human nature I was born of parents of advanced age, you think me to be beyond men. But know that I am a mere man, and he is God, from whom I have whatever I have. From me, weakness; from him, the sublimity of grace. Do you think that I would say anything against my own testimony? I do not baptize unto the remission of sins, but he does. He cannot. As if he should say: I, a mere man, have nothing 369Cexcept what is given from God, that is, to baptize unto penance, not unto remission; because I am not the Christ, but he is, as you know that I testified to you; therefore run ye to him.

VERS. 28.-- I am not the Christ. I am not anointed by the Father with the fullness of the Spirit, but of his fullness I have received, that I might run before him.

VERS. 29.-- This joy therefore. In this my joy is full, that I have been made the friend of the bridegroom, and because I stand and rejoice.

VERS. 30.-- He must increase. I rejoice at the voice of the bridegroom. That bridegroom shall increase, that is, he shall be found to be Christ, who was thought a prophet. I shall be diminished, because I shall be found his prophet, I who was thought to be Christ. He must increase. That is, be exalted; that is, while 369Dhe forgives sins and gives grace, in which his glory, which in itself is always perfect, grows into man, while man sees and holds more of it. Man is diminished, who before gloried in himself, so long as he confesses his sins and receives what that other gives. To which thing the passion of each bears witness: Christ is exalted upon the cross, John is diminished by the head. The creation too bears witness. John was born while the days were decreasing, but Christ while they were increasing.

VERS. 31.-- But I to be diminished. John is a figure of the law; Christ is the truth. The law is diminished when the truth appears.

He that cometh from above is above all, etc. (ALC.) That is, from the height of human nature before the sin of 370Athe first parent, etc., as far as secondly, according to the height of the Father, to whom he is equal.

Of the earth. That is, he who is born of earthly parents, from whom he has contracted sin and the penalty of sin, is of himself nothing but earth.

And what he hath seen. Since the Son of God is himself the Word of the Father, he does not hear another word of the Father nor testify another to men, but himself the Word which the Father has spoken, that is, has uttered and begotten out of himself: he knows and declares to men that he is from the Father. This the Evangelist says as he was able, which cannot fully be said or understood.

VERS. 33, 34.-- Hath set his seal that God. That is, he set a sign in his heart as something special, namely that this is the true God, who was sent for the salvation of men. Is true. For he whom God hath sent. 370BEvery man is of himself a liar; for he cannot speak true things unless he is enlightened by God. For he whom God the Father sent speaks the words of God, what is that? He whom God sent is God: therefore he speaks the words of God; therefore the words of the Son, bearing witness of himself that he is God, are the words of God. He speaketh the words of God. In this he distinguishes himself from Christ; but because he is sent and speaks the words of God, he adds more openly: For God doth not give the Spirit by measure. The Father loveth the Son, and hath given all things into his hand. To men he gives by measure; to the Son he does not give by measure, but as whole out of his whole self he begot his own Son, so to his own Son made flesh he gave his whole Spirit, not piecemeal, not by subdivisions, but generally and universally; and he gives it for no other cause save because the Father loves the Son.

370CVERS. 36.-- He that believeth in the Son hath life everlasting; but he that, etc. As if he should say: he who believes in the Son has the Son himself, and through this has life. But the wrath of God abideth on him. The wrath with which he was born remains; and therefore he does not say, cometh, but abideth, because through the Son who was sent, who is life, the effect of that wrath is not taken away, namely death, corruption, etc., all which remain.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Ut ergo cognovit. Sciebat Jesus scientiam eorum et invidiam, quod non ideo didicerant de eo ut eum sequerentur, sed persequerentur, et ideo fugit, docens suos cedere malis, non timens, vel evadere eos impotens. Et baptizat. Quod vere dicitur, ipse enim semper mundat in spiritu invisibiliter 370Det dono, sed ejus baptismus fit etiam in corpore, qui est in duobus, scilicet in aqua et verbo vitae; unde Paulus: Mundas eam lavacro aquae et verbo vitae (Ephes. V).

VERS. 2.-- Sed discipuli. In quorum baptismo dabatur spiritus, licet non ita manifeste, ut post Ascensionem datus est ineffabili et inexcogitabili modo.

VERS. 3.-- Reliquit Judaeam. Id est Pharisaeos incredulos, qui cogitabant eum persequi, propter baptisma ipsius quod crescebat, et quia per doctrinam ejus, legem evacuari intelligebant. Et abiit. Postquam paucos de Judaico populo suscepit, reliquit incredulos legis litteram sequentes, quae neminem 371Aad perfectum ducit. Et abiit in Galilaeam, id est in spiritualem ipsius litterae intelligentiam. In Galilaeam. In Galilaea transformatio Domini creditur facta, per quam significatur transmutatio humanae naturae in pristinam gloriam. In Galilaea dedit discipulis in monte doctrinam per quam ad vitam transituri sunt credentes.

VERS. 4.-- Oportebat. Inter Judaeam et Galilaeam Samaria est, id est, inter legem litterae, quae per Judaeam et divinas leges circa Deum revolutas, quarum figura est Galilaea, est lex naturalis in medio constituta, quia ergo ex lege sumpsit fundamenta Ecclesiae, oportebat eum ad gentes transire constitutas sub lege naturae, ut cum utrisque in Galilaeam aeternae beatitudinis rediret.

371BVERS. 5.-- Venit ergo. Ut agnoscerent et converterentur ad eum Samaritani, vindicantes sibi haereditatem patriarchae Israel.

Quae dicitur Sichar. Sichar, id est conclusio, vel ramus, id est gentilis populus conclusus sub peccato, qui est ramus de oleastro excisus et olivae insertus.

VERS. 6.-- Erat autem, etc. Quia naturalis ratio non aliunde surgit nisi a causa omnium bonorum, ideo pulchre dicitur fons esse Jacob, id est ratio infinita Patris altitudine procedens.

Jesus ergo fatigatus, etc. Allegorice. Iter Jesu carnis est assumptio in qua est fatigatio, id est infirmitas, relevans hominem quem creavit potentia: sessio est humilitas. Sexta hora, sexta aetas qua venit, 371Cputeus profunditas hujus saeculi. Samaritana Ecclesia venit non jam justa, sed justificanda. Aqua putei voluntas hujus saeculi, quae quasi de profundo extrahitur submissis et inclinatis ad eam hominibus, praemissa cupiditate quasi hydria. Sedebat. Quasi lassitudinem relevans in quo indicatur magister docturus accedentes et possessurus. Supra. Fons dicitur ubicunque manat aqua de terra, sed si in superficie fons tantum, si in profundo etiam dicitur puteus.

VERS. 7.-- Venit. In figura Ecclesiae de gentibus, quae prius hauriebat aquam, id est voluptatem de profundo saeculi cujus fidem sitit Jesus et petit ab ea potari.

(GREG.) Vel mulier, id est Synagoga, quae venit 371Dhaurire aquam, etc., usque ad primitivam Ecclesiam.

Da mihi. Jesus sedens supra fontem petit a primitiva Ecclesia quam de gentibus elegerat potum fidei, qua in eum credatur, petit a natura potum rationis, qua conditor et redemptor investigetur. Apostoli missi in civitatem, id est in mundum, emere spirituales escas, id est fidem, et actionem, et cognitionem, quibus magistri satiantur Ecclesiae. Da mihi. Licet Jesus post fatigationem sitire posset, tamen hunc potum non exigit, sed ut cor Ecclesiae gentium pro laboris ejus cognitione potus ei efficiatur, desiderat.

VERS. 8.-- Discipuli enim. In civitatem discipuli abierunt, scilicet ad Judaeos, qui legis valle muniti, 372Aet prophetiae sunt telis armati, ut cibos emerent ipsorum, scilicet corda praedicatione fidei Deo praeparantes.

VERS. 9.-- Dicit ergo. Nesciens quem potum petit ab ea, putans quod de aqua opponit se esse de alienigenis vasculis, quorum etiam conversatione non utuntur Judaei, sed Jesus qui fidem petebat, ei spiritum dare cupiebat, et eam admonet ut petat, spiritus est autem et donum Dei et viva aqua.

Non enim, etc. Samaritanos Judaei exsecrantur et supplantatores vocant, quia eos haereditate patris sui Jacob privaverunt abstinendo a cibis et vasis eorum.

VERS. 10.-- Respondit Jesus. Pedetentim et simplicibus verbis instruit, ut tandem donum Dei, id est spiritum possit accipere.

372BVERS. 11.-- Dixit ei mulier. Aqua viva dicitur non alia, nisi quae ubi oritur, excipitur, de qua mulier carnaliter sapiens dixit: Cum hauritorium non habeas, et puteus altus sit, quomodo habes aquam vivam? Sed ne forte alium fontem promittere videretur, subdit: Nunquid tu melior es, id est potes melior esse Jacob qui cum suis usus est isto? Contra quod ergo aperte Christus ostendit se non carnaliter, sed spiritualiter loqui, dicens non sitire eum qui suam aquam biberit.

VERS. 12.-- Nunquid tu. Alienigena Jacob patrem suum vocat, quia ipsa sub lege Moysi vixerat, et praedium quod Jacob filio suo dederat, possidebat.

(AUG.) Vasculum cupiditas, aqua voluptas, reficitur, non exstinguitur.

372CEt ipse ex eo. De puteo bibunt homines et pecora, et similiter de ratione, quasi depravata et obscurata magno labore foditur et exercetur: bibunt autem quidam ut homines qui ea ratione ad bonum utuntur, ut sancti. Quidam ut pecora, qui utuntur ea ad malum ut philosophi.

VERS. 13.-- Qui autem. Quicunque meae praedicationi cor accommodaverit et gratiae donum cognoverit, fides quae per dilectionem operatur in eo nascitur, et sic tandem in vitam transibit aeternam.

VERS. 14.-- Aqua. Aqua corporalis deorsum fluit, aqua spiritualis sursum salit et secum eos qui eam bibunt in aeternam gloriam subvehit.

VERS. 16.-- Vade, voca virum tuum, etc. Ut eo 372Dduce ad contemplationem spiritualis intelligentiae ascendas: vade, voca virum tuum. Tu sensualis voca rationalem intellectum, quo ut viro regaris, tu quae modo carnaliter sapis, hunc ego lux et caput viri illuminabo. Quasi dicat: Haec quae dico non carnaliter, ut tu animalis facis, sed spiritualiter sunt intelligenda et ductu rationis accipienda, et ideo tu para intellectum mihi et veni, intelligendo haec spiritualia quae dico.

VERS. 17.-- Respondit mulier. Revera haec mulier tunc non habebat virum, sed utebatur quodam non legitimo, de quo respondet adhuc carnaliter sapiens, qui nondum vocaverat virum, id est intellectum. Dicit ei Jesus. Paulatim ea attollens adhuc 373Amysteria loquitur, ut paulatim perduceret eam ad cognoscendum quis cum ea loqueretur. Et non opus bonum, non prudentem mulieris laudat responsionem, sed veritatis laudat confessionem.

VERS. 18.-- Quinque enim viros. Quinque viri sunt quinque leges humanae naturae datae. Prima, in paradiso ante peccatum in prohibitione ligni. Secunda, post praevaricationem et expulsionem de paradiso, de multiplicatione humanae propaginis. Tertia, Noe ante diluvium de fabricanda arca. Quarta, post diluvium de divisionibus gentium. Quinta, Abraham de circumcisione et immolatione filii. His quinque legibus quasi quinque viris humana anima ab initio mundi usque ad Moysen subjecta fuit: postea data est lex litterae, quae nullam salutem contulit, 373Bunde dicitur: Quem nunc habes, non est tuus vir. Dimitte litteram, vade ad spiritum litterae ad Novum Testamentum, a quo spiritualem virtutum prolem concipias.

(AUG.) Quinque viri, id est quinque sensus corporis te rexerunt, nunc in tempore discretionis non te regit vir, id est intellectus sapientiae, sed adulter, id est error corrumpit, dum carnaliter quae dico accipis, et ideo voca virum, ut (sicut dico) spiritualiter intelligas. Ideo quinque sensus in minori aetate dicuntur viri, quia sunt legitimi. Post lapsum enim hominis et decreto Dei naturaliter regitur illa aetas, non ad aeterna, sed ad ista temporalia appetenda si mulcent, vitanda si offendunt.

VERS. 19.-- Quia propheta tu es. Mulier ex auditis 373Cproficiens, jam coeperat aliquatenus habere intellectum, unde et prophetam vocat, sed non plenum, quia non credit venisse Christum.

VERS. 20.-- Panes nostri, etc. Propheta es tu, et ideo judica de hac contentione, quae me movet. Judaei se praeferebant Samaritanis pro templo quod aedificavit Salomon, in quo adorabant. Econtra Samaritani adversum Judaeos pro monte, in quo et non in templo patres adoraverunt, qui Deo placuerunt. Et sic patriarchae in montibus, Judaei in templo adoraverunt, praefigurantes modum bene orandi.

VERS. 21.-- Mulier. In hoc loco Dominus jactantiam Samaritanorum reprimit, qui putabant se non minoris religionis fuisse quam Judaeos. Loquitur ergo in persona prophetarum et omnium qui in Christum 373Dcredunt; unde ait: Vos adoratis Patrem quem nescitis, quia nullus adorat Patrem nisi Filium adoret, per quem ad notitiam Patris venitur, sed nos scimus Filium, et per Filium Patrem, unde sequitur: Quia salus ex Judaeis est, etc., id est, quia Christus, qui est salus, ex Judaeis est, non solum origine carnis, sed etiam ex eis orta est fides et primitiva Ecclesia. Crede mihi. Quia jam est in te vir qui potest credere et ideo credere: Quia nisi credideris non intelliges (Isa.). Quando neque in monte. Ecce pariter mons vester et templum Judaeorum reprobabitur, sed in hoc estis impares, quod vos Samaritani adoratis quod nescitis, sed nos Judaei non omnes, sed electi adoramus quod scimus, quia nobis cognitio 374ADei testamentum; prophetia promissa: de nobis Christus, et hoc est, quia salus ex Judaeis est, sed quamvis ita simus differentes, tamen non praefero locum Judaeorum loco Samaritanorum, et hoc est.

VERS. 23.-- Sed venit hora, et nunc est, quando. Priusquam venirem in carne, nemo potuit adorare Patrem in spiritu et veritate, praeter patriarchas et prophetas, quibus mea incarnatio revelata fuit, sed per meam praesentiam in carne fient veri adoratores.

In spiritu et veritate. Non in templo, non in hoc monte, sed interius in intimo templo cordis et in veritate cognitionis Patrem esse adorandum.

VERS. 24.-- Spiritus est Deus. Cum Deus sit spiritus et non corpus, non quaerit corporeum locum, 374Bmontem vel templum in quo oretur, sed spiritus purus est ejus templum, in quo oretur, non de visibilibus quae transeunt, sed de aeternis quae veritas sunt.

VERS. 25, 26.-- Scio quia Messias venit, qui dicitur, etc. Jam per verba docentis amplius provecta, scit a quo perfecte debeat instrui, id est a Messia. Ille nobis annuntiabit omnia. Dicit ei Jesus. Qualiter spreto templo vel monte in spiritu et veritate adoremus ostendet, et abjectis umbris veritate illustremur. Ego sum qui loquor. Quem venturum exspectas, venisse cognosce: et adducto tecum viro qui te regat, quae fidei sunt necessaria discere labora. Et quia sciebat jam quis eam posset docere cum eum jam docentem non agnosceret, digna erat cui ipse se 374Cmanifestaret, unde dicit: Ego sum Messias, qua manifestatione accepta non habet quid ultra quaerat, unde sequitur: Reliquit ergo hydriam, etc. Sed Evangelista interserit de discipulis.

VERS. 27.-- Et mirabantur quia. Non mirabantur quod cum muliere loquitur, cum quibus loqui consueverat, sed quia cum alienigena, ignorantes mysterium Ecclesiae de gentibus futurae. Et non malum suspicantur, sed clementiam mirantur, quia gentilem erroneam docet sicut qui venit quaerere quod perierat.

VERS. 28.-- Reliquit ergo. Audito ego sum, jam habens in intellectu mulier Christum caput viri, reliquit hydriam, id est cupiditatem, et cucurrit evangelizare. Hic discant evangelizaturi prius deponere 374Dcuram, et onus hujus saeculi.

VERS. 29.-- Venite et videte. Pedetentim inducit ad veritatem illos rudes; nec statim affirmat Christum, ne irascerentur et indignarentur.

VERS. 33.-- Nunquid aliquis attulit. Non ausi sunt interrogare Dominum timentes ne ab eo reprehendantur, si incaute interrogaverint.

VERS. 34.-- Meus cibus est. Non intellexerunt escam, sicut mulier supra non intellexit aquam, unde magister eos instruit non per circuitum ut illam, sed jam aperte dicens: Meus cibus est ut faciam, etc. Voluntatem ejus. Patris. Voluntas Patris est ut credatur in eum, id est in Filium quem ipse misit. Opus Patris est nostra salus. Christi ergo cibus, nostra 375Afides et nostra salus. Facit ergo voluntatem docendo credere in se, facit opus maturando mysterium redemptionis.

VERS. 35.-- Nonne vos dicitis. Cum quaerat fidem et salutem populorum, fervet in opus, et operarios mittere disponit. Levate oculos vestros. Intellectu mentis considerate quod, transacta hieme, id est infidelitate, adest calor fidei et parata sunt corda, ut opera justitiae ex eis colligatis, quasi dicat: Juvate ad implendum voluntatem Patris. Et bene potestis, quia parata est messis, id est, videte gentes facile credentes.

VERS. 36.-- Ut et qui seminat. Utroque opus erat, seminatore et messore, quia in hoc apparet probabile verbum, quia alius seminat, alius metit; 375Bnisi enim parati essent per prophetas, non audirent apostolos.

VERS. 38.-- Ego misi vos. Vos facio messores ibi ubi alii seminaverunt, id est in Judaea, ubi prima seges collecta est, unde aliqui exeuntes in toto mundo seminaverunt, unde alia messis quasi de granis surgit colligenda in fine saeculi messoribus angelis. Laboraverunt, et vos in labores eorum introistis. Multi enim labores fuerunt patriarchis et prophetis, in quibus omnibus erat prophetia Christi, et multa passi sunt quasi in frigore seminationis.

VERS. 40.-- Cum venissent. Prius audierunt famam, post conspexerunt praesentem, nec satis est eis, sed apud se manere faciunt, ut ipsius verbis instruerentur, quae praeferunt verbis mulieris. In 375Cquibus omnibus econtrario arguitur duritia Judaeorum, qui nec miraculis credunt. Et mansit ibi duos. Moraliter. Sic quotidie apud illos qui foris sunt, nuntiatur Christus per mulierem, id est Ecclesiam, et credunt per istam famam. Inde apud eos manet per duos dies sine mentione noctis, ut lux eorum duobus praeceptis charitatis instruatur.

VERS. 42.-- Et mulieri dicebant. Postquam Dominus carnis praesentia Judaeis spiritualiter edoctis gratiam suae cognitionis infudit mulieri, id est legi, dicebant, jam non propter tuam loquelam credimus.

VERS. 43.-- Ipsi enim audivimus. Quia sibi primum moratus miracula fecerat, nec nisi pauci in eum crediderunt, unde super viso signo crediderunt in eum discipuli ejus, non alii: et ideo a Samaritanis 375Dqui facile nullo signo viso crediderunt, revertitur ad istos, ut qui per priora non crediderunt, modo credant. Significat quod a gentibus facile conversis, in fine saeculi revertetur ad Judaeos qui sunt sua patria, quia prius in eis honorem non habuerat.

VERS. 44.-- Quia propheta. Allegorice. Patria Christi est populus Judaeorum, ubi sine honore est, quia pauci de his credunt quod de Galilaeis civibus Christi dicitur. Ecclesia gentium facile credens similis est Samaritanis, et hi multi solo verbo praedicationis, illi per miracula et pauci. Ecce cur venit, ideo revertitur, quia testatus est quod propheta non habet honorem in patria; alioquin videtur non debuisse ad hos reverti, ubi honorem non habet, 376Ased manere apud illos ubi bene receptus est.

VERS. 46.-- Et erat quidam regulus cujus filius. Iste regulus potest figurare aliquem gentis Judaicae doctorem, qui tandem post plenam Ecclesiae gentium conversionem ad quam Dominus a Judaeis expulsus per discipulos transmigravit, pro filio, Judaico populo, quem non nisi in fine saeculi fidem accepturum cognoscit, orat, ut de longa perfidiae infirmitate sanetur.

VERS. 47.-- Et rogabat. Jam credens quod posset sanare filium ejus, sed quia per deitatem, quae ubique est, non credit filio adesse, rogat ut descenderet et praesens corpore sanaret, unde infidelitatis arguitur: Nisi signa et prodigia, etc. Econtrario centurio laudatur, qui dicit: Dic verbo et sanabitur puer 376Bmeus (Matth. VIII); unde: Non inveni tantam fidem in Israel. Vel forsitan cupiebat tentare qualis esset Christus, unde arguitur: Nisi signa et prodigia. Propter salutem filii credidit ipse et domus ejus tota.

VERS. 48.-- Nisi signa. In hoc redarguitur de incredulitate, quod non ubique credebat eum esse, et per hujus duritiam significatur duritia Judaeorum, qui nec visis miraculis credunt. Vere econtra per Samariam signatur fides gentium, quae solo verbo credidit, hoc autem, quantum ad allegoriam, a primo Christi adventu usque post regnum Antichristi in perfidis Judaeis durabit.

Et prodigia. Prodigium quasi porro dictum, quasi quod aliquid significat. Vel, prodigium quasi procul a digito, quia praefigurat aliquod remotum et futurum.

376CVERS. 49, 50.-- Descende. Quasi aliter non posset sanare, nisi praesens esset. Unde Dominus indicans se non deesse ubi invitatur, solo jussu sanat, unde: Vade, filius tuus. Ad filium reguli non vadit, ne divitias honorare videatur, ad servum centurionis ire promittit qui naturam hominis non despicit: in quo superbiam destruit quae in hominibus non pensat naturam, sed quae extra patent. Credidit ipse. Non alii cives, in quo paucitas fidei Judaeorum signatur, quamvis signa viderint, unde infert: Hoc iterum secundum signum fecit Jesus. Sicut supra multitudo gentium solo verbo convertendarum in Samaritanis signabatur.

VERS. 54.-- Hoc iterum secundum signum fecit 376DJesus cum venisset a Judaea in Galilaeam. De omnibus miraculis quae fecit Jesus in anno ante mortem Joannis, non facit Joannes mentionem nisi de istis, et de disputatione Christi cum Judaeis, quae supra ponitur. Caeteri enim Evangelistae quae hic reticet, dixerunt plane.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- When therefore he knew. Jesus knew their knowledge and their envy: that they had not learned of him in order to follow him, but to persecute him; and therefore he fled, teaching his own to give way before evils, not because he feared, nor because he was powerless to escape them. And baptizeth. Which is truly said, for he himself always cleanses in the spirit invisibly 370Dand by gift; but his baptism is wrought also in the body, and it consists in two things, namely in water and the word of life; whence Paul: Thou cleansest her by the laver of water and by the word of life (Ephes. V) [Vulg. Eph 5:26 mundans lavacro aquae in verbo vitae, "cleansing her by the laver of water in the word of life"].

VERS. 2.-- But the disciples. In whose baptism the Spirit was given, though not so manifestly as it was given after the Ascension, in an ineffable and unimaginable manner.

VERS. 3.-- He left Judaea. That is, the unbelieving Pharisees, who were planning to persecute him because of his baptism, which was increasing, and because they understood that by his teaching the law was being made void. And he went away. After he had received a few of the Jewish people, he left behind the unbelieving who follow the letter of the law, which leads no one 371Ato perfection. And he went away into Galilee, that is, into the spiritual understanding of that same letter. Into Galilee. In Galilee the Lord's transformation is believed to have taken place, by which is signified the transmutation of human nature into its pristine glory. In Galilee he gave the disciples on the mountain the teaching by which believers are to pass over into life.

VERS. 4.-- He was obliged. Between Judaea and Galilee lies Samaria: that is, between the law of the letter, which [is signified] by Judaea, and the divine laws revolved about God, whose figure is Galilee, the natural law is set in the midst. Because therefore he took the foundations of the Church from the law, it behoved him to pass over to the Gentiles set under the law of nature, that with both he might return into the Galilee of eternal blessedness.

371BVERS. 5.-- He cometh therefore. That the Samaritans might acknowledge him and be converted to him, they who claimed for themselves the inheritance of the patriarch Israel.

Which is called Sichar. Sichar, that is, an enclosing, or a branch: that is, the Gentile people shut up under sin, which is a branch cut out of the wild olive and grafted into the olive.

VERS. 6.-- Now there was, etc. Because natural reason rises from no other source than from the cause of all good things, therefore it is beautifully said to be the fount of Jacob, that is, the infinite reason proceeding from the height of the Father.

Jesus therefore, being wearied, etc. Allegorically. The journey of Jesus is the assumption of flesh, in which there is weariness, that is, infirmity, relieving the man whom his power created: the sitting is humility. The sixth hour, the sixth age in which he came; 371Cthe well, the depth of this world. The Samaritan woman is the Church, coming not now just, but to be justified. The water of the well is the will of this world, which is drawn as it were out of the deep by men let down and bent toward it, with covetousness sent before like a water-pot. He was sitting. As one relieving weariness, wherein is indicated the master who is to teach those who draw near and to possess them. Upon. It is called a fount wherever water flows from the earth; but if at the surface, a fount only; if in the deep, it is also called a well.

VERS. 7.-- There cometh. In the figure of the Church of the Gentiles, which formerly drew water, that is, the pleasure of the deep of the world; whose faith Jesus thirsts for, and asks to be given drink by her.

(GREG.) Or the woman, that is, the Synagogue, who came 371Dto draw water, etc., as far as the primitive Church.

Give me. Jesus, sitting upon the fount, asks of the primitive Church, which he had chosen out of the Gentiles, the drink of faith by which men believe in him; he asks of nature the drink of reason by which the Creator and Redeemer is searched out. The apostles are sent into the city, that is, into the world, to buy spiritual foods, that is, faith and action and knowledge, with which the teachers of the Church are filled. Give me. Although Jesus could thirst after his weariness, yet he does not demand this drink, but he desires that the heart of the Church of the Gentiles should be made a draught for him in acknowledgement of his labour.

VERS. 8.-- For the disciples. The disciples went away into the city, namely to the Jews, who are fenced by the valley of the law 372Aand armed with the darts of prophecy, that they might buy their foods, namely preparing their hearts for God by the preaching of the faith.

VERS. 9.-- She saith therefore. Not knowing what drink he asks of her, and thinking that it was of the water, she objects that she is of the vessels of strangers, whose company also the Jews do not use; but Jesus, who was asking for faith, desired to give her the Spirit, and admonishes her to ask; and the Spirit is both the gift of God and living water.

For not, etc. The Jews execrate the Samaritans and call them supplanters, because they deprived them of the inheritance of their father Jacob by abstaining from their foods and their vessels.

VERS. 10.-- Jesus answered. Step by step and in simple words he instructs her, that at length she may be able to receive the gift of God, that is, the Spirit.

372BVERS. 11.-- The woman said to him. That is called living water, and no other, save that which is caught where it rises; of which the woman, savouring carnally, said: Since thou hast nothing to draw with, and the well is deep, how hast thou living water? But lest perchance he should seem to promise another fount, she adds: Art thou better, that is, canst thou be better than Jacob, who used this one with his own? Against which therefore Christ openly shows that he speaks not carnally but spiritually, saying that he shall not thirst who has drunk his water.

VERS. 12.-- Art thou. Being a stranger, she calls Jacob her father, because she herself had lived under the law of Moses, and possessed the field which Jacob had given to his son.

(AUG.) The little vessel is covetousness, the water is pleasure; it is refreshed, not extinguished.

372CAnd he himself drank thereof. Men and cattle drink of the well, and likewise of reason: as though depraved and darkened, it is dug out and worked with great labour. But some drink as men, who use that reason for good, as the saints; some as cattle, who use it for evil, as the philosophers.

VERS. 13.-- But he that. Whosoever shall lend his heart to my preaching and shall acknowledge the gift of grace, the faith which works through love is born in him, and so at length he shall pass over into life eternal.

VERS. 14.-- Water. Bodily water flows downward; spiritual water leaps upward, and carries up with itself into eternal glory those who drink it.

VERS. 16.-- Go, call thy husband, etc. That under his leading 372Dthou mayest ascend to the contemplation of spiritual understanding: go, call thy husband. Thou who art sensual, call the rational understanding, that thou mayest be governed by it as by a husband; thou who now savourest carnally, him will I, the light and head of the man, enlighten. As if he should say: These things which I say are to be understood not carnally, as thou, being animal, dost, but spiritually, and are to be received under the leading of reason; and therefore do thou prepare thy understanding for me and come, by understanding these spiritual things which I say.

VERS. 17.-- The woman answered. In truth this woman then had no husband, but was making use of a certain man not lawful, of whom she answers, still savouring carnally, she who had not yet called her husband, that is, her understanding. Jesus saith to her. Raising her little by little, he speaks still 373Ain mysteries, that little by little he might bring her to know who it was that spoke with her. And he praises not the good work, nor the prudent answer of the woman, but he praises the confession of the truth.

VERS. 18.-- For thou hast had five husbands. The five husbands are the five laws given to human nature. The first, in paradise before sin, in the prohibition of the tree. The second, after the transgression and the expulsion from paradise, concerning the multiplication of the human stock. The third, to Noah before the flood, concerning the building of the ark. The fourth, after the flood, concerning the divisions of the nations. The fifth, to Abraham, concerning circumcision and the immolation of his son. To these five laws, as to five husbands, the human soul was subject from the beginning of the world until Moses: afterward the law of the letter was given, which conferred no salvation; 373Bwhence it is said: He whom thou now hast is not thy husband. Put away the letter; go to the spirit of the letter, to the New Testament, from which thou mayest conceive a spiritual offspring of virtues.

(AUG.) The five husbands, that is, the five senses of the body, ruled thee; now, in the time of discretion, a husband, that is, the understanding of wisdom, does not rule thee, but an adulterer, that is, error, corrupts thee, while thou receivest carnally the things I say; and therefore call thy husband, that (as I say) thou mayest understand spiritually. Therefore the five senses in the lesser age are called husbands, because they are lawful. For after the fall of man, and by the decree of God, that age is naturally governed, not toward eternal things, but toward the seeking of these temporal things if they soothe, and the shunning of them if they offend.

VERS. 19.-- That thou art a prophet. The woman, profiting 373Cfrom what she had heard, had already begun to have understanding in some measure, whence she also calls him a prophet; but not full understanding, because she does not believe that Christ has come.

VERS. 20.-- Our loaves, etc. [Vulg. Jo 4:20 Patres nostri in monte hoc adoraverunt, "our fathers adored on this mountain"; Migne prints Panes, read at the plate, and the gloss that follows expounds patres.] Thou art a prophet, and therefore judge concerning this contention which moves me. The Jews set themselves before the Samaritans on account of the temple which Solomon built, in which they worshipped. On the contrary the Samaritans set themselves against the Jews on account of the mountain, on which, and not in the temple, the fathers worshipped who pleased God. And so the patriarchs worshipped on the mountains, the Jews in the temple, prefiguring the manner of praying well.

VERS. 21.-- Woman. In this place the Lord represses the boasting of the Samaritans, who thought that they had been of no less religion than the Jews. He speaks therefore in the person of the prophets and of all who believe in Christ; 373Dwhence he says: You adore the Father whom you know not, [Vulg. Jo 4:22 vos adoratis quod nescitis, "you adore that which you know not"] because no one adores the Father unless he adore the Son, through whom one comes to the knowledge of the Father; but we know the Son, and through the Son the Father; whence follows: For salvation is of the Jews, etc., that is, because Christ, who is salvation, is of the Jews, not only by the origin of the flesh, but from them also arose the faith and the primitive Church. Believe me. Because there is already in thee a husband who can believe, and therefore to believe: For unless thou shalt have believed, thou shalt not understand (Isa.) [Vulg. Is 7:9 si non credideritis, non permanebitis, "if you will not believe, you shall not continue"]. When neither on the mountain. Behold, your mountain and the temple of the Jews shall alike be reprobated; but in this you are unequal, that you Samaritans adore what you know not, but we Jews, not all, but the elect, adore what we know, because to us is the knowledge 374Aof God, the testament, the promised prophecy: of us is Christ; and this is, for salvation is of the Jews, but although we are thus different, yet I do not prefer the place of the Jews to the place of the Samaritans, and this is.

VERS. 23.-- But the hour cometh, and now is, when. Before I came in the flesh, no one could adore the Father in spirit and in truth, except the patriarchs and the prophets, to whom my incarnation had been revealed; but through my presence in the flesh there shall be true adorers.

In spirit and in truth. Not in the temple, not on this mountain, but inwardly, in the innermost temple of the heart, and in the truth of knowledge, is the Father to be adored.

VERS. 24.-- God is a spirit. Since God is a spirit and not a body, he does not seek a bodily place, 374Ba mountain or a temple, in which he may be prayed to; but a pure spirit is his temple, in which he is to be prayed to, not concerning visible things which pass away, but concerning the eternal things which are truth.

VERS. 25, 26.-- I know that the Messias cometh, who is called, etc. Now advanced further by the words of him who taught her, she knows by whom she ought to be perfectly instructed, that is, by the Messias. He will tell us all things. Jesus saith to her. He will show in what manner, the temple and the mountain being despised, we may adore in spirit and in truth, and how, the shadows being cast away, we may be illumined by the truth. I am he that speak. Him whom thou awaitest as one to come, know that he has come; and, having brought with thee the husband who is to govern thee, labour to learn the things that are necessary to faith. And because she already knew who could teach her, although she did not recognize him who was already teaching her, she was worthy that he should manifest himself to her; 374Cwhence he says: I am the Messias, which manifestation once received, she has nothing further to seek; whence follows: She left therefore her water-pot, etc. But the Evangelist interposes somewhat concerning the disciples.

VERS. 27.-- And they wondered that. They did not wonder that he was speaking with a woman, with such as he had been accustomed to speak with, but that it was with a stranger, being ignorant of the mystery of the Church that was to be of the Gentiles. And they suspect no evil, but wonder at his clemency, because he teaches a Gentile woman in error, as he who came to seek that which had perished.

VERS. 28.-- She left therefore. Having heard I am he, the woman, now holding Christ in her understanding as the head of the man, left the water-pot, that is, covetousness, and ran to preach the gospel. Here let those who are to preach the gospel learn first to lay aside 374Dthe care and the burden of this world.

VERS. 29.-- Come and see. Step by step she leads those rude ones to the truth; nor does she at once affirm him to be Christ, lest they should be angered and take offence.

VERS. 33.-- Hath any man brought. They did not dare to question the Lord, fearing lest they should be reproved by him if they questioned incautiously.

VERS. 34.-- My meat is. They did not understand the food, just as the woman above did not understand the water; whence the master instructs them, not by a circuit as he did her, but now openly, saying: My meat is to do, etc. His will. The Father's. The will of the Father is that men believe in him, that is, in the Son whom he himself sent. The work of the Father is our salvation. Christ's meat, therefore, is our 375Afaith and our salvation. He does the will, then, by teaching men to believe in him; he does the work by bringing the mystery of redemption to maturity.

VERS. 35.-- Do you not say. Since he seeks the faith and the salvation of the peoples, he grows fervent for the work and disposes to send labourers. Lift up your eyes. Consider with the understanding of the mind that, the winter being past, that is, unbelief, the warmth of faith is at hand and hearts are made ready, that you may gather from them the works of justice; as if he should say: Help toward the fulfilling of the Father's will. And well can you do it, because the harvest is ready, that is, see the Gentiles believing easily.

VERS. 36.-- That both he that soweth. Both were needed, the sower and the reaper, because in this the word appears probable, that one sows and another reaps; 375Bfor unless they had been made ready through the prophets, they would not have heard the apostles.

VERS. 38.-- I have sent you. I make you reapers there where others have sown, that is, in Judaea, where the first crop was gathered, whence some going forth have sown in the whole world, whence another harvest rises as it were from the grains, to be gathered at the end of the world by the angels as reapers. They have laboured, and you have entered into their labours. For many were the labours of the patriarchs and the prophets, in all of which was the prophecy of Christ, and they suffered many things as it were in the cold of the sowing.

VERS. 40.-- When they were come. First they heard the report, afterward they beheld him present; and it is not enough for them, but they make him abide with them, that they may be instructed by his own words, which they prefer to the words of the woman. In 375Call which, on the contrary, the hardness of the Jews is reproved, who do not believe even at miracles. And he abode there two. Morally. So daily among those who are without, Christ is announced through the woman, that is, the Church, and they believe through that report. Thence he abides with them for two days without mention of night, that their light may be instructed by the two precepts of charity.

VERS. 42.-- And they said to the woman. After the Lord, by the presence of the flesh, the Jews being spiritually taught, had poured the grace of his own knowledge into the woman, that is, into the law, they said: now not on account of thy speech do we believe.

VERS. 43.-- For we ourselves have heard. Because at the first, while he tarried among them, he had wrought miracles, and yet not any believed in him save a few; whence, upon the sign that was seen, his disciples believed in him, and no others: and therefore from the Samaritans, 375Dwho believed easily with no sign seen, he returns to these, that they who did not believe through the former things may now believe. It signifies that from the Gentiles, easily converted, he shall return at the end of the world to the Jews, who are his own country, because among them he had not before had honour.

VERS. 44.-- That a prophet. Allegorically. Christ's country is the people of the Jews, where he is without honour, because few of these believe what is said of the Galileans, Christ's fellow-citizens. The Church of the Gentiles, believing easily, is like the Samaritans; and these are many, by the word of preaching alone, those by miracles, and few. Behold why he came, and therefore he returns: because he testified that a prophet has not honour in his own country; otherwise it would seem that he ought not to have returned to these, where he has not honour, 376Abut to have remained with those among whom he was well received.

VERS. 46.-- And there was a certain ruler whose son. This ruler may figure some teacher of the Jewish nation, who at length, after the full conversion of the Church of the Gentiles, to which the Lord, driven out by the Jews, passed over through his disciples, prays for his son, the Jewish people, whom he knows will not receive the faith save at the end of the world, that he may be healed of the long infirmity of his faithlessness.

VERS. 47.-- And he besought. Already believing that he could heal his son; but because he does not believe him to be present to the son through the Godhead, which is everywhere, he asks that he would come down and, being present in body, heal him; whence he is convicted of unbelief: Unless signs and wonders, etc. On the contrary the centurion is praised, who says: Say the word, and my servant 376Bshall be healed (Matth. VIII); whence: I have not found so great faith in Israel. Or perhaps he was desiring to try what manner of man Christ was; whence he is reproved: Unless signs and wonders. For the salvation of his son he himself believed, and his whole house.

VERS. 48.-- Unless signs. In this he is convicted of unbelief, that he did not believe him to be everywhere; and by this man's hardness is signified the hardness of the Jews, who do not believe even when miracles are seen. Truly, on the contrary, by Samaria is signified the faith of the Gentiles, which believed by the word alone; and this, as far as regards the allegory, shall endure in the faithless Jews from Christ's first coming until after the reign of Antichrist.

And wonders. Prodigium as it were porro dictum, spoken far off, as it were that which signifies something. Or, prodigium as it were procul a digito, far from the finger, because it prefigures something remote and to come.

376CVERS. 49, 50.-- Come down. As though he could not heal otherwise unless he were present. Whence the Lord, showing that he is not absent where he is invited, heals by his command alone; whence: Go, thy son. He does not go to the ruler's son, lest he should seem to honour riches; he promises to go to the centurion's servant, he who does not despise the nature of man: wherein he destroys the pride which in men weighs not nature, but the things that lie open outwardly. He himself believed. Not the other citizens; wherein is signified the fewness of the faith of the Jews, although they had seen signs; whence he infers: This is again the second sign that Jesus did. Just as above the multitude of the Gentiles, who were to be converted by the word alone, was signified in the Samaritans.

VERS. 54.-- This is again the second sign that Jesus did 376Dwhen he was come out of Judaea into Galilee. Of all the miracles which Jesus did in the year before John's death, John makes mention of none but these, and of Christ's disputation with the Jews, which is set down above. For the other Evangelists have plainly told the things which he here passes over in silence.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 4.-- Angelus autem. Ecce duo miracula: unum invisibiliter per angelum in piscina, alterum visibiliter a Domino. In utroque unus sanatus, ut unitas commendetur. Alioquin nec in potestate ideo magnum et in benignitate parum fuit, unum de tot sanari ab eo qui omnes uno poterat erigere verbo. 377ASed in his corporalibus plus intendit aeternam salutem animarum.

VERS. 5.-- Triginta et octo. Id est duobus minus quadraginta, qui numerus constat ex quater decem, et significat perfectionem operum, in decem praeceptis legis et quatuor Evangeliis, a quo duo minus habet qui charitate Dei et proximi caret.

VERS. 6.-- Vis sanus fieri. Non nescit quid velit, sed accendit ad amorem sanitatis, de qua jam desperabat, unde jam conquerebatur, quod non haberet qui eum mitteret in aquam.

VERS. 8.-- Tolle grabatum tuum. Dilige proximum infirma ejus tolerando, qui te infirmum patienter tulit, et non solum aetate de tua erectione, sed etiam de proximorum sollicitus esto salute. Ecce angelus 377Bmagni consilii latens in carne, turbata aqua Judaeorum sanum fecit unum.

VERS. 9.-- Et ambulabat. Diligendo Deum festina usque ad visionem ejus, nec fratrem propter amorem ejus ad quem pergis deserens, nec propter fratrem ab illo quaerendo cessans.

VERS. 10.-- Dicebant ergo. Judaei observatores litterae nescientes gratiam qua lex impletur, quae ab omni servili opere, id est a peccato cessare jubet, unde hic dicitur: Tolle grabatum, etc. Non licet. Non calumniantur de salute qui in sabbato curant animalia, sed de lecti portatione.

VERS. 11.-- Respondit eis. Non cedit objicientibus, sed sanatoris mandatis obediens, auctoritatem sanationis suae objicit calumniantibus.

377CVERS. 13.-- Nesciebat quis esset. In turba positus non agnovit Jesum, sed post in templo. In quo docemur, ut quisquis vult ad visionem Dei pervenire, fugiat turbam suorum affectuum et nequitiam hominum, et adeat templum internae orationis.

VERS. 14.-- In templo. Non in turba, sed in templo, quia si gratiam conditoris cognoscere volumus, oportet ut conventicula fugiamus, et templum efficiamur. Noli peccare. Innuit quod propter peccata languebat, sed ipse Jesus illum sicut exterius, ita intus sanavit, unde praemonet ne iterum peccando gravius judicetur.

VERS. 16.-- Quia haec faciebat in sabbato. Quasi solutor legis agens contra Deum qui requievit in sabbato, sex diebus primo operatus. Contra quod 377Dait Jesus: Non sex diebus tantum ut putatis operatus est Deus, sed usque modo operatur, quia qui sex diebus condidit nova, a quibus requievit die septimo, postea operatur usque in finem gubernando, sustentando, renovando, propagando, ut permaneant condita. Et cum semper operatur Deus, constat sacramentum esse quod de requie sabbati legitur.

VERS. 17.-- Jesus autem respondit. Quasi dicat caeci in lege cur mihi invidetis, si hominem in sabbato sano, qui Deus cum Patre semper operor.

VERS. 18.-- Respondit itaque Jesus. Quia commoti sunt de audita aequalitate ejus quem putant hominem purum cum Deo Patre, subdit se esse Filium aequalem Patri, ejusdem voluntatis, ejusdem potestatis, 378Aejusdem operationis. In his autem disserendis hoc genere loquendi utitur, quo mentes parvulorum possit excitare, et si non implere, et quo mentes adultorum exerceantur et pascantur. Dicit enim de deitate Filii. Non potest a se facere, nisi quod videt Patrem facere, et demonstrat ei Pater, quae omnia non secundum litteram ut in hominibus fieret accipienda sunt, sed mystice. Ille autem haec secreta dicit, ut conturbentur, vel ut forte turbati medicum quaerant. Propter nos etiam ut nobis scriberentur, quae non bene intellecta in errorem, bene intellecta in veritatem ducerent.

VERS. 19.-- Amen, amen dico vobis. Agit hic de identitate sua cum Patre, et minoritate sua secundum hominem, et quid in his faciat, scilicet utramque 378Bresurrectionem, corporis scilicet et animae. Non potest filius. Ita sum aequalis Patri, ut non ipse a me, sed ego sim ab eo. Non facit a se, nec potest a se, qui non est a se, sed ab eo facit et potest a quo est. Haec et Filius. Videtur corpus omne facere quod animus, sed non similiter, cum animus imperet corpori; Pater vero et Filius eadem et similiter operantur, quia una natura, una substantia Patris et Filii, et una voluntas, et una potestas, et una operatio.

VERS. 20.-- Omnia demonstrat. Demonstrare Patrem Filio est cuncta operari per Filium, ut aeque habeantur in fide nostra, Pater et Filius. Et ne aliquo modo minus sentiatur de Filio, addit: Et majora his demonstrabit ei, hoc est, per eum majora faciet. 378CQuae? Sicut enim Pater suscitat mortuos et vivificat. Majus est suscitare mortuos quam languidum sanare, unde agebatur. Majora his. Suscitat Pater animas per Filium, ut animae vivant ex substantia Patris et Filii, et ei Filio homini facto in tempore demonstrabit majora, id est resurrectionem corporum, ut miremini eam fieri per Filium.

VERS. 21.-- Sicut enim Pater suscitat. Incipit ostendere quod Filius secundum quod aequalis est Patri, faciat, scilicet animas a morte resuscitat. Et est prima resurrectio a vitiis. In sequenti vero ostendit etiam resurrectionem mortuorum corporum secundum quod Filius hominis est et potestatem dedit ei, id est Filio.

Sic et Filius. (AUG.) Quia Pater et Filius sunt eadem 378Dvita. Judicat autem et suscitat corpora, etc., usque ad haec causa redditur, quia Filius hominis est.

VERS. 23.-- Ut omnes honorificent. Honorat Deum qui commendat Creatorem, omnipotentem, aeternum. Honorat Patrem, qui credit eum habere Filium aequalem sibi. Honorat Filium, qui credit eum esse aequalem Patri.

Qui non honorificat. (AUG.) Si enim minor crederetur Filius, Pater inhonoraretur, quod vel invidus nollet eum genuisse aequalem, vel infirmus non posset, et sic non esse Deus convinceretur, quia impotens.

VERS. 24.-- Quia qui, etc. Quia aequalis sum 379APatri et uno honore cum eo honorandus, ergo qui audit verbum meum, id est, qui credit me Filium, et credit eum Patrem, habet vitam aeternam, quia haec est vita aeterna, ut post dicet, ut cognoscant te quem misi Jesum Christum esse unum verum Deum. Audit et credit. Cur ait: Audit verbum meum et credit, non mihi, sed ei qui me misit: quia verbum ejus est in me, imo ego sum verbum ejus. Qui ergo ei credit, credit verbo ejus, id est mihi. Sed transiit. Hic a morte infidelitatis ad vitam fidei et justitiae, post a corruptione ad aeternitatem. De prima autem resurrectione apertius subdit: Amen, amen dico vobis, quia venit hora et nunc est, etc.

VERS. 25.-- Quia venit. Dixi quia Filius suscitat et vivificat, quod verum est, quia venit hora, etc., 379Bet ostensio Patris sui, hic enim de suscitatione animae a peccatis agitur. Vocem Filii. Hoc solum justis convenit, qui sic audiunt de incarnatione ejus, ut credant quia Filius Dei est.

Sicut enim Pater. (AUG.) Vita quae est ipse Pater et Filius pertinet ad animam, non ad corpus, etc., usque ad vita et ipsa est vita corporis.

In semetipso. Non aliunde, sed ipse est vita, sic et Filius in semetipso est vita. Sed differunt, quia Pater est vita non nascendo, Filius est vita ex Patre nascendo, unde ait: Dedit, non quod aliquando Filius fuerit sine vita, sed nascitur ex eo vita, dedit ergo, id est, vita genuit vitam.

VERS. 27.-- Et potestatem. Postquam dixit de resurrectione animae, subdit de resurrectione 379Ccarnis. Et potestatem quam habet Filius cum Patre in occulto, dedit Pater ei, quia Filius hominis est, in qua forma judicans bonis et malis videbitur. Visio enim formae Dei solis mundis corde promittitur, unde: Beati mundo corde, quoniam ipsi Deum videbunt (Matth. V). (AUG.) Christus in eo quod est Filius Dei, etc., usque ad per verbum factum in carne Filium hominis fit corporum resurrectio.

VERS. 28.-- Quia veniet hora. Sicut supra auctor de resurrectione mentis in vitam aeternam, ita etiam hic est auctor de resurrectione carnis, aliter nemo crederet.

VERS. 30.-- Non possum ego a meipso facere. Dedit mihi potestatem judicii, non tamen possum a me, id est, qui non est a se, non facit a se, et ita 379Dqui culpat eum de opere sabbati, culpat Patrem. Sicut autem. Audio per unitatem substantiae, id est, scio quia non est aliud audire, quam scire vel esse. Vel, ego Filius hominis nihil a me possum, sed sicut audio a Deo judico, et hoc judicium meum de quibuscunque est justum, ideo quia non quaero voluntatem meam, id est hominis, sed Patris, quae est de salute hominum, qui ad hoc me misit, id est, incarnari fecit. Non quaero. Loquitur nunc secundum quod aequalis est Patri, ut probetur in eo vera deitas, nunc secundum quod minor, ut probetur vera humanitas.

VERS. 31.-- Si ego testimonium.. Dico de identitate essentiae suae cum Patre, ex qua vivunt non 380Acorpora quae non sentiunt, sed animae rationales mediae inter Deum et corpora: dicto etiam de minoritate humanitatis, in qua scilicet moritur, resurgit, suscitat corpora et judicat: supponit de testimoniis suis quae non indiget habere ab homine, quia habet ab operibus quae Pater dedit et ab ipso Patre. Habet tamen et ab hominibus, sed ut a lucernis, id est a Joanne et a Moyse, qui si non possunt conjungere animas Deo, possunt tamen confutare inimicos.

VERS. 34.-- Sed haec. Sed tamen testimonium Joannis induco, ut per Joannem vos homines ad veritatem ipsam ducamini, et credentes salvi sitis.

VERS. 35.-- Ille erat, etc. Qui me exterius oculis hominum visibilem exhibuit, interius corda hominum 380Bad me cognoscendum accendit. Etiam omnes prophetae et apostoli sunt lucernae: Christus verum lumen, quod illuminat omnem hominem venientem in hunc mundum.

VERS. 37.-- Neque vocem. Et si in Sina vocem ejus audisse et speciem vidisse putetis.

VERS. 41.-- Claritatem. Nec ideo hoc loquor, ut quaeram gloriam meam, sed vobis errantibus condoleo, qui non habetis dilectionem Dei, et ad viam veritatis reducere volo.

VERS. 42.-- Quia dilectionem. Dilectio Dei non est in vobis, quia me non accipitis, id est non creditis, quia veni ut per me Pater glorificetur.

VERS. 43.-- Si alius. Antichristus, qui propriam gloriam quaerit non Dei, id est in nomine suo veniet, 380Choc est quod Apostolus ait, ut credant mendacio, qui non crediderunt veritati.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 4.-- And an angel. Behold two miracles: one invisibly through an angel in the pool, the other visibly by the Lord. In each one man was healed, that unity might be commended. Otherwise neither in power was it therefore great, and in kindness it was but little, that one out of so many should be healed by him who could raise them all up with one word. 377ABut in these bodily things he intends rather the eternal salvation of souls.

VERS. 5.-- Thirty and eight. That is, two less than forty, which number consists of four times ten, and signifies the perfection of works in the ten precepts of the law and the four Gospels; from which he has two less who lacks the love of God and of his neighbour.

VERS. 6.-- Wilt thou be made whole. He is not ignorant of what the man wills, but he kindles him to the love of health, of which he was already despairing, whence he was already complaining that he had no one to put him into the water.

VERS. 8.-- Take up thy bed. Love thy neighbour by bearing with his infirmities, thou whom he patiently bore when thou wast infirm; and be solicitous not only in age concerning thine own raising up, but also concerning the salvation of thy neighbours. Behold, the angel 377Bof great counsel, lying hid in the flesh, when the water of the Jews had been troubled, made one man whole.

VERS. 9.-- And he walked. By loving God, hasten unto the vision of him; neither forsaking thy brother for the love of him to whom thou goest, nor for thy brother's sake ceasing from the seeking of him.

VERS. 10.-- The Jews therefore said. The Jews, observers of the letter, not knowing the grace by which the law is fulfilled, which bids us cease from every servile work, that is, from sin; whence it is here said: Take up thy bed, etc. It is not lawful. They do not calumniate concerning health, they who on the sabbath tend their beasts, but concerning the carrying of the bed.

VERS. 11.-- He answered them. He does not yield to those who object, but, obeying the commands of him who healed him, he sets the authority of his healing against those who calumniate.

377CVERS. 13.-- He knew not who it was. Set in the crowd, he did not recognize Jesus, but afterward in the temple. Wherein we are taught that whosoever wishes to come to the vision of God should flee the crowd of his own affections and the wickedness of men, and approach the temple of inward prayer.

VERS. 14.-- In the temple. Not in the crowd, but in the temple; because if we wish to know the grace of the Creator, it behoves us to flee conventicles and to become a temple. Sin no more. He intimates that he had been languishing on account of his sins; but Jesus himself healed him within as he had healed him without, whence he forewarns him lest by sinning again he be more grievously judged.

VERS. 16.-- Because he did these things on the sabbath. As one who loosed the law and acted against God, who rested on the sabbath, having first wrought for six days. Against which 377DJesus says: God has not wrought for six days only, as you suppose, but he works until now; because he who in six days founded new things, from which he rested on the seventh day, afterward works unto the end by governing, sustaining, renewing and propagating, that the things founded may endure. And since God always works, it is plain that what is read of the rest of the sabbath is a sacrament.

VERS. 17.-- But Jesus answered. As if he should say: Blind in the law, why do you envy me if I make a man whole on the sabbath, I who as God always work with the Father.

VERS. 18.-- Jesus therefore answered. Because they were moved at hearing of the equality with God the Father of him whom they suppose to be a mere man, he adds that he is the Son equal to the Father, of the same will, of the same power, 378Aof the same operation. And in setting these things forth he uses that manner of speech by which he can rouse the minds of little ones, even if not fill them, and by which the minds of the grown may be exercised and fed. For he speaks of the deity of the Son. He cannot do of himself, save what he sees the Father do; and the Father shows him: all which things are to be received not according to the letter, as though they were done among men, but mystically. But he speaks these secret things that they may be troubled, or that, being troubled, they may perchance seek the physician; and for our sakes also, that they might be written for us, which, ill understood, would lead into error, and well understood, into truth.

VERS. 19.-- Amen, amen I say to you. Here he treats of his identity with the Father, and of his lesser state according to his manhood, and of what he does in these, namely both 378Bresurrections, that is, of the body and of the soul. The Son cannot. I am so equal to the Father that he is not from me, but I am from him. He does not act of himself, nor can he of himself, who is not of himself; but he acts and can act of him from whom he is. These also the Son. The whole body seems to do what the mind does, but not in like manner, since the mind commands the body; but the Father and the Son work the same things and in like manner, because there is one nature, one substance of the Father and of the Son, and one will, and one power, and one operation.

VERS. 20.-- He sheweth all things. For the Father to show to the Son is to work all things through the Son, that the Father and the Son may be equally held in our faith. And lest in any way less should be thought of the Son, he adds: And greater things than these will he shew him, that is, through him he will do greater things. 378CWhat things? For as the Father raiseth up the dead and giveth life. It is a greater thing to raise the dead than to heal a sick man, of which it was being treated. Greater than these. The Father raises up souls through the Son, that souls may live of the substance of the Father and of the Son; and to that Son, made man in time, he will show greater things, that is, the resurrection of bodies, that you may wonder that it is done through the Son.

VERS. 21.-- For as the Father raiseth up. He begins to show what the Son does according as he is equal to the Father, namely that he raises up souls from death. And this is the first resurrection, from vices. But in what follows he shows also the resurrection of dead bodies, according as he is the Son of man, and power was given to him, that is, to the Son.

So the Son also. (AUG.) Because the Father and the Son are the same 378Dlife. And he judges and raises up bodies, etc., as far as this cause is rendered, because he is the Son of man.

VERS. 23.-- That all may honour. He honours God who commends the Creator, the omnipotent, the eternal. He honours the Father who believes that he has a Son equal to himself. He honours the Son who believes him to be equal to the Father.

He that honoureth not. (AUG.) For if the Son were believed to be less, the Father would be dishonoured: either that, being envious, he would not have willed to beget him equal, or that, being weak, he could not; and so he would be convicted of not being God, because powerless.

VERS. 24.-- That he who, etc. Because I am equal 379Ato the Father and to be honoured with one honour together with him, therefore he who hears my word, that is, who believes me to be the Son and believes him to be the Father, has life everlasting; because this is life everlasting, as he shall say afterward, that they may know thee, Jesus Christ whom I sent, to be one true God. He heareth and believeth. Why does he say: He heareth my word and believeth, not me, but him that sent me: because his word is in me; nay rather, I am his word. He therefore who believes him, believes his word, that is, me. But is passed. Here, from the death of unbelief to the life of faith and of justice; afterward, from corruption to eternity. But of the first resurrection he adds more openly: Amen, amen I say to you, that the hour cometh and now is, etc.

VERS. 25.-- That the hour cometh. I said that the Son raises up and gives life, which is true, because the hour cometh, etc., 379Band the showing forth of his Father; for here it is treated of the raising up of the soul from sins. The voice of the Son. This belongs to the just alone, who so hear of his incarnation as to believe that he is the Son of God.

For as the Father. (AUG.) The life which is the Father himself and the Son pertains to the soul, not to the body, etc., as far as it is life, and it is itself the life of the body.

In himself. Not from elsewhere, but he himself is life; so the Son also is life in himself. But they differ, because the Father is life by not being born, the Son is life by being born of the Father; whence he says, He gave: not that the Son was at any time without life, but that life is born of him; he gave therefore, that is, life begot life.

VERS. 27.-- And power. After he had spoken of the resurrection of the soul, he adds concerning the resurrection 379Cof the flesh. And the power which the Son has with the Father in secret, the Father gave to him, because he is the Son of man; in which form, judging, he shall be seen by the good and by the evil. For the vision of the form of God is promised to the clean of heart alone, whence: Blessed are the clean of heart, for they shall see God (Matth. V). (AUG.) Christ, in that he is the Son of God, etc., as far as through the Word made flesh, the Son of man, the resurrection of bodies comes to pass.

VERS. 28.-- That the hour shall come. As above he is the author of the resurrection of the mind unto life eternal, so also here he is the author of the resurrection of the flesh; otherwise no one would believe it.

VERS. 30.-- I cannot of myself do anything. He gave me the power of judgment, yet I cannot of myself, that is, he who is not of himself does not act of himself; and so 379Dhe who blames him for the work of the sabbath blames the Father. But as. I hear through the unity of substance, that is, I know, because to hear is nothing other than to know or to be. Or: I, the Son of man, can do nothing of myself, but as I hear from God I judge, and this judgment of mine, of whatsoever things it be, is just, for this reason, because I seek not my own will, that is, a man's, but the Father's, which is concerning the salvation of men, who sent me for this, that is, caused me to be made flesh. I seek not. He speaks now according as he is equal to the Father, that the true deity may be proved in him; now according as he is less, that the true humanity may be proved.

VERS. 31.-- If I bear witness.. I speak of the identity of his essence with the Father, from which live not 380Athe bodies, which do not feel, but the rational souls, midway between God and bodies: having spoken also of the lesser state of the humanity, in which, namely, he dies, rises again, raises up bodies and judges, he subjoins concerning his testimonies, which he has no need to have from man, because he has them from the works which the Father gave, and from the Father himself. Yet he has them also from men, but as from lamps, that is, from John and from Moses, who, if they cannot join souls to God, can nevertheless confute his enemies.

VERS. 34.-- But these things. Yet I bring forward the testimony of John, that through John you men may be led to the truth itself, and believing may be saved.

VERS. 35.-- He was, etc. He who set me forth outwardly visible to the eyes of men, inwardly kindled the hearts of men 380Bto the knowing of me. All the prophets and apostles also are lamps: Christ is the true light, which enlighteneth every man that cometh into this world.

VERS. 37.-- Nor the voice. Even if you suppose that on Sinai you heard his voice and saw his form.

VERS. 41.-- Glory. Nor do I speak this that I may seek my own glory, but I grieve with you who go astray, who have not the love of God, and I wish to lead you back to the way of truth.

VERS. 42.-- That the love. The love of God is not in you, because you do not receive me, that is, you do not believe, because I came that through me the Father may be glorified.

VERS. 43.-- If another. Antichrist, who seeks his own glory and not God's, that is, he shall come in his own name, 380Cthis is what the Apostle says, that they should believe a lie, who have not believed the truth.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 1.-- Trans mare. Tiberiadis et Capharnaum in eodem littore ex parte nostra, ultra in alio littore opposito, locus est ubi miraculum istud factum est. Nec proprie vocatur mare, sed quaedam magna Jordanis refluxio.

VERS. 2.-- Et sequebatur. Viso hoc miraculo, gentium magna turba sequitur, quia sicut eos quos a corporis infirmitate sanabat, intus spiritus reintegrabat, sic ipsos a morbo incredulitatis sanabat.

VERS. 5.-- Cum sublevasset. Exemplo suo nos admonet levare oculos cordis nostri, ut si forte eo 380Dducente ad altitudinem actionis et scientiae ascendere permittamur, turba sequentium carnalium cogitationum nos non perturbet, nec ab altitudine contemplationis dejiciat, sed eam spiritualibus escis, quantum capere possit, pascere curemus.

VERS. 7.-- Ducentorum. Centenarius duplicatus significat perfectionem bonae actionis et rationalis scientiae, quae erudiendis in fide non sufficiunt, nisi eis altitudo theologiae addatur, quae animas per bonam operationem purgatas, et per scientiam rerum creatarum illuminatas, ad consummatam spiritualium refectionum plenitudinem perducit.

VERS. 9.-- Est puer unus. Hic puer est Moyses legislator, cujus lex neminem ad perfectum perduxit. 381AUnus, quia vanitatem Ecclesiae futuram praefigurabat. Hic, id est, in Veteri Testamento quod quamvis Novo clarescente jam vilescit, adhuc tamen omnino non recessit. Sed haec, etc. Quasi dicat: Legis littera, vel corporei sensus quid prosunt ad multitudinem in te creditorum, qui spiritualia alimenta sunt petituri, quae omnem litteram omnemque corporeum sensum superant?

VERS. 10.-- Facite homines. Magister veritatis prius in simplicitate litterae quasi in infimis locis, et in simplicitate visibilis creaturae auditores erudivit, ut sic in altitudinem contemplationis erigantur.

VERS. 14.-- Illi ergo homines cum vidissent quod, etc. Illi nondum plena fide proficiebant, quare hunc Deum dicere nesciebant. Nos certiores illis dicimus: 381BHic est Verbum, sine quo non est propheta vel angelus.

VERS. 15.-- Jesus ergo. Qui volunt rapere et regem facere significant discipulos et alios credentes, qui volunt praevenire tempus regni ejus, putantes eum sic venisse, ut jam regnaret. Sed hoc tempore colligitur regnum, quod in fine erit manifestum, cum dicet: Percipite regnum quod vobis paratum est. Fugit ergo non timens teneri, qui postea eisdem colloquitur, sed signare volens quod altitudo ejus non potest intelligi. Fugit in montem, id est ascendit coelos, solus sacerdos interpellans pro populo foris constituto, qui interim jactatur fluctibus, unde subdit: Ut autem sero factum est. Sero mundi est post ascensionem Christi in quo discipuli descendunt ad labores 381Cmundi, et regunt navem, id est Ecclesiam.

VERS. 19.-- Timuerunt. Quod discipuli qui omnium in Ecclesia futurorum figuram gerebant, viso Domino super fluctum maris ad se venientem timuerunt, et ut alius evangelista apertius dicit, phantasma crediderunt eum, mysterio non vacat, quia adhuc hodie multi in Ecclesia, cum audiunt sibi persuaderi ut praesentia bona et caduca pro aeternis quaecunque habendis dimittant, timent et se deceptos credunt, quibus Dominus ait: Nolite timere, ego sum.

VERS. 22.-- Altera die. Insinuatum est turbae hoc miraculum, quod super aquas Jesus ambulasset, quia non fuerit ibi nisi navis una, et in eam non intravit Jesus cum discipulis, unde ergo factus esset transitus, nisi super aquas ambulasset?
