381DVERS. 25.-- Et cum invenissent. Ecce praesens est eis quos fugerat, ne eum regem facerent. Cur ergo fugerat, nisi pro praedicto sacramento? Iterum praesens loquitur eis, ut quorum satiavit ventres, eorum satiet et mentes, dicens: Amen, amen dico vobis, docens eos quos doctores instituit, fugere laudem et terrenum imperium, sed tamen insistere praedicationi.

VERS. 26.-- Amen, amen dico vobis. Postquam de divinitate sua qua idem est cum Patre tractavit, et eodem honore honorandus, et de humanitate sua qua de morte liberat et suscitat grandi honore dignus, vult tractare de cibo vitae, id est verbo Dei. Et haec continuatio quantum ad sententiam. Ad litteram 382Avero sic: Vos quaeritis me, non propter me, sed propter panes qui pereunt. Ergo operamini, id est, quaerite me propter me, non propter aliud. Ipse est enim cibus ille qui permanet in vitam aeternam, ut post aperte dicet. Similiter quotidie quaerunt multi Jesum, non propter Jesum, sed ut bene sit eis in tempore.

VERS. 27.-- Signavit Deus. Quasi proprio signo a caeteris discrevit, quia qui est Filius hominis, est et Filius Dei et ideo me nolite contemnere Filium hominis, sed petite a me cibum spiritualem.

VERS. 29.-- Hoc est opus Dei. Fides per dilectionem operans, est opus, quia si desit facultas, non quaeritur nisi voluntas. Merito etiam fides quae per dilectionem operatur, opus dicitur, quae est initium et finis totius boni.

382BVERS. 32.-- Dixit ergo. Quod praeferebant opus Moysi suo operi, ostendit illud esse minus, quia non fuit panis de coelo, sed tantum figura illius et suum majus, qui est panis de coelo qui dat vitam, et hic est cibus quem supra dixit permanere in vitam aeternam.

VERS. 33.-- Panis enim verus. Angeli purum verbum solidum cibum comedunt: nos vero verbum, sed in lac versum, quia si non possumus comedere, possumus sugere. Nisi enim incarnaretur, a nobis non cognosceretur, a nobis non gustaretur.

VERS. 34.-- Domine, semper, etc. Sicut mulier Samaritana audiens: Qui biberit de hac aqua, non sitiet in aeternum, secundum corpus accepit, et tamen labore et indigentia carere volens, dixit: Da mihi 382Chanc aquam, sic isti quaerunt panem qui eos reficiat, et nunquam deficiat, propter quod eum regem facere voluerunt. Sed Jesus ad se revocat, et quem panem dixit evidentius aperit, Ego sum panis vitae. Potero dare vitam, quia sum panis vivificans, igne Spiritus sancti in clibano uteri virginalis coctus et formatus, et mundo ad pellendum famem ignorantiae Dei donatus.

VERS. 36.-- Sed dixi vobis. Quasi dicat: Non dixi de hoc pane, qui sciam vos esse satiandos, sed ad improperium vestrae infidelitatis, quia videtis et non creditis.

VERS. 37.-- Omne quod dat mihi Pater, etc. Id est, quaecunque Pater trahit ad me, Pater dat Filio. Pater trahit ad Filium illos qui ideo credunt in Filium, 382Dquia credunt eum Patrem habere Deum.

Veniet. Mihi unitur humilis humili, et ideo non foras ejicitur, quod est tantum superbi, qui nihil habet in bono interiori, neque hic neque in futuro.

VERS. 39.-- Haec est enim voluntas ejus qui misit me Patris, etc. Non est voluntas Dei ut pereat unus de pusillis, de tumentibus perit, de pusillis nihil.

Non perdam, etc. Id est, quia videt me et credit in me, qui sum vita sicut et Pater, et ideo modo habet vitam quae non est ei sola, sed et resuscitabo eum.

VERS. 40.-- Ut omnis, etc. Supra dicebat: Qui audit verbum meum et credit ei qui me misit; hic dicitur: 383AQui videt Filium et credit in eum. Non dicit: Qui credit in Patrem, quia hoc est credere in Filium quod et in Patrem, quia sicut Pater habet vitam in semetipso, sic et Filius habet vitam in semetipso.

VERS. 41.-- Murmurabant. Nescientes hunc panem, nec Patrem ejus coelestem, quia inflati de sua justitia in qua de viribus suis praesumunt, justitiam Dei quae de coelo venit, non esuriunt.

VERS. 43.-- Nolite murmurare. Quasi non sim panis qui de coelo descendi, quia vere sum, sed nemo potest venire ad me nisi a Patre tractus, et ideo vos non creditis me esse Patrem, quia non estis tracti a Patre.

VERS. 44.-- Nisi Pater. (AUG.) Illum Pater trahit ad Filium, qui ideo credit in Filium, quia cogitat, etc., 383Busque ad in quo commendatur magna gratia.

Et ego resuscitabo, etc. Ego aequalis Patri faciam eum videre quod credit, manducare quod esurit, habere quod amavit et desideravit, quod non potest esse in praesenti, ubi tantum trahitur.

VERS. 45.-- Est scriptum. Vere nemo venit ad me nisi Pater docuerit, quia ita testantur prophetiae. Omnes homines regni illius, id est Christianae fidei, erunt docibiles Dei, quia etsi homines foris loquuntur, tamen Deus solus intus sensum aperit, intus instruit veritatis cognitione et amore, ita quicunque audit et discit a Patre, venit ad me. In prophetis. Prophetis, dicit pluraliter, quia omnes prophetae uno et eodem spiritu repleti, licet diversa prophetarant, tamen ad idem tendebant.

383CVERS. 46.-- Non quia Patrem, etc. Id est ego novi Patrem, quia ab illo sum, et ideo credite, quia qui credit in me habet vitam aeternam. Potuit dicere, habet me, sed revelat quod ipse sit vita aeterna, quia verbum est vita hominum quod et carni suae quam susceperat, cui intervenit mors ut mortem tolleret, dat vitam in resurrectione, et aliis credentibus in se.

VERS. 48, 49.-- Ego sum panis vitae. Patres vestri, etc. Manna de quo superbitis, patribus murmuratoribus quorum estis filii, murmurando non fuit panis vitae, quia non intellexerunt in eo spiritualem cibum, sic et vos qui me auditis, non spiritualiter intelligitis. Vixit autem Moyses et alii electi de manna, quia spiritualiter manducaverunt. Sic et qui modo verba 383Dmea spiritualiter accipiunt. Ego sum panis vitae. Ecce sacramentum verbis tegit, ut Judaei et etiam aliqui discipuli non credentes verbis illius magis scandalizentur, et humiles apostoli humiliter credentes probentur; unde Petrus: Verba vitae aeternae habes.

VERS. 52.-- Et panis, etc. Quomodo sit panis determinat non solum secundum verbum quo vivunt omnia, sed etiam secundum carnem assumptam pro mundi vita. Humana enim caro quae erat peccato obnoxia, et ideo mortua, carni ejus mundae unita, incorporata, unum cum illo effecta, vivit de spiritu ejus, sicut vivit corpus de suo spiritu: qui vero non est de corpore Christi, non vivit de spiritu Christi. 384AQuia autem Judaei non erant hujus unitatis, litigabant, nec talibus exponitur quomodo Dominus carnem suam det ad manducandum, sed audivit, unde magis moveantur.

VERS. 54.-- Nisi manducaveritis carnem, etc. (AUG.) Hunc cibum et potum societatem vult intelligere, etc., usque ad ut ex multis granis unus panis sit, et ex multis acinis vinum confluit.

VERS. 55.-- Qui manducat. Exponunt quidam sancti de comestione altaris, et bene dicunt, sed quaerunt quomodo fiat in vitam aeternam, cum quidam ad mortem, ut dicit Apostolus. Si quis comedit in altari cum hac comestione de qua agit hic, scilicet cum fide operante et dilectione, hic comedit in vitam aeternam. Si quis vero aliter, in mortem.

384BVERS. 56.-- Caro enim. Vere vivet anima et corpus ex hoc cibo, quia caro mea vere est cibus. Qui solus facit immortales, ut neque esurient neque sitient, alius cibus hoc non praestat, etiam si in eo hoc appetatur.

VERS. 57.-- Qui manducat. Jam exponit quomodo fiat quod dixit, et quid sit manducare carnem, et bibere sanguinem, hoc est manere in Christo, et Christum habere in se manentem. Qui vero non manet in Christo, et in quo non manet Christus, non manducat spiritualiter, et sic sacramentum sibi sumit in judicium corporaliter.

VERS. 58.-- Sicut misit me. Me aequalem sibi misit, id est, hominem fecit, et inde est ut ad eum referam vitam meam quasi ad majorem. Et ipse vivet. 384CUt vivam ex Patre, mea exinanitio facit: ut homo vivat ex me, participatio facit quae me manducat.

VERS. 59.-- Hic est panis. Et ita constat quod hic est vere panis de coelo, non manna, quia illud manducantes patres vobis similes mortui sunt in aeternum: hunc panem homo qui vitam non habebat manducans, vivet in aeternum. Supra dixerat, Ego sum panis qui de coelo descendi. Hic dicit: Hic, id est Filius hominis, ad esum cujus carnis invito, est ille panis qui de coelo descendit: ergo idem est Filius hominis et Filius Dei.

VERS. 61.-- Multi ergo. Multi carnaliter sapientes, quasi particulatim carnem qua indutus erat se eis distributurum promitteret, contra naturam esse putant, ut cibum hominum humanam carnem daret.

384DDurus est, etc. (AUG.) Sic oportebat dici ut ab hominibus non intelligeretur, sed secretum Dei verbis tegeretur: secretum Dei intentos debet facere, non adversos.

VERS. 63.-- Si ergo, etc. Suspenditur oratio, Quasi dicat: Tunc intelligetis quod non eodem modo quo putatis erogo corpus, et quod haec gratia morsibus non consumitur, sed spirituali gratia me illis dando, ipsos in corpus meum converto; hinc plane solvit quod ipsos moverat; putabant enim quod corpus distribueret, ille dicit in coelum se ascensurum utique integrum. Quid ergo tenendum sit, aperte subdit:

VERS. 64.-- Spiritus est qui vivificat. Caro, sicut 385Aintelligitis; sicut quae in macello emitur, sicut alius cibus non prodest, ipsa tamen vas spiritus, qui in ea salutem operatur multum prodest, sicut sola scientia inflat, ipsa tamen non est mala, sed per charitatem aedificat.

Verba quae ego, etc. Quibus dixi manducandum me esse, manere in me et me in ipso. Ut autem aliquis maneat in Christo ut membrum, et Christus in ipso ut in templo, unitas facit. Unitas autem ex charitate est, charitas autem ex spiritu, ergo spiritus qui vivificat compaginata membra non separat.

VERS. 65.-- Sed sunt quidam, etc. Propheta. Nisi credideritis, non intelligetis: per fidem copulamur, per intellectum vivificamur.

Et quis traditurus. Quia Judas inter scandalizatos 385Brecedit, sed manet ad insidiandum, quem nec silet, nec aperte ostendit, ut omnes timeant dum unus perit.

VERS. 66.-- Quia nemo potest, etc. Causam quare quidam non credant subdit: Quia non est a Deo datum; fides enim non est hominibus a se, quasi propria, sed a Deo quasi gratia.

VERS. 67.-- Ex hoc, etc. Nota esse caute loquendum, sed si tamen ex verbis tuis aliquis scandalizabitur, habet consolationem in eo, ex cujus dicto multi scandalizati sunt, qui hoc fecit ut tibi esset exemplum.

VERS. 71.-- Nonne ego vos, etc. Duodecim numerus sacratus est qui permanet, etsi Judas inde perit. Elegit undecim ad permanendum, elegit Judam 385Cut per eum divinae dispensatio misericordiae in salutem mundi impleretur, utens malo ad bonum, ut mali utuntur bonis Dei ad malum.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- Beyond the sea. Tiberias and Capharnaum are upon the same shore, on our side; beyond, upon the other shore opposite, is the place where that miracle was done. Nor is it properly called a sea, but a certain great overflowing of the Jordan.

VERS. 2.-- And there followed. This miracle having been seen, a great crowd of the nations follows, because, as the spirit within restored those whom he was healing from infirmity of the body, so he was healing these same men from the disease of unbelief.

VERS. 5.-- When he had lifted up. By his own example he admonishes us to lift up the eyes of our heart, that if perchance, under his 380Dleading, we are permitted to ascend to the height of action and of knowledge, the crowd of carnal thoughts that follow may not disturb us, nor cast us down from the height of contemplation, but that we may take care to feed it with spiritual food, as much as it can take.

VERS. 7.-- Of two hundred. The number of a hundred doubled signifies the perfection of good action and of rational knowledge, which do not suffice for those who are to be instructed in the faith, unless the height of theology be added to them, which leads souls purged by good working and enlightened by the knowledge of created things to the consummated fullness of spiritual refreshments.

VERS. 9.-- There is one boy. This boy is Moses the lawgiver, whose law brought no one to perfection. 381AOne, because he prefigured the future vanity [vanitatem; read unitatem, "the unity"] of the Church. Here, that is, in the Old Testament, which, although it now grows cheap as the New shines out, has nevertheless not altogether withdrawn. But these, etc. As if he should say: The letter of the law, or the bodily senses, what do they profit toward the multitude of those who believe in thee, who are about to ask for spiritual nourishments, which surpass every letter and every bodily sense?

VERS. 10.-- Make the men. The Master of truth first instructed his hearers in the simplicity of the letter, as in the lowest places, and in the simplicity of the visible creature, that so they might be raised up to the height of contemplation.

VERS. 14.-- Those men therefore, when they had seen that, etc. They were not yet advancing in full faith, wherefore they knew not how to call him God. We, more certain than they, say: 381BThis is the Word, without whom there is no prophet or angel.

VERS. 15.-- Jesus therefore. They who wish to take him by force and to make him king signify the disciples and the other believers, who wish to anticipate the time of his kingdom, thinking that he had come in such wise that he should already reign. But in this time the kingdom is being gathered, which at the end will be made manifest, when he shall say: Receive the kingdom which is prepared for you. [Vulg. Matt 25:34 possidete paratum vobis regnum a constitutione mundi, "possess the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world"] He flees therefore, not fearing to be held, he who afterwards converses with those same men, but wishing to signify that his height cannot be understood. He flees into the mountain, that is, he ascends the heavens, alone a priest interceding for the people set outside, who meanwhile is tossed by the waves; whence he adds: But when evening was come. The evening of the world is after the ascension of Christ, in which the disciples go down to the labours 381Cof the world, and steer the ship, that is, the Church.

VERS. 19.-- They were afraid. That the disciples, who bore the figure of all who were to be in the Church, when they had seen the Lord coming to them upon the wave of the sea, were afraid, and, as another evangelist says more openly, believed him a phantasm, is not void of mystery, because even to this day many in the Church, when they hear themselves urged to let go present and perishable goods for the sake of eternal ones, whatsoever they are to have, are afraid and believe themselves deceived; to whom the Lord says: Fear not, it is I.

VERS. 22.-- The next day. This miracle was made known to the crowd, that Jesus had walked upon the waters, because there had not been there any ship but one, and Jesus did not enter into it with the disciples; whence then had the crossing been made, unless he had walked upon the waters?

381DVERS. 25.-- And when they had found him. Behold, he is present to those from whom he had fled, lest they should make him king. Why then had he fled, if not for the aforesaid sacrament? Again, being present, he speaks to them, that of those whose bellies he filled, he may fill the minds also, saying: Amen, amen I say to you, teaching those whom he appointed teachers to flee praise and earthly rule, but nevertheless to press on in preaching.

VERS. 26.-- Amen, amen I say to you. After he had treated of his divinity, by which he is the same with the Father and to be honoured with the same honour, and of his humanity, by which he frees from death and raises up, being worthy of great honour, he wishes to treat of the food of life, that is, of the Word of God. And this is the connection as regards the sense. But according to the letter, 382Athus: You seek me, not for my own sake, but for the sake of the loaves that perish. Therefore work, that is, seek me for my own sake, not for the sake of anything else. For he himself is that food which endures unto life everlasting, as he will afterwards say openly. In like manner many seek Jesus daily, not for Jesus' sake, but that it may be well with them in this time.

VERS. 27.-- God hath sealed. As by a proper sign he marked him off from the rest, because he who is the Son of man is also the Son of God; and therefore do not despise me, the Son of man, but ask of me spiritual food.

VERS. 29.-- This is the work of God. Faith working by love is the work, because if the means be wanting, nothing is required but the will. Deservedly also is that faith called a work which works by love, which is the beginning and the end of all good.

382BVERS. 32.-- He said therefore. Because they preferred the work of Moses to his work, he shows that that was the less, since it was not bread from heaven but only a figure of it, and that his own is the greater, which is the bread from heaven that gives life; and this is the food which he said above endures unto life everlasting.

VERS. 33.-- For the true bread. [Vulg. Jo 6:33 Panis enim Dei est, "For the bread of God is"] The angels eat the pure Word, solid food; but we the Word, yet turned into milk, because if we cannot eat, we can suck. For unless he were made flesh, he would not be known by us, he would not be tasted by us.

VERS. 34.-- Lord, always, etc. As the Samaritan woman, hearing: He that shall drink of this water shall not thirst for ever, [Vulg. Jo 4:13–14 qui autem biberit ex aqua quam ego dabo ei, non sitiet in aeternum, "he that shall drink of the water that I will give him"] took it according to the body, and yet, wishing to be without labour and want, said: Give me 382Cthis water, so these seek a bread which may refresh them and never fail, on account of which they wished to make him king. But Jesus calls them back to himself, and more evidently opens what bread he spoke of: I am the bread of life. I shall be able to give life, because I am the bread that gives life, baked and formed by the fire of the Holy Spirit in the oven of the virginal womb, and given to the world to drive away the hunger of the ignorance of God.

VERS. 36.-- But I said to you. As if he should say: I did not say of this bread that I know you are to be filled, but for the reproach of your unbelief, because you see and do not believe.

VERS. 37.-- All that the Father giveth me, etc. That is, whomsoever the Father draws to me, the Father gives to the Son. The Father draws to the Son those who believe in the Son for this reason, 382Dthat they believe him to have God for his Father.

He shall come. The humble man is united to me, myself humble, and therefore he is not cast out, which belongs only to the proud man, who has nothing in the inward good, neither here nor in time to come.

VERS. 39.-- For this is the will of him that sent me, the Father, etc. It is not the will of God that one of the little ones should perish; of the swollen there perishes one, of the little ones nothing.

I will not lose, etc. That is, because he sees me and believes in me, who am life as also the Father is; and therefore he now has a life which is not his alone, but I will also raise him up.

VERS. 40.-- That every one, etc. Above he was saying: He that heareth my word and believeth him that sent me; here it is said: 383AHe that seeth the Son and believeth in him. He does not say: He that believeth in the Father, because to believe in the Son is the same thing as to believe in the Father, because as the Father has life in himself, so also the Son has life in himself.

VERS. 41.-- They murmured. Not knowing this bread, nor his heavenly Father, because, puffed up with their own justice, in which they presume upon their own powers, they do not hunger for the justice of God which comes from heaven.

VERS. 43.-- Murmur not. As though I were not the bread which came down from heaven, whereas truly I am; but no one can come to me unless he be drawn by the Father, and therefore you do not believe me to be the Father [me esse Patrem; read me esse a Patre, "that I am from the Father"], because you are not drawn by the Father.

VERS. 44.-- Unless the Father. (AUG.) The Father draws to the Son him who for this reason believes in the Son, that he thinks, etc., 383Bas far as in which a great grace is commended.

And I will raise him up, etc. I, equal to the Father, will make him see what he believes, eat what he hungers for, have what he has loved and desired, which cannot be in the present, where he is only drawn.

VERS. 45.-- It is written. Truly no one comes to me unless the Father shall have taught him, because the prophecies so testify. All the men of that kingdom, that is, of the Christian faith, shall be taught of God, because although men speak outwardly, yet God alone opens the understanding within, within instructs with the knowledge and love of truth; so that whosoever hears and learns from the Father comes to me. In the prophets. Prophets, he says, in the plural, because all the prophets, filled with one and the same spirit, although they had prophesied diverse things, yet tended to the same end.

383CVERS. 46.-- Not that the Father, etc. That is, I know the Father, because I am from him; and therefore believe, because he who believes in me has life everlasting. He could have said, has me; but he reveals that he himself is life everlasting, because the Word is the life of men, which also gives life in the resurrection to his own flesh, which he had taken up, and upon which death came in that it might take away death, and to others believing in him.

VERS. 48, 49.-- I am the bread of life. Your fathers, etc. The manna of which you are proud was, by their murmuring, not the bread of life to the murmuring fathers whose sons you are, because they did not understand in it the spiritual food; so also you who hear me do not understand spiritually. But Moses and the other elect lived of the manna, because they ate spiritually. So also do they who now receive my words 383Dspiritually. I am the bread of life. Behold, he covers the sacrament with words, that the Jews, and also some disciples not believing his words, may be the more scandalized, and the humble apostles, believing humbly, may be proved; whence Peter: Thou hast the words of eternal life.

VERS. 52.-- And the bread, etc. He determines how he is bread, not only according to the Word by which all things live, but also according to the flesh taken up for the life of the world. For human flesh, which was liable to sin and therefore dead, being united to his clean flesh, incorporated, made one with him, lives by his spirit, as the body lives by its own spirit: but he who is not of the body of Christ does not live by the spirit of Christ. 384ABut because the Jews were not of this unity, they strove; nor is it expounded to such men how the Lord gives his flesh to be eaten, but he heard, whence they are the more moved.

VERS. 54.-- Unless you eat the flesh, etc. (AUG.) He wishes this food and drink to be understood as fellowship, etc., as far as that from many grains there may be one bread, and from many berries the wine flows together.

VERS. 55.-- He that eateth. Certain holy men expound this of the eating of the altar, and they say well; but they ask how it may be unto life everlasting, since for some it is unto death, as the Apostle says. If any man eats at the altar with this eating of which he here treats, namely with faith working and with love, he eats unto life everlasting. But if any man eats otherwise, unto death.

384BVERS. 56.-- For the flesh. Truly the soul and the body shall live from this food, because my flesh is meat indeed. Which alone makes men immortal, so that they shall neither hunger nor thirst; another food does not afford this, even if this be sought in it.

VERS. 57.-- He that eateth. Now he expounds how that comes to pass which he said, and what it is to eat the flesh and to drink the blood: that is, to abide in Christ and to have Christ abiding in oneself. But he who does not abide in Christ, and in whom Christ does not abide, does not eat spiritually, and so takes the sacrament to himself bodily unto judgment.

VERS. 58.-- As the Father hath sent me. He sent me, equal to himself; that is, he made me man, and thence it is that I refer my life to him as to one greater. And he shall live. 384CThat I live by the Father, my own emptying-out effects; that a man live by me, the participation effects which eats me.

VERS. 59.-- This is the bread. And so it is established that this is truly the bread from heaven, not the manna, because the fathers like to you who ate it are dead for ever: whereas the man who had not life, eating this bread, shall live for ever. Above he had said, I am the bread that came down from heaven. Here he says: This man, that is, the Son of man, to the eating of whose flesh I invite you, is that bread which came down from heaven: therefore the Son of man and the Son of God are the same.

VERS. 61.-- Many therefore. Many, being wise after the flesh, as though he were promising that he would distribute to them, piece by piece, the flesh wherewith he was clothed, think it to be against nature that he should give human flesh as food for men.

384DThis is a hard saying, etc. (AUG.) So it behoved to be said, that it might not be understood by men, but that the secret of God might be covered with words: the secret of God ought to make men intent, not adverse.

VERS. 63.-- If then, etc. The speech is left suspended. As if he should say: Then shall you understand that I do not give out my body in the manner you suppose, and that this grace is not consumed by bites, but that, by giving myself to them with a spiritual grace, I turn them into my body; hereby he plainly resolves what had moved them; for they thought that he would distribute his body, whereas he says that he is to ascend into heaven whole and entire. What therefore is to be held, he openly adds:

VERS. 64.-- It is the spirit that quickeneth. The flesh, as 385Ayou understand it, as that which is bought in the shambles, as any other food, profits nothing; yet that same flesh, the vessel of the spirit which works salvation in it, profits much: as knowledge alone puffs up, yet is not itself evil, but by charity builds up.

The words that I, etc. By which I said that I am to be eaten, that one abides in me and I in him. But that anyone should abide in Christ as a member, and Christ in him as in a temple, unity effects. And unity is from charity, and charity from the spirit; therefore the spirit which quickens does not separate the members joined together.

VERS. 65.-- But there are some, etc. The prophet: Unless you believe, you shall not understand. By faith we are coupled, by understanding we are quickened.

And who should betray. Because Judas withdraws among those who were scandalized, 385Bbut remains to lay snares; whom he neither passes over in silence nor openly points out, that all may fear while one perishes.

VERS. 66.-- Because no man can, etc. He adds the cause why certain men do not believe: Because it is not given by God; for faith is not to men from themselves, as if their own, but from God, as a grace.

VERS. 67.-- From this, etc. Note that one must speak warily; but if nevertheless someone shall be scandalized at thy words, he has consolation in him at whose saying many were scandalized, who did this that it might be an example to thee.

VERS. 71.-- Have not I, etc. The number twelve is sacred, and it abides, even though Judas perishes from it. He chose eleven to abide; he chose Judas 385Cthat through him the dispensation of the divine mercy might be fulfilled unto the salvation of the world, using evil for good, as evil men use the good things of God for evil.

CAPUT VII.

VERS. 1.-- Post haec autem. Dominus in carne manens frequenter in Galilaeam ambulabat, ut eum imitari volentes, de vitiis ad virtutes transmigrare discant: quod illa figurabat. Vel ut illius temporis sancti de Judaea ad gentes verbi Dei transitum exspectarent.

VERS. 2.-- Dies festus. Non quod unus, sunt enim septem, sed more eorum quotquot sunt, quasi unus dicuntur propter unam festivitatem scenopegia festivitas tabernaculorum, quia mense septimo 385DJudaei habitabant in tabernaculis: in memoriam illorum in quibus patres eorum habitaverunt in deserto.

VERS. 3.-- Ut et discipuli. Opera quae veri discipuli sciebant, laudibus divinis et non populari favore efferenda.

VERS. 4.-- Nemo. Gloriae ejus consulunt. Quasi: Mira facis, scilicet in abscondito, sed innotescere, ut ab omnibus lauderis.

VERS. 6.-- Tempus. Ideo non est nunc tempus meum, sed vestrum, quia non sum amator mundi ut vos, sed accusator, et ideo non audit vos mundus suos falsos testes, qui dicitis bona mala et mala bona.

386AVERS. 8.-- Ego enim non ascendo. (AUG.) Quidam dicunt hoc ad passionem pertinere, quia non in festivitate scenopegiae, sed in paschali qua agnus occidi solet, crucem ascendit.

VERS. 10.-- Non manifeste, etc. Typice: Christus occultus ascendit in festivitatem, quia in omnibus illis figuralibus latuit qui modo est manifestus nobis. Et quod latuit, exemplum est vitandi malos: quod palam loquitur et non tenetur ab his qui quaerebant, potestatis est.

VERS. 16.-- Respondit eis: Mea doctrina, hominis, non est mea, sed Dei. Vel quia non est verbum nullius, sed alicujus doctrina ejus, id est ipse verbum, doctrina ejus non est sua, sed ejus a quo est: quod est - Ego non sum a meipso. Et quia hoc profundum 386Bvidit non omnes intellecturos, dedit consilium: Si vis intelligere, crede. Hoc est: Si quis credit me Filium Dei, cognoscet de doctrina, id est, intelliget quod doctrina mea non est mea.

VERS. 18.-- Qui a semetipso. Antichristus non est verax, et injustitia in illo est, quia quaerit gloriam suam, non Dei, a quo non est missus, sed venire permissus. Qui autem, etc. Id est, ego qui licet aequalis Patri, tamen in servi forma quaero gloriam Patris, non meam, in hoc exemplum humilitatis tribuit, ut omnia bona Deo tribuamus.

VERS. 19.-- Nonne Moyses, etc. Ego ex Deo loquor et gloriam ejus quaero, verax, justus: sed vos qui gloriamini in lege, contra legem quae vetat occidere, quaeritis me interficere.

386CVERS. 20.-- Daemonium. Quaerunt occidere et, quod pejus est, dicunt eum daemonium habere, qui daemonia pellit.

VERS. 21.-- Unum opus feci. De uno miramini, quid si omnia videretis? Unum opus fuit, quod totum hominem sanavit in sabbato, unde turbati sunt stulti observatores sabbati, sed convincit eos Moyses, jubens circumcidere octava die, quod aliquando occurrit in sabbato fieri. Et hoc est: Propterea ne quis turbetur, si salus semper fit etiam in sabbato. Et sciant omnes semper debere fieri quae salutis sunt.

VERS. 22.-- Circumcisionem. (ALC.) Propter tres causas data fuit circumcisio. Primo, etc., usque ad et cor a cupiditatibus spoliatur.

386DVERS. 23.-- Si circumcisionem, etc. Quasi vos qui in lege gloriamini contra legem quae me nuntiat, de morte mea tractatis, sed non est contra legem, quod totum hominem sanum feci in sabbato ego Dominus sabbati. Custodia enim sabbati sicut caetera omnia propter salutem hominis sunt, sicut vos ipsi circumcidentes in sabbato, (quia ad salutem pertinet) non solvere vos sabbatum creditis.

VERS. 24.-- Nolite judicare, etc. Non debetis mihi irasci de salvatione hominis in sabbato, sicut non irascemini de circumcisione in sabbato, quia non est judicandum secundum faciem, id est actionem personarum, ut quod licuit Moysi, mihi non liceat, sed justum judicium, id est, secundum veritatem 387Arerum: qua cognita, nec me nec Moysen damnabitis, nec me contrarium illi dicetis. In quo illos arguit, nos instruit, ne in judicandis causis personas accipiamus, non quod pro diversitate graduum diversimode personas non honoremus.

VERS. 25.-- Dicebant ergo. Quia etsi occultus ad festum ascendit, palam loquitur, et apparet potestas quae putabatur timiditas. Mirantur aliqui, quia potentia non tenetur cujus mors tantopere quaerebatur, et nondum plene intelligentes ejus esse potentiam, putant esse scientiam principum, quia cognoverunt eum esse Christum, unde sibi ipsis objiciunt non esse hunc Christum, cum nemo sciat unde sit Christus, quia etsi per prophetas dicitur locus nativitatis et hujusmodi, tamen aeterna nativitas ejus ex 387BPatre nascitur, unde Propheta: Generationem ejus quis enarrabit (Isa. LIII) Unde Christus ad utrumque respondet, et de infirmitate, et de majestate.

VERS. 28.-- Et me scitis. Id est, effigiem vultus mei, et unde sim nativitatem et parentes, quod est: secundum carnem me noscitis, sed non secundum Divinitatem, quia a me ipso non veni. Nesciebant eum Dei Filium esse, nec a Patre missum: nesciebant etiam Deum Patrem qui verus est genitor ipsius veritatis. Sed unde possunt scire, subdit: Ego scio. Ergo a me quaerite, in me credite, quia nemo novit Patrem nisi Filius, et cui Filius voluerit revelare (Marc. XI).

VERS. 29.-- Sed scio eum. Ideo scio eum, quia ab ipso sum ipse, etc. Breviter utrumque complectatur, 387Cab ipso sum, quia Filius de Patre, Pater non de alio, et in carne me videtis, quia ipse me misit. In quo ei magna generantis auctoritas et sibi communis cum hominibus natura.

VERS. 30.-- Hora ejus. Non quod necessitas sit facti, sed opportunitas voluntatis. Voluit enim opportuno tempore nasci, praemissa longa serie praeconum. Similiter praedicator Evangelii voluit opportuno tempore mori.

VERS. 32.-- Audierunt Pharisaei. Principes insani, audito murmure quo Christus a turba glorificabatur, quaerunt occidere, quem tamen nondum volentem apprehendere non potuerunt, sed docentem audierunt.

387DVERS. 33.-- Adhuc modicum tempus. Quasi dicat: Quod modo vultis, post facturi estis, sed non modo, quia adhuc modicum tempus vobiscum sum, quo dispensationem meam implere debeo, et tunc vado ad eum qui me misit, tunc pati debeo. Et qui modo non vultis audire praesentem et miracula facientem, quaeretis me post resurrectionem et assensionem cum tanta miracula fient per apostolos mea virtute, et cum tanta credet multitudo. Tunc enim compuncti de tanto scelere mortis Christi dixerunt Petro: Quid faciemus? Vobiscum sum et vado. Non ait ero, sed sum, quia et si secundum quod homo mutat loca, secundum quod Deus non mutat, cum ubique sit. Unde alibi: Filius 388Ahominis qui est in coelo, quasi dicit, loquens in terra.

VERS. 34.-- Non potestis venire. Non ait poteritis, sed potestis. Tales enim tunc erant qui non possent, sicut et alibi dicit Petro: Non potes me sequi modo, sequeris autem postea (Joan. XIII), sic nec de istis datur desperatio.

VERS. 35.-- Ad semetipsos, etc. Unde Domino dicere nescierunt, et tamen illud quod futurum erat nescientes pronuntiaverunt. Quod etsi non praesentia per apostolos, tamen iturus est ad gentes credituras.

VERS. 36.-- Quis est hic? Cum ignorantes prophetassent veritatem, replicantes eamdem quaestionem, ostendunt se prophetasse ex ignorantia, ut potius 388Bignorasse videantur quam prophetasse.

VERS. 37.-- In novissimo autem. Post dissensiones Judaeorum de Jesu, et post verba quae dixit, quibus alii conderentur, alii docerentur, in ultimo die scenopegiae vocat non solum loquendo, sed et clamando, ut qui sitit veniat ad eum.

Si quis sitit. Bibat spiritum, ut post exponit, et cum biberit, flumina de ventre ejus fluent aquae vivae. Venter est conscientia cordis, quae bibito spiritu purgata vivit, et habet fontem. Et ipsa est fons vel fluvius, qui manat benevolentia, dum proximo vult consulere, qui bona sua putat sibi soli sufficere, non fuit ille fons.

(HIER.) Audacter et tota libertate pronuntio, ex eo tempore, etc., usque ad quamobrem cum duceretur 388CJesus ad passionem negant, et Christum se nescire jurant.

VERS. 39, 40.-- Nondum enim, etc. Et si prophetae acceperunt eumdem spiritum: et Joannes et alii ante resurrectionem Christi; tamen erat alius modus donationis futurus apertior et plenior: post resurrectionem enim, quando primum apparuit apostolis, insufflavit in faciem eorum, qui flatu primo homini animam indidit, et de limo erexit. Significans ergo se esse eumdem, insufflavit spiritum apostolis, ut de luto exsurgerent. Deinde post ascensionem plenius Spiritum misit in igneis linguis, ut omnes gentes docerent, sicut illi tunc omnibus linguis: sic nunc unitas Ecclesiae loquitur omnibus linguis, a qua unitate praecisus non habet spiritum.

388DQuia Jesus nondum erat glorificatus, etc. Ex illa ergo turba, etc. Postquam autem baptizantur Spiritu sancto, et effunditur gratia Spiritus sancti in eis; tunc libere loquuntur ad principes Judaeorum: Oportet obedire magis Deo quam hominibus (Act. V): mortuos suscitant, inter flagella laetantur, sanguinem fundunt, et aliis suppliciis coronantur. Nondum ergo erat Spiritus in apostolis, nec de ventre eorum fluebant gratiae spirituales: quia nondum fuerat Dominus glorificatus.

VERS. 45.-- Venerunt ergo. Missi ad capiendum eum redierunt immunes a crimine, pleni admiratione divinae doctrinae testes.

VERS. 47.-- Nunquid et vos, etc. Laudabiliter 389Aseducti erant qui dimisso malo infidelitatis transierant ad fidem.

VERS. 48.-- Nunquid ex principibus aliquis, etc. Qui docent legem contemnunt eum qui misit legem, sed qui non noverant legem, credunt in eum qui dedit legem, ut impleatur quod dicitur: Ut non videntes videant, et videntes caeci fiant (Joan. IX).

VERS. 51.-- Nunquid lex. Quia illi perversi volebant prius esse damnatores quam cognitores, iste vero putabat quod si patienter eum praesentem audirent, forsan essent similes ministris qui maluerunt credere quam tenere. Auctoritate ergo legis vult eos inducere, ut eum prius velint audire.

VERS. 52, 53.-- Et tu Galilaeus es, etc. Et reversi sunt, etc. Galilaeus dicebatur Jesus, quoniam de 389BNazareth erant parentes ejus.

CHAPTER VII.

VERS. 1.-- After these things. The Lord, while abiding in the flesh, frequently walked in Galilee, that they who wish to imitate him may learn to pass over from vices to virtues: which that walking figured. Or that the holy men of that time might await the passing over of the word of God from Judea to the Gentiles.

VERS. 2.-- A festival day. Not that it was one, for there are seven; but after their manner, however many they are, they are called as it were one, on account of the one festivity: the scenopegia, the festivity of tabernacles, because in the seventh month 385Dthe Jews dwelt in tabernacles, in memory of those in which their fathers dwelt in the desert.

VERS. 3.-- That thy disciples also. The works which the true disciples knew, to be set forth by divine praises and not by popular favour.

VERS. 4.-- No man. They take counsel for his glory. As if: Thou doest wondrous things, namely in hiding, but to become known, that thou mayest be praised by all.

VERS. 6.-- The time. Therefore it is not now my time, but yours, because I am not a lover of the world as you are, but an accuser; and therefore the world does not hear [audit; read odit, "hate" -- Jo 7:7 Non potest mundus odisse vos, and the sentence turns on the world not hating its own] you, its own false witnesses, who call good things evil and evil things good.

386AVERS. 8.-- For I go not up. (AUG.) Some say that this pertains to the passion, because he went up upon the cross not at the festivity of scenopegia, but at the paschal one, at which the lamb is wont to be slain.

VERS. 10.-- Not openly, etc. Typically: Christ went up hidden to the festivity, because he lay hid in all those figures who is now made manifest to us. And that he lay hid is an example of avoiding evil men; that he speaks openly and is not held by those who were seeking him, belongs to his power.

VERS. 16.-- He answered them: My doctrine, the doctrine of a man, is not mine, but God's. Or because a word is not the word of no one, but of someone, his doctrine, that is, he himself the Word, his doctrine is not his own, but his from whom he is: which is - I am not from myself. And because he saw that this deep thing 386Bwould not be understood by all, he gave counsel: If thou wouldst understand, believe. This is: If anyone believes me to be the Son of God, he shall know concerning the doctrine, that is, he shall understand that my doctrine is not mine.

VERS. 18.-- He that speaketh of himself. Antichrist is not truthful, and injustice is in him, because he seeks his own glory, not God's, by whom he was not sent, but permitted to come. But he that, etc. That is, I, who though equal to the Father, yet in the form of a servant seek the glory of the Father and not my own, in this gave an example of humility, that we may ascribe all good things to God.

VERS. 19.-- Did not Moses, etc. I speak from God and seek his glory, truthful, just: but you who glory in the law, against the law which forbids to kill, seek to slay me.

386CVERS. 20.-- A demon. They seek to kill him, and, what is worse, they say that he has a demon, who drives out demons.

VERS. 21.-- One work I have done. You wonder at one; what if you saw all? It was one work, which healed the whole man on the sabbath, whereat the foolish observers of the sabbath were troubled; but Moses convicts them, commanding to circumcise on the eighth day, which sometimes fell to be done on the sabbath. And this is it: Therefore let no one be troubled if health is always wrought, even on the sabbath. And let all know that the things which belong to salvation ought always to be done.

VERS. 22.-- Circumcision. (ALC.) For three causes was circumcision given. First, etc., as far as and the heart is stripped of covetous desires.

386DVERS. 23.-- If circumcision, etc. As if: You who glory in the law, against the law which announces me, treat of my death; but it is not against the law that I made the whole man sound on the sabbath, I the Lord of the sabbath. For the keeping of the sabbath, like all other things, is for the salvation of man, just as you yourselves, circumcising on the sabbath (because it belongs to salvation), believe that you do not break the sabbath.

VERS. 24.-- Judge not, etc. You ought not to be angry with me for the saving of a man on the sabbath, as you are not angry over circumcision on the sabbath, because it is not to be judged according to the face, that is, the action of persons, so that what was lawful for Moses should not be lawful for me; but a just judgment, that is, according to the truth 387Aof things: which being known, you will condemn neither me nor Moses, nor will you say that I am contrary to him. Wherein he reproves them and instructs us, that in judging causes we accept not persons; not that we should not honour persons in diverse ways according to the diversity of degrees.

VERS. 25.-- They said therefore. Because, although he went up to the feast secretly, he speaks openly, and there appears as power what was thought to be timidity. Some wonder, because by his power he is not held, he whose death was so greatly sought; and not yet fully understanding that it is his own power, they think it is the knowledge of the princes, because they knew him to be the Christ; whence they object to themselves that this is not the Christ, since no one knows whence the Christ is, because although the place of his birth and things of this kind are told by the prophets, yet his eternal nativity is born of 387Bthe Father; whence the prophet: Who shall declare his generation (Isa. LIII) Whence Christ answers to both, both concerning his infirmity and concerning his majesty.

VERS. 28.-- And you know me. That is, the appearance of my countenance, and whence I am, my birth and parents, which is: you know me according to the flesh, but not according to the Divinity, because I am not come of myself. They did not know him to be the Son of God, nor sent from the Father; neither did they know God the Father, who is the true begetter of truth itself. But whence they can know it, he adds: I know. Therefore seek of me, believe in me, because no man knoweth the Father but the Son, and he to whom the Son will reveal him (Marc. XI).

VERS. 29.-- But I know him. Therefore I know him, because I am from him myself, etc. Let him briefly comprise both: 387CI am from him, because the Son is of the Father, the Father not of another; and you see me in the flesh, because he himself sent me. Wherein there is to him the great authority of one begetting, and to himself a nature common with men.

VERS. 30.-- His hour. Not that there was necessity of the deed, but opportunity of the will. For he willed to be born at a fitting time, a long series of heralds having been sent before. In like manner the preacher of the Gospel willed to die at a fitting time.

VERS. 32.-- The Pharisees heard. The mad princes, having heard the murmur by which Christ was glorified by the crowd, seek to kill him; whom nevertheless, while he was not yet willing, they could not apprehend, but they heard him teaching.

387DVERS. 33.-- Yet a little while. As if he should say: What you now wish, you will do afterwards, but not now, because yet a little while I am with you, in which I must fulfil my dispensation, and then I go to him who sent me, then I must suffer. And you who now will not hear me present and working miracles, will seek me after the resurrection and the assent [assensionem; read ascensionem, "the ascension", which the same chunk expounds throughout], when so great miracles shall be done by the apostles through my power, and when so great a multitude shall believe. For then, pricked in heart for so great a crime as the death of Christ, they said to Peter: What shall we do? I am with you, and I go. He does not say I shall be, but I am, because although, in that he is man, he changes places, in that he is God he does not change, since he is everywhere. Whence elsewhere: The Son 388Aof man who is in heaven, as if he should say it while speaking on earth.

VERS. 34.-- You cannot come. He does not say you shall be able, but you are able. For such were they then that they could not; as also he says elsewhere to Peter: Thou canst not follow me now, but thou shalt follow hereafter (Joan. XIII); so neither is despair given concerning these men.

VERS. 35.-- Among themselves, etc. Whence they knew not how to say it to the Lord; and yet, not knowing, they pronounced that which was to come. For although not in his own presence, yet through the apostles he is to go to the Gentiles who will believe.

VERS. 36.-- Who is this? Since, being ignorant, they had prophesied the truth, by repeating the same question they show that they had prophesied out of ignorance, so that they seem rather 388Bto have been ignorant than to have prophesied.

VERS. 37.-- And on the last. After the dissensions of the Jews concerning Jesus, and after the words which he spoke, by which some might be founded and others taught, on the last day of the scenopegia he calls, not only by speaking but also by crying out, that he who thirsts may come to him.

If any man thirst. Let him drink the spirit, as he afterwards expounds, and when he shall have drunk, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water. The belly is the conscience of the heart, which, the spirit being drunk, is purged and lives, and has a fountain. And it is itself a fountain or a river, which flows with benevolence while it wishes to take counsel for its neighbour; he who thinks his own good things sufficient for himself alone, that man was not a fountain.

(HIER.) Boldly and with all liberty I pronounce that from that time, etc., as far as wherefore, when 388CJesus was being led to the passion, they deny him, and swear that they know not Christ.

VERS. 39, 40.-- For as yet, etc. And although the prophets received the same spirit, both John and others before the resurrection of Christ, yet there was to be another manner of the giving, more open and more full: for after the resurrection, when he first appeared to the apostles, he breathed upon their face, he who by his breath put a soul into the first man and raised him up from the slime. Signifying therefore that he was the same, he breathed the spirit upon the apostles, that they might rise up out of the mud. Then, after the ascension, he sent the Spirit more fully in fiery tongues, that they might teach all nations, as they then spoke in all tongues: so now the unity of the Church speaks in all tongues, and he who is cut off from that unity has not the spirit.

388DBecause Jesus was not yet glorified, etc. Of that crowd therefore, etc. But after they are baptized with the Holy Spirit, and the grace of the Holy Spirit is poured out in them, then they speak freely to the princes of the Jews: We ought to obey God rather than men (Act. V): they raise the dead, they rejoice amid scourges, they pour out their blood, and are crowned with other torments. Not yet therefore was the Spirit in the apostles, nor did spiritual graces flow from their belly: because the Lord had not yet been glorified.

VERS. 45.-- They came therefore. Those sent to take him returned free from the crime, full of wonder, witnesses of the divine teaching.

VERS. 47.-- Are you also, etc. Praiseworthily 389Awere they seduced who, having let go the evil of unbelief, had passed over to faith.

VERS. 48.-- Hath any one of the rulers, etc. They who teach the law despise him who sent the law; but they who knew not the law believe in him who gave the law, that what is said may be fulfilled: That they who see not may see, and they who see may become blind (Joan. IX).

VERS. 51.-- Doth our law. Because those perverse men wished to be condemners before they were knowers, whereas this man thought that if they were patiently to hear him present, perhaps they would be like the officers who chose rather to believe than to hold him. By the authority of the law therefore he wishes to lead them on, that they may be willing to hear him first.

VERS. 52, 53.-- Art thou also a Galilean, etc. And they returned, etc. Jesus was called a Galilean, since his parents were of 389BNazareth.

CAPUT VIII.

VERS. 1.-- Jesus autem. Quia mos ei erat die docere in templo, vespere reverti in Bethaniam, quae est in monte Oliveti.

Mystice: Signat altitudinem misericordiae, unde nobis chrisma, id est unctio. Mane redit ad templum. Mane est ortus novae lucis post tenebras legis, quo fidelibus, id est, in templo ejus misericordiam venit pandere et dare. Sessio est humilitas Incarnationis, qua miseretur et docet, unde libentius populus venit ad eum, sed inimici Pharisaei de justitia tentant, an contra eam dicat. Sciebant mansuetum et misericordiae praedicatorem, unde placebat populo, 389Cunde putabant dicturum dimittendam esse adulteram, et in hoc diceretur hostis legis contrarius Moysi et Deo auctori et cum adultera reus mortis. Quod si secundum legem diceret lapidandam, deriderent, quasi non haberet mansuetudinem quam praedicabat pro qua amabatur. Ipse autem neutra capitur calumnia, sed servata mansuetudine respondet quod est verae justitiae: Qui sine peccato est, primus in illam lapidem mittat. Non autem statim judicat, sed inclinans se digito scribit in terram.

VERS. 3.-- Adducunt. Mystice: Mulier in adulterio deprehensa signat Synagogam, quae adulteravit legem Moysi, sequendo traditiones patrum. Vel quae dimissa lege Moysi, jam sequitur fidem Christi, unde Judaeus jam quasi adulteram accusat. Vel signat 389DEcclesiam, quae relinquens Deum, fornicata erat cum daemonibus, quam Judaeus accusat, quod ad Christum accedat, et quasi novum idololatriae genus reputata est.

VERS. 6.-- Jesus autem inclinans se deorsum. Quid scriberet in terra, sive verba illorum, sive quid aliud, non est diffinitum, secundum quod se inclinat, et digito scribit. Docet tamen ut antequam judicium fiat, nos debere inclinare, id est, condescendere in judicio aut faciendo uti discretione.

VERS. 8.-- Et iterum se inclinans. Data sententia justitiae qua illi conscii malorum, percussi sunt, iterum scribit in terram ex more, vultum alio vertens: ut illis sit liberum exire, quos praevidebat 390Acitius exituros quam plura interrogaturos. Docet autem nos sicut et ante correctionem alterius, ita et post nos ipsos humiliter investigare, ne idem vel aliud simile in nobis sit.

VERS. 11.-- Nec ego te condemnabo. Sed quia poterat timere ne iste qui sine peccato est eam puniret, dicit ei, ut misericors praeterita peccata dimittens: Nec ego te condemnabo, ut justus ne amplius peccet interdicens.

VERS. 12.-- Iterum ergo. Repulsis tentatoribus, sanata culpa mulieris, apertius subdit quid possit ex Divinitate, ne ambigant de eo homine an possit peccata dimittere. Ego sum lux mundi. Quae nube carnis tegitur, et sic toleranda hominibus efficitur, unde et peccata possum dimittere et etiam hominem 390Billuminare.

VERS. 13.-- Testimonium. Putant non credendum ei. Quasi: Solus de se testatur obliti praemissarum lucernarum, et ipsius Joannis Baptistae, qui de eo evidenter ostendit.

VERS. 14.-- Verum est testimonium. Meum testimonium ideo verum est habendum, quia scio unde venio, hoc est, quia testimonio Patris confirmatur. veni missus a Patre, non tamen eum deserens, et impleto ministerio ad Patrem redeo, nec tamen mundum relinquo.

VERS. 15.-- Vos secundum carnem judicatis. Quia illud nescitis, ideo vos secundum carnem judicatis, hoc est, hominem putatis, cui in laude sua credendum non sit, et etiam damnatis.

390CEgo non judico quemquam. Ego non judico, quasi, secundum carnem, ut nos, vel penitus non judico hoc primo adventu, sed salvo, sed quia secundo adventu judicabit, subdit hoc: Et si judico ego, etc.

VERS. 16.-- Quia solus (AUG.) Ideo verum, quia non solus, sed ego et Pater. Ecce ergo duo in personis, licet unum in substantia; quod est: Ideo verum est judicium meum, quia verus Dei Filius sum, unde probatur quod Filius, quia non solus, sed Pater mecum.

VERS. 17.-- Et in lege vestra scriptum est. (ID.) Dicto de judicio quod suum et Patris testimonium verum sit, probat ab auctoritate legis. Dicitur enim, etc., usque ad ut accusata Susanna habuit.

VERS. 19.-- Dicebant ergo ei. Secundum carnem 390Djudicantes, Patrem Christi carnaliter acceperunt; sed erat homo in aperto, Verbum Patris in occulto. Ubi est Pater tuus. Quasi dicant: Te videmus sed non Patrem tecum. Quibus econtra dicitur: Sed nec me scitis, quia si hominem quem videtis, non quod in occulto sum scitis, et ideo nec Patrem scitis. Sed si me sciretis, quod est prius, me cognoscite, et sic Patrem sciretis, quia unum sumus. Forsitan et Patrem, etc. Non dubitat, sed dubitantes quasi irridet et increpat; ut et dominus servo, forsitan Dominus tuus sum.

VERS. 20.-- Haec verba, etc. Gazae divitiae, Phylaxe servare. Typice: Gazophylacium est mysteria Scripturarum, quae sunt in templo, id est in Christo. 391AHaec secreta post resurrectionem aperuit apostolis, sed in his modo loquitur per parabolas non intelligentibus Judaeis. Et nemo apprehendit eum. Id est, loquentem de Deitate.

VERS. 21.-- Ego vado. Quasi sponte, et ideo frustra laboratis. Vadit illo unde venerat, et unde non discesserat. Quaeretis, ideo persequentes assumptum in coelis non ut amici habere cupientes. Unde eis poenam praedicit: In peccato vestro moriemini.

VERS. 22.-- Nunquid interficiet semetipsum? Stulta verba quasi si de morte diceret, non possent eum sequi ad mortem. Dicebat ergo non de morte, sed de gloria ad quam ibat per mortem, unde illis carnalibus subdit:

VERS. 23.-- Vos deorsum estis. Et ideo terram 391Bsapitis, sicut serpens terram comeditis.

VERS. 24.-- Dixi ergo vobis. Hic exponit quid sit esse de mundo, id est, esse peccatores infideles terrena sapientes, unde hoc eis exponit. Si enim. Sed econtrario spem dat credituris. Credideritis. Credideritis dicit, non perceperitis, quia nemo nunc capit immutabilem essentiam. Ego sum, moriemini, etc. Non dicit quid, vel Christus, vel Filius Dei, vel aliud hujusmodi, sed ad istos utitur verbo aeternae essentiae, sicut olim ad Moysen quem mittebat ad liberandum Israel. Quasi dicat: Qui misi Moysen ad liberandum filios Israel, ego per meipsum veni liberare perditos. Caetera vero omnia quae mittuntur, non vere sunt.

VERS. 25.-- Principium. (AUG.) Credite me principium, 391Cetc., usque ad ipse manus dextera, fortitudo, sapientia, et Verbum est Patris.

(ID.) Ecce quid est esse, quia principium in se manet, innovat omnia, et mutari non potest.

Loquor vobis. Et habeo vobis multa loqui, docendo, si credideritis, accusando si non credideritis, et qui modo non judico, sed salvo, habeo post judicare. Habeo dico, sed totum ex Patre qui verax est ex se, ex quo ego sum veritas quam genuit ex se, et ideo vera dico, et judico. Ecce quomodo dat gloriam Patri cui aequalis est, ut doceat nos minores dare gloriam Deo.

VERS. 27.-- Et non cognoverunt. Cum dicit, qui misit me, verax est, non intellexerunt quod de Patre diceret, quia adhuc habebant clausos oculos, et ideo 391Dsubdit eis: Cum exaltaveritis Filium, etc., ubi ostendit aliquos ibi esse, qui post passionem suam essent credituri et cognituri quis ipse esset.

VERS. 29.-- Quia ego sum. Esse est immutabile divinae substantiae. Sed ne ipse qui loquitur, intelligeretur esse Pater, subdit: Et a meipso facio nihil; quia a meipso non sum, sed a Patre.

Quia ego, etc. Semper ipsa aequalitas est. Semper non ex quodam initio et deinceps, sed sine initio et fine. Dei enim generatio non habet initium temporis, quia per Unigenitum facta sunt tempora.

VERS. 30.-- Multi crediderunt in eum. Fides est credere quod non vides, veritas est videre quod credidisti. 392ANon cognoscimus ut credamus, sed credimus ut cognoscamus: ideo Christus homo in terra fidem jacit, post se videndum dabit, quod est merces fidei.

VERS. 31.-- Vere discipuli. Ad hanc altitudinem pervenietis ex fide quae est humilis, et contemnitur, et est granum sinapis.

VERS. 33, 34.-- Liberi. Hoc verbum Dominus posuit a libertate, non ut plerumque Latine dici solet, liberari aliquis ab infirmitate. Et non dixerat Dominus: liberi eritis, sed: Veritas liberabit vos, in quo tamen verbo non intellexerunt nisi libertatem carnalem. Respondit eis, etc. Non illi qui crediderunt, sed alii in turba qui non crediderunt, qui audito liberabit vos, indignati sunt se vocari servos, putantes dixisse de carnali libertate. Et nemini servivimus. 392BMentiuntur. Joseph servivit in Aegypto, patres in Aegypto servierunt in luto, isti eidem tributis Caesaris.

Amen, amen dico, etc. Amen Hebraice, verum dicitur Latine, quod nullus interpres mutavit, ne vilesceret nudatum, sed honorem habeat secretum, et est quasi juramentum, unde Hieronymus super Isaiam: Jurat Dominus per semetipsum, quoniam sententiam oris sui et verbum quod semel protulit, nequaquam irritum facit, hoc est, per semetipsum juravit.

VERS. 35.-- Servus autem, etc. In hoc terruit nos. Quae ergo spes est libertatis his qui non sunt sine peccato?

VERS. 37.-- Scio, etc. Exposito a Domino contra 392Cgloriantes de vana libertate carnis, et vera servitute peccati, et vera libertate per se filium, concedens istis, scilicet quod filii Abrahae sunt per carnem, tales liberos ostendit non esse filios Abrahae, per imitationem fidei et justitiae, sed esse filios homicidae diaboli, quaerentes se occidere, et ita esse vere servos.

VERS. 39.-- Responderunt et dixerunt ei. Quia arguit eos facere mala patris, fatentes patrem suum esse Abraham, provocant aliqua maledicere contra Abraham, ut sit eis occasio faciendi quod volebant, sed Dominus Abraham laudat et istos degeneres damnat, dicens: Si filii Abrahae, etc.

VERS. 40.-- Nunc autem quaeritis, etc. Non dicit (quod poterat) se esse dominum Abrahae, ne sit occasio 392Dcalumniae, nec filium Dei, sed quod invident, et quod possunt occidere.

VERS. 41.-- Vos facitis opera. Jam innuit alium patrem quam Abraham, cujus filii sunt, non natura, sed imitatione. Dixerunt, etc. Nos, etc. Jam intelligentes non de origine carnis, sed de vita Dominum loqui, et ex usu Scripturarum scientes fornicationem spiritualiter dici, cum anima falsis diis subjicitur, mutant responsionem et tacent jam de patre Abraham, ne eis dicatur: Cur de eo gloriamini, cujus vitam non imitamini? et dicunt patrem Deum, volentes videre quid ad hoc posset eis dicere.

VERS. 42.-- Dixit ergo. Sicut praeostendit de Abraham, ita nunc ostendit Deum non patrem eis esse 393Aeorum, quia non cognoscunt Filium quem misit nec eum diligunt. Ego enim. Processio ex Patre aeterna est, in qua est Filius unigenitus Patri aequalis; venit homo factus, in quo frater hominibus.

VERS. 43.-- Quare loquelam. Ita res est ut dico, sed non potestis quod dico cognoscere, quia non potestis audire, cum non velitis credendo corrigi. Et hoc quare? Quia ex diabolo estis: filii diaboli perseverare elegistis.

VERS. 44.-- Vos ex patre, etc. Ecce jam determinat quis sit pater eorum, cujus filii sunt non nascendo, ut Manichaei dicunt, sed imitando; unde et Judaeis dicitur: Pater tuus Amorrhaeus est, mater tua Cethaea (Ezech. XVI).

Et desideria. Ideo filii diaboli quia desideria patris 393Bvestri vultis facere, et occidere me in carne, quia non potestis in mente. Ille homicida. Nota homicidam non solum qui ferro, sed etiam qui verbo occidit, ut diabolus primos parentes, unde in psalmo: Lingua eorum gladius acutus (Psal. LVI). Et in veritate non stetit. Quare non stetit? Quia non est in eo veritas. Id est, Verbum et sapientia Patris. Cum dicitur, in Christo est veritas, ita dicitur, ut ipse sit veritas. Si ergo diabolus stetisset in veritate, in Christo stetisset, sed non stetit in ea, quia non est ipsa in eo. Ex propriis loquitur, quia mendax est. Et hoc non sufficit, quia et homo mendax est qui non habet de proprio, sed ab alieno coepit, id est a diabolo, ideo addidit: Et pater ipsius. Scilicet mendacii quod invenit. Ex propriis. Quia non aliunde habet, 393Csed a se postquam cecidit a veritate; unde subditur esse pater mendacii quod ex se genuit, ut Deus ex se veritatem.

VERS. 46.-- Quis ex vobis, etc. Non dedignatur ex ratione ostendere se non esse peccatorem, etc.

VERS. 48.-- Nonne, etc. Quia convicti sunt non esse filii Abrahae vel Dei, sed diaboli, ingerunt convicia, quia non poterant resistere.

VERS. 49.-- Honorifico. Non me (ne arrogans vobis videar) facio quod debeo, sed vos non facitis quod debetis, quia non honorastis me, quem si cognosceretis, sicut ego honorifico Patrem, sic vos me honoraretis

VERS. 50.-- Ego autem. Audito convicio, Daemonium habes, econtra de gloria sua hoc solum dicit: 393DHonorifico Patrem, omnia tribuendo ei, et ego non quaero gloriam meam ut homines, qui illatis contumeliis aliquid reddunt, sed Patri reservo, et est qui quaerat et judicet. Duae sunt tentationes. Una probationis, de qua: Tentat vos Deus (Deut. XIII). Alia deceptionis, de qua: Deus intentator malorum est (Jac. II). Sic et duo timores: unus quem charitas foras mittit, alter qui permanet in saeculum saeculi. Sic et duo judicia: unum damnationis, quo non judicat Pater, sed omne judicium dedit Filio: alterum discretionis; unde: Judica me, Deus, et discerne causam meam (Psal. XLII). Secundum hoc dicitur: Est Pater qui quaerat gloriam meam, et discernat a gloria vestra, quia vos secundum saeculum gloriamini, 394Aego non, sed ea gloria quam habui apud Patrem antequam mundus esset, quae est ab humana inflatione discreta.

VERS. 51.-- Amen, amen dico vobis. Non ego, sed Pater quaerit gloriam meam; ego autem dico, Si vos dicitis, Daemonium habes, ego vos ad vitam voco. Hinc discat homo Jesus, non malum, sed bonum reddere. Non videbit. Quod dicitur, videbit vel gustabit, nihil aliud est quam experietur.

VERS. 52.-- Nunc cognoscimus. Sicut accepta contumelia bonus fit melior, ita malus de beneficio pejor.

VERS. 53.-- Quem teipsum facis? Secundum illos se faciebat Filium Dei, aequalem sibi, quasi non esset, sed non faciebat, imo vere erat; unde Apostolus: 394BNon rapinam arbitratus est esse se aequalem Deo (Philip. II). Ideo non qui es, sed quem teipsum facis?

VERS. 54.-- Respondit Jesus. Contra hoc quod dicunt: Quem te facis? refert gloriam suam ad Patrem, de quo est quod Deus est.

VERS. 55. Est Pater meus. Aperte dicit Patrem suum esse Deum qui annuntiatus est Judaeis: non recentem Deum vobis annuntio, sed illum de cujus cognitione soletis gloriari. Qui glorificat. Vox Patris glorificat Filium in baptismo, in monte, et in aliis hujusmodi. Et non cognovistis. Non cognovistis spiritualiter, dum pro terrenis servistis. Ecce videt aperte eos sibi resistere, sed tamen non desinit se eis praedicare. Et si dixero, etc. Ne in hoc arrogans 394Cvideatur gloriari, addit, et si dixero. Arrogantia sic caveatur, ut veritas non relinquatur.

VERS. 56.-- Abraham. Credens exsultavit sperando, ut videret intelligendo tantum mysterium.

Diem meum. (AUG.) Incertum est in diem temporalem, etc., usque ad et quod ipsum caro factum est?

VERS. 59.-- Et exivit. Exiens de templo, id est, de figuris Judaeorum, videamus quid fecerit in templo gentium.

CHAPTER VIII.

VERS. 1.-- And Jesus. Because it was his custom to teach in the temple by day, and in the evening to return to Bethania, which is on the Mount of Olives.

Mystically: It signifies the height of mercy, whence comes to us the chrism, that is, the anointing. In the morning he returns to the temple. The morning is the rising of the new light after the darkness of the law, in which he comes to open and to give mercy to the faithful, that is, in his temple. The sitting is the humility of the Incarnation, by which he has mercy and teaches; whence the people came the more willingly to him, but the hostile Pharisees tempt him concerning justice, whether he will speak against it. They knew him to be meek and a preacher of mercy, whence he was pleasing to the people; 389Cwhence they thought he would say that the adulteress was to be let go, and in this he would be called an enemy of the law, contrary to Moses and to God its author, and, along with the adulteress, guilty of death. But if according to the law he should say that she was to be stoned, they would deride him, as though he had not the meekness which he preached, for which he was loved. But he himself is caught by neither calumny; rather, his meekness being kept, he answers what belongs to true justice: He that is without sin, let him first cast a stone at her. Yet he does not judge at once, but bowing himself down he writes with his finger on the ground.

VERS. 3.-- They bring. Mystically: The woman taken in adultery signifies the Synagogue, which adulterated the law of Moses by following the traditions of the fathers. Or her who, the law of Moses being let go, now follows the faith of Christ, whence the Jew accuses her as an adulteress. Or she signifies 389Dthe Church, which, forsaking God, had fornicated with demons, whom the Jew accuses because she comes to Christ, and she was reputed as it were a new kind of idolatry.

VERS. 6.-- But Jesus bowing himself down. What he wrote on the ground, whether their words or something else, is not determined, according as he bows himself down and writes with his finger. Yet he teaches that, before judgment is made, we ought to bow ourselves, that is, to condescend in judgment, or by acting to use discretion.

VERS. 8.-- And again bowing himself down. The sentence of justice having been given, by which they, conscious of their evils, were struck, he writes again on the ground after his custom, turning his face elsewhere: that it might be free for them to go out, whom he foresaw 390Awould go out sooner than ask more questions. And he teaches us, as before the correction of another, so also afterwards, to search ourselves humbly, lest the same thing, or something else like it, be in us.

VERS. 11.-- Neither will I condemn thee. But because she could fear lest he who is without sin should punish her, he says to her, as one merciful forgiving past sins: Neither will I condemn thee, as one just forbidding that she sin any more.

VERS. 12.-- Again therefore. The tempters being repulsed and the woman's fault healed, he adds more openly what he can do out of his Divinity, lest they doubt concerning him as a man whether he can forgive sins. I am the light of the world. Which is covered with the cloud of the flesh, and so is made bearable to men; whence I can both forgive sins and also enlighten 390Ba man.

VERS. 13.-- Testimony. They think that he is not to be believed. As if: He alone testifies of himself, forgetful of the lamps sent before, and of John the Baptist himself, who showed evidently concerning him.

VERS. 14.-- My testimony is true. My testimony is therefore to be held true, because I know whence I come, that is, because it is confirmed by the testimony of the Father. I came sent by the Father, yet not forsaking him, and, my ministry fulfilled, I return to the Father, nor yet do I leave the world.

VERS. 15.-- You judge according to the flesh. Because you know not that, therefore you judge according to the flesh, that is, you think me a man who is not to be believed in his own praise, and you even condemn me.

390CI judge not any man. I judge not, as it were, according to the flesh, as we do [ut nos; read ut vos, "as you do" -- Christ is answering Vos secundum carnem judicatis]; or I do not judge at all at this first coming, but save; but because at the second coming he will judge, he adds this: And if I do judge, etc.

VERS. 16.-- Because I am alone (AUG.) Therefore true, because not alone, but I and the Father. Behold therefore two in persons, though one in substance; which is: Therefore my judgment is true, because I am the true Son of God; whence it is proved that he is the Son, because he is not alone, but the Father is with me.

VERS. 17.-- And in your law it is written. (ID.) Having spoken of the judgment, that his own and the Father's testimony is true, he proves it from the authority of the law. For it is said, etc., as far as as Susanna when accused had.

VERS. 19.-- They said therefore to him. Judging according to the flesh, 390Dthey took Christ's Father carnally; but the man was in the open, the Word of the Father in secret. Where is thy Father. As if they should say: We see thee, but not the Father with thee. To whom on the contrary it is said: But neither do you know me; because if it is the man whom you see, you do not know what I am in secret, and therefore neither do you know the Father. But if you knew me, which is first, know me, and so you would know the Father, because we are one. Perhaps also the Father, etc. He does not doubt, but as it were mocks and rebukes those who doubt; as a master to a servant, perhaps I am thy lord.

VERS. 20.-- These words, etc. Gazae, riches; Phylaxe, to keep. Typically: The treasury is the mysteries of the Scriptures, which are in the temple, that is, in Christ. 391AThese secret things he opened to the apostles after the resurrection, but in them he now speaks by parables, the Jews not understanding. And no man apprehended him. That is, speaking of his Godhead.

VERS. 21.-- I go. As it were of my own accord, and therefore you labour in vain. He goes thither whence he had come, and whence he had not departed. You shall seek, therefore persecuting him taken up into the heavens, not desiring to have him as friends do. Whence he foretells them their punishment: You shall die in your sin.

VERS. 22.-- Will he kill himself? Foolish words, as if, if he spoke of death, they could not follow him unto death. He was speaking therefore not of death, but of the glory to which he was going through death; whence to those carnal men he adds:

VERS. 23.-- You are below. And therefore you savour of the earth, 391Bas the serpent you eat earth.

VERS. 24.-- I said therefore to you. Here he expounds what it is to be of the world, that is, to be sinners, unbelievers, savouring earthly things; whence he expounds this to them. For if. But on the contrary he gives hope to those who will believe. You shall believe. He says, you shall believe, not, you shall perceive, because no one now grasps the unchangeable essence. I am, you shall die, etc. He does not say what: neither Christ, nor the Son of God, nor anything else of this kind; but to these men he uses the word of the eternal essence, as of old to Moses, whom he was sending to deliver Israel. As if he should say: I who sent Moses to deliver the children of Israel, am come myself to deliver the lost. But all other things which are sent, are not truly.

VERS. 25.-- The beginning. (AUG.) Believe me to be the beginning, 391Cetc., as far as he himself is the right hand, the strength, the wisdom, and the Word of the Father.

(ID.) Behold what it is to be: because the beginning abides in itself, makes all things new, and cannot be changed.

I speak to you. And I have many things to speak to you, teaching if you shall have believed, accusing if you shall not have believed; and I who now judge not, but save, have afterwards to judge. I have, I say, but the whole from the Father, who is truthful of himself, from whom I am the truth which he begot of himself; and therefore I say true things, and I judge. Behold how he gives glory to the Father, to whom he is equal, that he may teach us who are less to give glory to God.

VERS. 27.-- And they understood not. When he says, he that sent me is true, they did not understand that he spoke of the Father, because they still had their eyes closed; and therefore 391Dhe adds to them: When you shall have lifted up the Son, etc., where he shows that some were there who after his passion would believe and would know who he was.

VERS. 29.-- Because I am. To be is the unchangeableness of the divine substance. But lest he himself who speaks should be understood to be the Father, he adds: And of myself I do nothing; because I am not of myself, but of the Father.

Because I, etc. That equality itself always is. Always, not from some beginning and thenceforward, but without beginning and without end. For the generation of God has no beginning of time, because through the Only-begotten times were made.

VERS. 30.-- Many believed in him. Faith is to believe what thou seest not; truth is to see what thou hast believed. 392AWe do not know that we may believe, but we believe that we may know: therefore Christ as man on earth casts faith, afterwards he will give himself to be seen, which is the reward of faith.

VERS. 31.-- Truly disciples. To this height you shall come out of faith, which is humble, and is despised, and is a grain of mustard seed.

VERS. 33, 34.-- Free. This word the Lord set from liberty, not as it is for the most part wont to be said in Latin, that someone is freed from infirmity. And the Lord had not said: you shall be free, but: The truth shall make you free; in which word nevertheless they understood nothing but carnal liberty. He answered them, etc. Not those who believed, but others in the crowd who did not believe, who, having heard shall make you free, were indignant that they were called servants, thinking that he had spoken of carnal liberty. And we have served no man. 392BThey lie. Joseph served in Egypt; the fathers in Egypt served in the mud; these men serve the same by the tributes of Caesar.

Amen, amen I say, etc. Amen in Hebrew is said in Latin to be "true," which no interpreter has changed, lest, being stripped bare, it should grow cheap, but that it may keep its honour by being secret; and it is as it were an oath, whence Jerome upon Isaias: The Lord swears by himself, since he in no wise makes void the sentence of his mouth and the word which he has once uttered; that is, he swore by himself.

VERS. 35.-- But the servant, etc. In this he terrified us. What hope of liberty then is there for those who are not without sin?

VERS. 37.-- I know, etc. The Lord having expounded against 392Cthose who gloried in the vain liberty of the flesh, and having expounded the true servitude of sin and the true liberty through himself the Son, granting to these men, namely that they are sons of Abraham through the flesh, he shows that such children are not sons of Abraham by the imitation of faith and of justice, but are sons of the murderer the devil, seeking to kill him, and so are truly servants.

VERS. 39.-- They answered and said to him. Because he reproves them for doing the works of their father, confessing their father to be Abraham, they provoke him to speak some evil things against Abraham, that it may be for them an occasion of doing what they wished; but the Lord praises Abraham and condemns these degenerate men, saying: If you are the children of Abraham, etc.

VERS. 40.-- But now you seek, etc. He does not say (what he could) that he is the Lord of Abraham, lest it be an occasion 392Dof calumny, nor that he is the Son of God; but that which they envy, and that which they are able to kill.

VERS. 41.-- You do the works. Now he hints at another father than Abraham, whose sons they are, not by nature, but by imitation. They said, etc. We, etc. Now understanding that the Lord spoke not of the origin of the flesh but of the life, and knowing from the usage of the Scriptures that fornication is spoken of spiritually, when the soul is subjected to false gods, they change their answer and are now silent about father Abraham, lest it be said to them: Why do you glory in him whose life you do not imitate? And they say that God is their father, wishing to see what he could say to them in answer to this.

VERS. 42.-- He said therefore. As he showed beforehand concerning Abraham, so now he shows that God is not their father to them, 393Abecause they do not know the Son whom he sent, nor do they love him. For I. The procession from the Father is eternal, in which the Son is the only-begotten, equal to the Father; he came made man, in which he is brother to men.

VERS. 43.-- Why do you not know my speech. The matter is as I say, but you cannot know what I say, because you cannot hear, since you are not willing to be corrected by believing. And why is this? Because you are of the devil: you have chosen to persevere as sons of the devil.

VERS. 44.-- You are of your father, etc. Behold, now he determines who is their father, whose sons they are not by being born, as the Manichees say, but by imitating; whence also it is said to the Jews: Thy father is an Amorrhite, thy mother a Cethite (Ezech. XVI).

And the desires. Therefore sons of the devil, because you will do the desires of your father, 393Band kill me in the flesh, because you cannot in the mind. He is a murderer. Note that he is a murderer not only who kills with the sword, but also who kills with a word, as the devil killed the first parents; whence in the psalm: Their tongue is a sharp sword (Psal. LVI). And he stood not in the truth. Why did he not stand? Because the truth is not in him. That is, the Word and wisdom of the Father. When it is said, in Christ is the truth, it is so said that he himself is the truth. If therefore the devil had stood in the truth, he would have stood in Christ; but he did not stand in it, because it is not itself in him. He speaketh of his own, because he is a liar. And this is not enough, because a man also is a liar who has it not of his own, but has begun from another, that is, from the devil; therefore he added: And the father thereof. Namely, of the lie which he invented. Of his own. Because he has it not from elsewhere, 393Cbut from himself, after he fell from the truth; whence it is added that he is the father of the lie which he begot of himself, as God of himself the truth.

VERS. 46.-- Which of you, etc. He does not disdain to show by reason that he is not a sinner, etc.

VERS. 48.-- Do not we, etc. Because they were convicted of not being sons of Abraham or of God, but of the devil, they heap up reproaches, because they could not resist.

VERS. 49.-- I honour. Not myself (lest I seem arrogant to you): I do what I ought, but you do not do what you ought, because you have not honoured me; whom if you knew, as I honour the Father, so would you honour me

VERS. 50.-- But I.
