Having heard the reproach, Thou hast a demon, on the contrary concerning his own glory he says this only: 393DI honour the Father, ascribing all things to him; and I seek not my own glory as men do, who render something back for the insults offered them, but I reserve it to the Father, and there is one who seeketh and judgeth. There are two temptations. One of probation, of which: God tempteth you (Deut. XIII). The other of deception, of which: God is no tempter of evils (Jac. II). So also there are two fears: one which charity casts out, another which abides for ever and ever. So also there are two judgments: one of damnation, by which the Father judges not, but has given all judgment to the Son; the other of discernment; whence: Judge me, O God, and distinguish my cause (Psal. XLII). According to this it is said: There is a Father who seeks my glory, and distinguishes it from your glory, because you glory according to the world, 394AI do not, but with that glory which I had with the Father before the world was, which is distinguished from human puffing up.

VERS. 51.-- Amen, amen I say to you. Not I, but the Father seeks my glory; but I say, If you say, Thou hast a demon, I call you to life. Hence let man learn Jesus, to render not evil but good. He shall not see. That which is said, he shall see, or he shall taste, is nothing else than he shall experience.

VERS. 52.-- Now we know. As a good man, having received an insult, becomes better, so a bad man becomes worse from a benefit.

VERS. 53.-- Whom dost thou make thyself? According to them he was making himself the Son of God, equal to God, as though he were not so; but he was not making himself so, nay rather he truly was; whence the Apostle: 394BHe thought it not robbery to be equal to God (Philip. II). Therefore not, who art thou, but, whom dost thou make thyself?

VERS. 54.-- Jesus answered. Against this which they say: Whom dost thou make thyself? he refers his glory to the Father, of whom is that which he is God.

VERS. 55. He is my Father. He says openly that his Father is the God who was announced to the Jews: I announce to you no new God, but him of whose knowledge you are wont to glory. Who glorifieth. The voice of the Father glorifies the Son in baptism, on the mountain, and in other such places. And you have not known. You have not known him spiritually, while you served for earthly things. Behold, he sees openly that they resist him, and yet he does not cease to preach himself to them. And if I shall say, etc. Lest in this he should seem 394Cto glory arrogantly, he adds, and if I shall say. Let arrogance be so avoided that truth be not forsaken.

VERS. 56.-- Abraham. Believing, he rejoiced in hoping, that he might see by understanding so great a mystery.

My day. (AUG.) It is uncertain: unto a temporal day, etc., as far as and that this same was made flesh?

VERS. 59.-- And he went out. Going out from the temple, that is, from the figures of the Jews, let us see what he did in the temple of the Gentiles.

CAPUT IX.

VERS. 1.-- Et praeteriens. Non stans in via peccatorum, sed secus viam nostrae mortalitatis. Caecum. Caecus significat humanum genus, in quo caecitas 394Dnaturalis, quia peccante primo homine, vitium propter naturam inolevit, unde secundum mentem omnis homo caecus natus est.

VERS. 3.-- Neque hic peccavit. Peccaverunt quidem, sed non illo peccato factum est, ut caecus nasceretur, sed haec est causa, ut manifestentur opera Dei in illo. Ut eum illuminando et per eum quid in caecitate humani generis facturus sit significando, Filius Dei manifestetur; unde subdit: Me oportet operari.

VERS. 5.-- Quandiu sum in mundo. Non solum tunc Christus lumen erat in mundo, sed et semper usque ad consummationem saeculi per fidem est cum electis, et est tempus operandi. Nox illa est infernalis, 395Ain qua nulli licet operari, ut nec ardenti diviti licuit, sed tantum est tempus recipiendi.

VERS. 6.-- Et fecit lutum. Sicut primum hominem de limo terrae formavit, ita per idem genus luti genus humanum reformavit. De saliva lutum fecit, quia Verbum caro factum est. Saliva est sapientia quae ex ore Altissimi prodiit. Terra est caro Christi. Unxit oculos. Id est, catechumenum fecit, qui credit Verbum carnem factum. Unctus nondum videt, sed mittitur ut lavet, id est, baptizetur in Misso, id est in Christo, propter quod nomen piscinae interpretatur. Baptizatus a peccatis lumen recepit.

VERS. 7.-- Et venit videns, etc. Quaeritur quomodo istum sine fide sanaverit, cum legatur neminem sine sanatione interiore, exterius sanasse? Sed hoc dicitur 395Bde illis qui propter peccata infirmabantur: iste non propter peccatum, sed propter gloriam Dei languebat.

VERS. 16.-- Non est hic homo a Deo, etc. Ille spiritualiter custodit sabbatum, qui est sine peccato; illi non, qui servi sunt peccati.

VERS. 17.-- Tu quid dicis, etc. Quaerunt calumniam, ut ille de synagoga pelleretur, sed a Christo inveniretur. Ille autem dixit, etc. Adhuc inunctus in corde, qui confitetur, non tamen mentitur.

VERS. 18.-- Donec vocaverunt, etc. Mystice: Parentes, id est diabolus vel vitia, qui dicunt. Aetatem habet, ipse de se loquatur, qui nolunt hominem confiteri Christum.

Da gloriam Deo: Nos scimus, etc. Dare gloriam Deo, est veritatem de aliquo quasi praesente Deo dicere, 395Choc est: Confiteri istum non esse Deum sicut nos, sed peccatorem.

VERS. 27.-- Quid iterum, etc. (AUG.) Stomachatur contra duritiam eorum, de caeco factus videns, et caecos jam non ferens.

VERS. 28.-- Maledixerunt ergo ei. Secundum cor eorum maledictio est. Sed quod dicitur optabile est omnibus, ut discipuli Christi sint.

VERS. 29.-- Hunc autem nescimus. Superius dixerant: Hunc scimus: unde hinc dicunt se nescire, sed nescire nos dicimus quae abjicimus. Ille nescitur, latet humilitate, proditur signorum virtute: nescitur unde sit, et ut Deus caecos illuminat.

VERS. 33.-- Nisi esset hic a Deo, etc. Spreta eorum ira, libere veritatem confitetur. Haec enim non 395Dnisi a Domino, nec a discipulis fierent, nisi Deus in eis.

VERS. 34.-- In peccatis natus est totus. Id est, cum clausis oculis, quod dicunt esse pro peccatis parentum, sed Christus totum sanat, extra oculos, intus cor aperit. Et tu doces nos? Et ejecerunt eum foras. Toties interrogando ut discerent, magistrum fecerant, sed ingrati docentem projecerunt. Sed quia expulsus, magis factus est Christianus.

VERS. 36.-- Quis est, etc. Adhuc inunctus his verbis ostendit se jamdudum cor ad credendum habuisse paratum, sed in quem credere debeat, ignorasse.

VERS. 37.-- Et vidisti. Jam Dominus lavat faciem 396Ainuncti, jam cordis oculos illuminat et ille non tantum Filium hominis, sed etiam confitetur Filium Dei.

VERS. 39.-- Ut qui non vident. Id est, qui se videre putant, quaerunt ut videant, et qui vident, id est, qui se videre putant, et medicum non quaerunt, et hoc est judicium, id est discretio quod discernit causam credentium a superbis. Judicium enim damnationis non modo, sed in fine faciet; unde: Ego non judico quemquam.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- And passing by. Not standing in the way of sinners, but beside the way of our mortality. A blind man. The blind man signifies the human race, in which blindness is 394Dnatural, because, when the first man sinned, the fault grew ingrained by reason of nature; whence according to the mind every man is born blind.

VERS. 3.-- Neither hath this man sinned. They did indeed sin, but it was not brought about by that sin that he should be born blind; rather this is the cause, that the works of God should be made manifest in him. That, by enlightening him and by signifying through him what he is to do in the blindness of the human race, the Son of God may be made manifest; whence he adds: I must work.

VERS. 5.-- As long as I am in the world. Not only then was Christ the light in the world, but he is also always with the elect through faith unto the consummation of the age, and it is the time for working. That night is the infernal night, 395Ain which it is permitted to none to work, as neither was it permitted to the burning rich man; but there is only a time of receiving.

VERS. 6.-- And he made clay. As he formed the first man out of the slime of the earth, so through the same kind of clay he re-formed the human race. Of spittle he made clay, because the Word was made flesh. The spittle is the wisdom which came forth from the mouth of the Most High. The earth is the flesh of Christ. He anointed the eyes. That is, he made him a catechumen, who believes the Word made flesh. The anointed man does not yet see, but is sent to wash, that is, to be baptized in the Sent One, that is, in Christ, on account of which the name of the pool is so interpreted. Baptized, baptized from his sins, he received the light.

VERS. 7.-- And he came seeing, etc. It is asked: how did he heal this man without faith, when it is read that he healed no one outwardly without an inward healing? But this is said 395Bof those who were sick on account of their sins: this man was languishing not on account of sin, but for the glory of God.

VERS. 16.-- This man is not of God, etc. He keeps the sabbath spiritually who is without sin; not they, who are servants of sin.

VERS. 17.-- What sayest thou, etc. They seek a pretext for accusation, that he might be driven out of the synagogue, but be found by Christ. But he said, etc. Still anointed in heart: he confesses, yet he does not lie.

VERS. 18.-- Until they called, etc. Mystically: the parents, that is the devil or the vices, who say. He is of age, let him speak for himself: they who are unwilling that a man confess Christ.

Give glory to God: We know, etc. To give glory to God is to speak the truth about someone as though God were present, 395Cthat is: to confess that this man is not God, as we do, but a sinner.

VERS. 27.-- Why again, etc. (AUG.) He is vexed at their hardness, having from a blind man been made one that sees, and now not bearing with the blind.

VERS. 28.-- They reviled him therefore. According to their heart it is a curse. But what is said is to be desired by all, that they should be disciples of Christ.

VERS. 29.-- But this man we know not. Above they had said: This man we know: whence here they say that they know not; but we say that we know not the things which we cast away. He is not known, he lies hid by humility, he is betrayed by the power of his signs: it is not known whence he is, and as God he enlightens the blind.

VERS. 33.-- Unless this man were of God, etc. Their anger despised, he freely confesses the truth. For these things would not be done 395Dsave by the Lord, nor by the disciples, unless God were in them.

VERS. 34.-- He was born wholly in sins. That is, with closed eyes, which they say to be for the sins of the parents; but Christ heals the whole: outside, the eyes; within, he opens the heart. And dost thou teach us? And they cast him out. By questioning him so often that they might learn, they had made him a master; but ungrateful, they cast out the one who taught. Yet because he was expelled, he was the more made a Christian.

VERS. 36.-- Who is he, etc. Still anointed, by these words he shows that he had long had a heart ready to believe, but had not known in whom he ought to believe.

VERS. 37.-- And thou hast seen. Now the Lord washes the face 396Aof the anointed, now he enlightens the eyes of the heart, and he confesses not only the Son of man but also the Son of God.

VERS. 39.-- That they who see not. That is, they who think that they see seek to see; and they who see, that is, they who think that they see and do not seek the physician; and this is the judgment, that is, the discernment which distinguishes the cause of believers from the proud. For he will execute the judgment of damnation not now, but at the end; whence: I judge not any man.

CAPUT X.

VERS. 1.-- Amen, amen dico vobis. Quia Pharisaei jactabant se videre, quod possent, si oves Christi essent, Dominus contra eorum arrogantiam de grege suo, et de ostio quo intratur in ovile similitudinem proponit, ostendens quod nec sapientia, nec observatio legis, nec bona vita quidquam valet nisi 396Bper eum: sine eo nihil sunt in quocunque Judaeo, vel gentili et philosopho vel Pharisaeo qui docent beate vivere: vel haeretico sub nomine Christi: nec tamen in Christo praedicante, sed omnes sine eo rapiunt et occidunt. Per ostium. Id est per me, ut post exponit. Haec janua est humilis: qui per hanc vult intrare, oportet ut se humiliet, ne, si erectus est, offendat in humilem januam. Fur. Quia quod alienum est suum dicit, id est, oves Dei suas facit. Latro, quia quod furatus est occidit. Sed omnes qui veritatem tenent et docent in Christo pastore, sunt unus pastor.

VERS. 2.-- Pastor est ovium. Christus proprie pastor est secundum officium pascendi oves, ut ipse 396Cpost exponit, qui per se ostium intrat in ovile ovium. Sed quia alii comparticipes sunt ei pastoralis officii, quae de eo proprie dicuntur, etiam eis suo modo congruunt. Ostium est quo intratur, Christus ergo pastor per se ostium, et quicunque sub eo pastor, per ipsum ostium intrat in corda ovium; quia si aliud, praedicat, non audiunt oves, si Christum, oves Christi hanc vocem agnoscunt.

VERS. 3.-- Huic ostiarius aperit. Ostiarius, id est Christus qui se aperit, vel Scriptura quae ducit ad Christum, vel Spiritus sanctus qui docet omnem veritatem.

Et oves, etc. (AUG.) Aliqui sunt verae oves, quia novit Dominus, etc., usque ad hanc nullus proprius contemnit, nullus alienus audit.

396DVERS. 6.-- Illi autem non cognoverunt. Praedestinatae oves ad tempus se nesciunt, nec vocem pastoris audiunt, sed in fine perseverant, e contra lupi ad tempus audiunt, sed in fine perseverant.

VERS. 8.-- Omnes, etc. Fur est qui per simoniam vel aliquo alio modo contra Christum intrat, et postquam intraverit malo exemplo occidit et sic aperte latro. Nec talis dicendus est mercenarius, ideo lupo veniente non resistit, sed pro mercede cedit. Si lupo violenter distrahente non resistit, mercedem hanc recipit, quod non distrahitur cum ovibus, sed postea male vivendo oves corrumpit, et jam in aliam partem relapsus fit fur vel latro, nomen mercenarii amittens.

397AVERS. 9.-- Si quis. Hic evidenter ostendit, non solum pastorem, sed et oves intrare per ostium. Et ingredietur. Intus bene cogitando, egredietur extra exemplo aliorum bene operando. Vel ingredietur in Ecclesiam, ut hic vivat per fidem. Unde post dicit: Ut vitam habeant. Et egredietur de ista vita, ut in aeternum vivat. Unde post et abundantius habeat vitam et pascua inveniet ingrediens et egrediens.

VERS. 10.-- Nisi ut furetur, etc. Furetur alienam rem sibi usurpando, mactet, a fide retrahendo, perdat in aeternam damnationem.

VERS. 12.-- Mercenarius autem, etc. Mercenarius est qui quaerit quae sua sunt, non quae Christi, qui servit Deo non propter Deum, sed pro aliqua mercede; qui culpabilis est, et non est filius, sed tamen 397Bnecessarius et tolerandus. Fur vero semper est cavendus. Praepositi vero qui filii sunt, sunt et pastores, et tamen unus est pastor, quia illi omnes sunt membra illius cujus sunt oves propriae. Et lupus rapit. Lupus est vel violentus qui corporaliter vastat, vel diabolus qui spiritualiter dissipat, cui qui non animas ovium, sed sua quaerit, non occurrit resistendo vel mala arguendo, quod est fugere, quia timor est fuga animi: fugit ne perdat quod diligit, unde reddens causam fugae subdit: Quia mercenarius est, et non amat oves, sed sua: pastores autem non dicuntur fugere si cedunt persecutioni, et servant se ovium utilitati, quibus etiam corpore absentes, sunt spiritu praesentes.

VERS. 14.-- Ego sum pastor. Post culpam ficti, 397Citerum formam veri pastoris opponit. Ego sum pastor bonus et animam pono pro ovibus. Quod non ipse unus fecit, nisi quod omnes sui et ipse unus sunt, quia ipse sine illis hoc potuit, illi non sine eo sine quo nihil possunt.

VERS. 15.-- Et ego. Filius per se agnoscit Patrem, nos per illum, sicut alibi, Deum nemo vidit unquam, nisi Unigenitus qui est in sinu Patris, ipse enarraverit (Matth. XI). Item nemo novit Filium nisi Pater, neque Patrem quis novit nisi Filius, et cui Filius voluerit revelare (Matth. XI; Luc. X). Et animam meam pono. Hoc est probatio dilectionis ad Patrem, et ad oves. Sic et Petrus tertio confitens amorem, jubetur pascere oves, et pro eis mori. Pro ovibus. Pro ovibus domus Israel quae perierant, ad quas corporaliter 397Dmissus, a quibus non sunt dissimiles illae quae sunt de gentibus, unde dicit:

VERS. 16.-- Oportet me. Quamvis per suos, quia in illis ipse. Et vocem meam. Non dicit meorum, quia in ipsis ille loquitur.

VERS. 17.-- Quia ego, etc. (AUG.) Verbum ex quo suscepit hominem, id est carnem et animam, etc., usque ad non potestate sua, sed potestate inhabitantis carnem Deitatis, scilicet.

VERS. 22.-- Facta sunt autem encaenia, etc. Caenon, id est novum. Inde encaenia, id est initiare. Encaenia festa dedicationis templi, quae facta est a Juda Machabaeo tempore hiemis. Hiems autem hic commemoratur, ut significet Judaeos frigidos, qui 398Anon accedunt ad ignem, id est, non credunt in Christum. Salomon autem facto templo in autumno illud dedicavit (III Reg. VIII). Zorobabel, et Jesus sacerdos post captivitatem reaedificatum templum, in vere dedicaverunt (II Esdr. VI). Judas Machabaeus pro profanatione Antiochi in hieme (I Machab. IV).

VERS. 23.-- In porticu. Porticus quibus templum cingebatur, solent significari nomine templi.

VERS. 24.-- Circumdederunt ergo eum Judaei. Domino patienter inter opprobria veritatem praedicante excaecati non obsequuntur, sed tentant, Quousque animam nostram tollis? Calumniantur illum tollere animas qui salvat, quem purum hominem putabant, sed prae omnibus regnaturum. Si tu es Christus, dic nobis palam. Non veritatem desiderant, sed parant 398Bcalumniam, ut si dixerit se regem, contra Augustum facientem potestati tradant. Sed Dominus sic temperat responsum, ut nec illis sit locus calumniae, et fidelibus propter quos haec referuntur manifeste appareat quod ipse sit Christus, non homo tantum, ut illi putant, sed Filius Dei.

VERS. 29.-- Et nemo potest rapere de manu Patris mei. Quia ex hoc quod sum ex Patre, major sum ad protegendum, quam illi ad impugnandum.

VERS. 30.-- Ego et Pater unum sumus. His verbis non solum Judaeorum solvit quaestionem, qui ut tentarent accesserunt, sed etiam futurorum haereticorum damnat insaniam.

VERS. 31.-- Sustulerunt ergo lapides. Hoc jam non ferunt qui caetera tulerunt, sed duri ad lapides 398Ccurrunt. Sed quia Jesus non patitur nisi quod vult, adhuc loquitur.

VERS. 33.-- Facis teipsum. Intellexerunt non posse dici. Ego et Pater unum sumus, nisi ubi est aequalitas Patris et Filii, quod Arius non intellexit; Christus autem, quia vidit illos tardos non ferre splendorem veritatis, temperavit eum in his verbis: Nonne scriptum est, etc.

VERS. 34.-- Quia ego. Tribus modis dicitur Deus, natura, adoptione, ut Ego dixi: Dii falso, ut daemones et idola.

VERS. 35.-- Si illos dixit deos. Si per sermonem Dei participando fiunt homines dii, quomodo ille qui per naturam est Filius quo participant, non est Deus?

398DVERS. 38.-- Operibus credite. Fidem adhibete operibus, ut merito fidei amplius cognoscendo opera, veniatis ad fidem deitatis.

VERS. 39.-- Quia in me est Pater. Pater est in Filio, Filius in Patre, ut aequalis in eo cui aequalis est; unde et potest dicere: Ego et Pater unum sumus. Et qui videt me, videt et Patrem; et hoc est ei proprium. Munus autem est homini per participationem quod dicitur esse in Deo continente et Deus in eo, ut in vase.

VERS. 41.-- Et multi venerunt. Isti apprehendunt permanentem, non quomodo iniqui apprehendere volebant discedentem. Quia Joannes, etc. Nullum in Hierusalem fecit ut iste, et quidquid dicebat testimonium 399Ahuic perhibebat, quasi per lucernam veniamus ad diem.

CHAPTER X.

VERS. 1.-- Amen, amen I say to you. Because the Pharisees boasted that they saw, which they would be able to do if they were Christ's sheep, the Lord sets forth against their arrogance a similitude of his flock, and of the door by which one enters into the sheepfold, showing that neither wisdom, nor the observance of the law, nor a good life avails anything except 396Bthrough him: without him they are nothing in whatever Jew, or Gentile and philosopher, or Pharisee who teach men to live blessedly, or heretic under the name of Christ; nor yet in Christ preaching, but all of them without him snatch away and kill. By the door. That is, through me, as he afterwards expounds. This gate is low: he who wishes to enter through it must humble himself, lest, if he stands upright, he strike against the low gate. A thief. Because he calls his own what belongs to another, that is, he makes God's sheep his own. A robber, because what he has stolen he kills. But all who hold and teach the truth in Christ the shepherd are one shepherd.

VERS. 2.-- Is the shepherd of the sheep. Christ is properly the shepherd according to the office of feeding the sheep, as he himself 396Cafterwards expounds, who through himself as the door enters into the sheepfold of the sheep. But because others are sharers with him in the pastoral office, the things which are properly said of him are fitting to them also in their own manner. The door is that by which one enters; Christ therefore, the shepherd, is himself the door, and whoever is a shepherd under him enters through that same door into the hearts of the sheep; because if he preaches something else, the sheep do not hear; if Christ, the sheep of Christ acknowledge this voice.

VERS. 3.-- To him the porter openeth. The porter, that is, Christ who opens himself, or Scripture which leads to Christ, or the Holy Spirit who teaches all truth.

And the sheep, etc. (AUG.) Some are true sheep, because the Lord knoweth, etc., as far as this no one who is his own despises, no stranger hears.

396DVERS. 6.-- But they understood not. The predestined sheep for a time do not know themselves, nor do they hear the voice of the shepherd, but at the end they persevere; on the contrary the wolves for a time hear, but at the end they persevere.

VERS. 8.-- All, etc. He is a thief who enters by simony or in some other way against Christ, and after he has entered kills by his bad example, and so is openly a robber. Nor is such a one to be called a hireling, therefore when the wolf comes he does not resist, but yields for a wage. If he does not resist the wolf tearing them by violence, he receives this wage, that he is not torn asunder along with the sheep; but afterwards, by living badly, he corrupts the sheep, and having now slipped back into the other party he becomes a thief or a robber, losing the name of hireling.

397AVERS. 9.-- If any man. Here he plainly shows that not only the shepherd but the sheep also enter through the door. And he shall go in. Within, by thinking well; he shall go out, outwardly, by working well for the example of others. Or he shall go in into the Church, that here he may live by faith. Whence he says afterwards: That they may have life. And he shall go out of this life, that he may live for ever. Whence afterwards, and may have life more abundantly, and going in and going out shall find pastures.

VERS. 10.-- But to steal, etc. He would steal, by usurping to himself what is another's; kill, by drawing them back from the faith; destroy, unto eternal damnation.

VERS. 12.-- But the hireling, etc. The hireling is he who seeks the things that are his own, not the things that are Christ's; who serves God not for God's sake, but for some wage; who is blameworthy, and is not a son, but is nevertheless 397Bnecessary and to be borne with. But the thief is always to be guarded against. Yet the prelates who are sons are shepherds also, and nevertheless there is one shepherd, because they are all members of him whose the sheep properly are. And the wolf snatcheth. The wolf is either the violent man who lays waste bodily, or the devil who scatters spiritually; and he who seeks not the souls of the sheep but his own things does not go to meet him by resisting or by rebuking evils, which is to flee, because fear is the flight of the mind: he flees lest he lose what he loves; whence, giving the cause of his flight, he adds: Because he is a hireling, and loves not the sheep, but his own things. But shepherds are not said to flee if they give way to persecution and preserve themselves for the profit of the sheep, to whom, though absent in body, they are present in spirit.

VERS. 14.-- I am the shepherd. After the fault of the counterfeit shepherd, 397Che again sets forth the form of the true one. I am the good shepherd, and I lay down my life for the sheep. [Vulg. Jo 10:11 reads Bonus pastor animam suam dat pro ovibus suis, "the good shepherd giveth his life for his sheep"; the second clause here follows Jo 10:15] Which he alone did not do, save that all his own and he himself are one, because he could do this without them, they not without him, without whom they can do nothing.

VERS. 15.-- And I. The Son through himself knows the Father, we through him, as elsewhere: No man hath seen God at any time, unless the Only-begotten who is in the bosom of the Father, he shall have declared him (Matth. XI). [Vulg. Jo 1:18 unigenitus Filius … ipse enarravit, "the only-begotten Son … he hath declared him"] Likewise: no man knoweth the Son but the Father, neither doth anyone know the Father but the Son, and he to whom the Son will reveal him (Matth. XI; Luc. X). And I lay down my life. This is the proof of love toward the Father and toward the sheep. So too Peter, confessing his love a third time, is bidden to feed the sheep and to die for them. For the sheep. For the sheep of the house of Israel which had perished, to whom he was bodily 397Dsent, from whom those which are of the Gentiles are not unlike; whence he says:

VERS. 16.-- I must. Although through his own, because in them he himself is. And my voice. He does not say "of mine," because in them he himself speaks.

VERS. 17.-- Because I, etc. (AUG.) The Word, from which he took manhood, that is, flesh and soul, etc., as far as not by its own power, but by the power of the Godhead inhabiting the flesh, namely.

VERS. 22.-- And it was the feast of the dedication, etc. Caenon, that is, new. Hence encaenia, that is, to inaugurate. The encaenia is the feast of the dedication of the temple, which was made by Judas Maccabaeus in the time of winter. And winter is mentioned here that it may signify the Jews as cold, who 398Ado not come to the fire, that is, do not believe in Christ. Solomon, when the temple was built, dedicated it in autumn (III Reg. VIII). Zorobabel and Jesus the priest dedicated the rebuilt temple after the captivity, in spring (II Esdr. VI). Judas Maccabaeus, for the profanation of Antiochus, in winter (I Machab. IV).

VERS. 23.-- In the porch. The porches with which the temple was girded are wont to be signified by the name of the temple.

VERS. 24.-- The Jews therefore came round about him. While the Lord patiently preached the truth amid reproaches, the blinded do not obey but tempt him: How long dost thou take away our soul? They falsely charge with taking away souls him who saves them; whom they thought a mere man, but one who would reign before all. If thou be the Christ, tell us plainly. They do not desire the truth, but prepare 398Ba calumny, that if he should say he was a king, they might deliver him to the power as one acting against Augustus. But the Lord so tempers his answer that there is no place of calumny for them, and that to the faithful, for whose sake these things are recounted, it may appear plainly that he himself is the Christ, not a man only, as they think, but the Son of God.

VERS. 29.-- And no man can snatch them out of my Father's hand. Because from this, that I am of the Father, I am greater for protecting than they for assailing.

VERS. 30.-- I and the Father are one. By these words he not only resolves the question of the Jews, who came in order to tempt him, but also condemns the madness of the heretics to come.

VERS. 31.-- They took up stones therefore. This they no longer bear who bore the rest, but being hard they run 398Cto stones. But because Jesus suffers nothing save what he wills, he still speaks.

VERS. 33.-- Makest thyself. They understood that it could not be said, I and the Father are one, except where there is an equality of Father and Son, which Arius did not understand; but Christ, because he saw them slow to bear the splendour of the truth, tempered it in these words: Is it not written, etc.

VERS. 34.-- That I. In three ways is one called God: by nature; by adoption, as I have said: Gods; falsely, as demons and idols.

VERS. 35.-- If he called them gods. If by partaking of the word of God men are made gods, how is he who by nature is the Son, in whom they partake, not God?

398DVERS. 38.-- Believe the works. Give faith to the works, that by the merit of faith, knowing the works more fully, you may come to faith in the Godhead.

VERS. 39.-- That the Father is in me. The Father is in the Son, the Son in the Father, as an equal in him to whom he is equal; whence he can also say: I and the Father are one. And he that seeth me seeth the Father also; and this is proper to him. But it is by participation a gift to man that he is said to be in God who contains him, and God in him, as in a vessel.

VERS. 41.-- And many came. These lay hold of him abiding, not as the wicked wished to lay hold of him departing. Because John, etc. He did none in Jerusalem such as this man did, and whatever he said bore witness to him, so that as by a lamp we might come to 399Athe day.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 1.-- Erat autem. Dum moratur trans Jordanem, infirmatur Lazarus, et mittunt ad eum sorores illuc. Istae sorores Lazari figura sunt Judaeorum in aeterna beatitudine jam receptorum, qui pro fratre sanando, id est pro reliquis in tenebris ignorantiae adhuc errantibus, Domino supplicant ex notissima actione. Languens Lazarus. Lazarus figura est Judaeorum qui infirmi erant in fide Christi: Martha et Maria significant doctrinam bonorum Judaeorum.

VERS. 3.-- Ecce quem amas, etc. Lazarus, per quem peccator significatur, qui a Domino amatur, quia non venit vocare justos, sed peccatores. Sorores, 399Bpeccatoris sunt bonae cogitationes, quae pro peccatis dissolvendis orant.

VERS. 4.-- Pro gloria Dei, etc. Id est, ut glorificetur Filius Dei. Ecce se Deum dicit, contra illos qui dicunt Filium Dei non esse Deum.

VERS. 5.-- Diligebat. Diligebat languentium Salvator et mortuorum suscitator Lazarum, quia languens et mortuus.

VERS. 6.-- Duobus diebus. Ut quatriduum impleretur. Prima enim die quo nuntiatum est ei, mortuus est languidus trans Jordanem; per duos dies mansit, ecce tres: quarto autem die venit.

VERS. 7.-- Eamus in Judaeam. Mystice: Significat fidem redituram ad Judaeos in fine per Eliam et Enoch.

VERS. 8.-- Dicunt ei discipuli. Territi, volentes 399Cconsilium dare magistro ne moreretur, sicut et alibi Petrus: Absit a te, Domine, non fiet tibi istud. Sed sicut Petrus laudatur de alieno: Beatus es, Simon Barjona, quia Pater revelavit tibi, culpatur de suo. Redi post me, Satana, quia sapis non quae Dei, sed quae hominis sunt. Ita et hos corripuit dicens se diem in quo debent ambulare ne offendant, et sine eo ambulantes offendant, ut modo nolentes eum mori qui pro hominibus venit mori. Illos autem dicit esse horas quae diem sequuntur, non dies eas, sed dies eas illuminat. Duodecim est sacratus numerus qui permanet plenus: dejecto enim Juda, qui non lucebat inter horas, alius subrogatur.

VERS. 9.-- Nonne duodecim sunt horae. (HIER.) Dies Christus, et aliter dies apostoli, quomodo Christus, 399Detc., usque ad ipsi intelliguntur duodecim apostoli.

VERS. 15.-- Ut credatis. Non quod modo inciperent credere, sed ut robustius crederent. Semper enim fides eorum miraculis augebatur.

VERS. 17.-- Et invenit. (AUG.) Mystice: Quatuor sunt dies mortis. Nascitur homo, etc., usque ad sed nec tales relinquit misericordia.

VERS. 22.-- Sed et nunc scio. Hoc scio, quia potes suscitare si vis, quod tuo relinquo arbitrio, non autem praesumo, neque rogo ut suscites, quae nescio an sit utilis fieri resurrectio.

VERS. 25.-- Etiam si mortuus fuerit. Tres mortuos suscitavit Jesus, unum in domo, id est in 400Acorde; secundum in porta, id est in ore; tertium in monumento, id est in opere et consuetudine peccandi. Vivet. Ut vivit Abraham, Isaac et Jacob, quorum Deus dicitur sicut vivorum. Credens etiam mortuus vivit, sicut non credens etiam vivens mortuus est.

VERS. 28.-- Silentio. Quia si audissent Judaei eam vocatam fuisse a Jesu, non sequeretur, et ita non viderent miraculum. Sed quia putaverunt eas ad sepulcrum fratris ituras, secuti sunt. Et vocat te. Causa brevitatis in verbis Marthae ostendit non prius dictum, id est quod Dominus Mariam vocaverit.

VERS. 31.-- Secuti sunt. Haec ideo evangelista narrare curavit, ut videamus quae occasio fecerit ut plures haberet testes resurrectio quatriduani.

400BVERS. 33.-- Infremuit spiritu. Nota potestatem: Ipse fremuit et turbavit se, quem alius turbare non potuit, sicut omnia hujusmodi suscepit, quia voluit. Significat autem quantum homo turbari debeat, pressus tanta mole peccati. Quando autem peccator computans quae bona Dei accepit, quae mala reddidit, fremit in spiritu et turbatur: Christus fremit in eo et turbatur, quia fides de Christo, Christus est in corde. Fremit vero fides in homine dum de peccatis increpat se.

VERS. 34.-- Ubi posuistis eum? Qui absens scivit mortuum, scit et ubi sit sepultus, sed sic dicens, se quasi nescire perditum hominem significat; unde dicitur: Non novi vos (Matth. VII). Et: Ubi est Adam? (Gen. III.)

400CVERS. 38.-- Jesus ergo, etc. Difficultatem ostendit vivificationis talis peccatoris. Erat autem spelunca. Moraliter: Unusquisque peccator mole peccatorum pressus et sua lege gravatus, et consuetudine sua mala jam fetens, alios exemplo suo malo aperte corrumpit: sorores, cogitationes aliquae, quia quandoque cogitans se male fecisse, dolet.

VERS. 39.-- Domine, jam fetet. Timebat remoto lapide potius fetorem excitari, quam fratrem resuscitari.

VERS. 42.-- Sed propter populum, etc. His verbis se gloriam Patris quaerere, sicut in aliis operibus ostendit. Nam quia secundum hominem minor erat Patre, ab eo petit suscitationem Lazari, et ideo se exauditum dicit, et hoc propter circumstantes, ut sic 400Dcrederent Dei Filium.

VERS. 43.-- Voce magna. Fremuit, lacrymavit, clamavit, quia difficile surgit quem moles malae consuetudinis premit, sed tamen post magnam vocem surgit, id est, per magnam Dei gratiam vivificatur dum poenitet.

VERS. 44.-- Et statim prodiit. Dum peccator contemnit, sepultus jacet; quando poenitet, surgit; quando confitetur, prodit, quasi de occultis exeundo manifestus. Solvite eum. Christus per se vivificat intus, ministris tribuit potestatem solvendi.

VERS. 48.-- Credent in eum. Et venient Romani. Et quia sentiebant doctrinam ejus esse contra templum et paternas leges, timebant ne si omnes crederent 401Ain Christum, nemo remaneret qui contra Romanos civitatem Dei et templum defenderet. Et venient Romam. De temporalibus timent, non de aeternis, et utraque perdiderunt. Locus est Hierusalem quem perdiderunt, gentem suam perdiderunt ubique dispersi.

VERS. 51.-- Hoc a semetipso. (AUG.) Nota etiam malos per spiritum prophetiae praedicere futura, etc., usque ad quod non licet nisi summo sacerdoti, ut de Zacharia legitur.

VERS. 52.-- Pro gente. Judaeorum, scilicet in qua erant oves, hoc tantum Caiphas. Sed quia noverat evangelista esse alias oves, addidit: Sed ut filios Dei qui erant dispersi congregare. Filii praedestinatione, sicut et oves in Israel, nondum enim erant, quia 401Bnondum crediderant.

VERS. 55.-- Proximum autem. Imminente Pascha figurali in quo jam verus agnus immolandus est, consilium erat inter Judaeos de eo occidendo, et ipse appropinquat passionis loco.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 1.-- Now there was. While he tarries beyond the Jordan, Lazarus falls sick, and the sisters send to him there. These sisters of Lazarus are a figure of the Jews already received into eternal blessedness, who for the healing of their brother, that is, for the rest still wandering in the darkness of ignorance, beseech the Lord out of their most familiar manner of acting. Lazarus was sick. Lazarus is a figure of the Jews who were weak in the faith of Christ: Martha and Mary signify the teaching of the good Jews.

VERS. 3.-- Behold, he whom thou lovest, etc. Lazarus, by whom the sinner is signified, who is loved by the Lord, because he came not to call the just but sinners. The sisters 399Bare the sinner's good thoughts, which pray for the loosing of his sins.

VERS. 4.-- For the glory of God, etc. That is, that the Son of God may be glorified. Behold, he calls himself God, against those who say that the Son of God is not God.

VERS. 5.-- He loved. The Saviour of the sick and the raiser of the dead loved Lazarus, because he was sick and dead.

VERS. 6.-- Two days. That the four days might be fulfilled. For on the first day, on which it was announced to him, the sick man died beyond the Jordan; for two days he remained: behold, three; but on the fourth day he came.

VERS. 7.-- Let us go into Judea. Mystically: it signifies that faith is to return to the Jews at the end through Elias and Enoch.

VERS. 8.-- The disciples say to him. Terrified, wishing 399Cto give counsel to their master that he should not die, as elsewhere Peter also: Far be it from thee, Lord, this shall not be unto thee. But as Peter is praised for what is another's: Blessed art thou, Simon Bar-Jona, because the Father hath revealed it to thee, he is blamed for what is his own: Go back behind me, Satan, because thou savourest not the things that are of God, but the things that are of man. [Vulg. Mt 16:23 Vade post me Satana, "Go behind me," and it adds scandalum es mihi, "thou art a scandal unto me"] So too he rebuked these, saying that he is the day in which they ought to walk lest they stumble, and that walking without him they stumble, as now unwilling that he should die who came to die for men. But he says that they are the hours which follow the day, not days, but the day enlightens them. Twelve is a consecrated number which remains complete: for when Judas was cast down, who did not shine among the hours, another is substituted.

VERS. 9.-- Are there not twelve hours. (HIER.) Christ is the day, and in another manner the apostles are the day, as Christ is, 399Detc., as far as they are understood to be the twelve apostles.

VERS. 15.-- That you may believe. Not that they should only now begin to believe, but that they might believe more robustly. For their faith was always increased by miracles.

VERS. 17.-- And he found. (AUG.) Mystically: there are four days of death. A man is born, etc., as far as but mercy does not leave behind even such as these.

VERS. 22.-- But now also I know. This I know, that thou canst raise him if thou wilt; which I leave to thy judgment. But I do not presume, nor do I ask that thou raise him, since I know not whether the resurrection be useful to be done.

VERS. 25.-- Yea, though he be dead. Jesus raised three dead men: one in the house, that is, in 400Athe heart; a second at the gate, that is, in the mouth; a third in the tomb, that is, in the deed and the habit of sinning. He shall live. As Abraham, Isaac and Jacob live, whose God he is called as of the living. The believer, though dead, lives, as the unbeliever, though living, is dead.

VERS. 28.-- Secretly. Because if the Jews had heard that she had been called by Jesus, she would not follow, and so they would not see the miracle. But because they thought that they were going to their brother's tomb, they followed. And calleth thee. By reason of brevity in Martha's words he shows what was not said before, that is, that the Lord had called Mary.

VERS. 31.-- They followed. The evangelist took care to relate this, that we might see what occasion brought it about that the resurrection of the man four days dead should have more witnesses.

400BVERS. 33.-- He groaned in the spirit. Note the power: he himself groaned and troubled himself, whom another could not trouble, as he took upon himself all things of this kind because he willed it. And it signifies how greatly a man ought to be troubled when pressed by so great a mass of sin. But when the sinner, reckoning what good things of God he has received and what evils he has rendered, groans in spirit and is troubled, Christ groans in him and is troubled, because faith concerning Christ is Christ in the heart. But faith groans in a man while he rebukes himself for his sins.

VERS. 34.-- Where have you laid him? He who though absent knew that he was dead knows also where he is buried; but speaking thus, he signifies that he as it were does not know the lost man; whence it is said: I know you not (Matth. VII). And: Where is Adam? (Gen. III.)

400CVERS. 38.-- Jesus therefore, etc. He shows the difficulty of the quickening of such a sinner. Now it was a cave. Morally: every sinner pressed by the mass of his sins and weighed down by his own law, and now stinking by his own evil habit, openly corrupts others by his bad example: the sisters are certain thoughts, because sometimes, thinking that he has done ill, he grieves.

VERS. 39.-- Lord, by this time he stinketh. She feared that, the stone being taken away, the stench would rather be stirred up than her brother raised.

VERS. 42.-- But for the people, etc. By these words he shows that he seeks the glory of the Father, as in his other works. For because according to his manhood he was less than the Father, he asks of him the raising of Lazarus, and therefore says that he was heard, and this for the sake of the bystanders, that they might so 400Dbelieve him the Son of God.

VERS. 43.-- With a loud voice. He groaned, he wept, he cried out, because he rises with difficulty whom the mass of an evil habit presses; but nevertheless after the great voice he rises, that is, by the great grace of God he is quickened while he repents.

VERS. 44.-- And forthwith he came forth. While the sinner despises, he lies buried; when he repents, he rises; when he confesses, he comes forth, as though going out of hiding into the open. Loose him. Christ by himself quickens within; to his ministers he grants the power of loosing.

VERS. 48.-- They will believe in him. And the Romans will come. And because they perceived that his teaching was against the temple and the laws of the fathers, they feared lest, if all should believe 401Ain Christ, none would remain who would defend the city of God and the temple against the Romans. And they will come to Rome. They fear for temporal things, not for eternal, and they lost both. Jerusalem is the place which they lost; they lost their own nation, scattered everywhere.

VERS. 51.-- This of himself. (AUG.) Note that even the wicked foretell things to come by the spirit of prophecy, etc., as far as which is not lawful save to the high priest, as is read of Zacharias.

VERS. 52.-- For the nation. Of the Jews, namely, in which there were sheep: this only did Caiphas [say]. But because the evangelist knew that there were other sheep, he added: But that he should gather together the children of God that were dispersed. Children by predestination, as also the sheep in Israel; for they were not yet, because 401Bthey had not yet believed.

VERS. 55.-- And it was nigh. The figural Pasch being at hand, in which the true Lamb is now to be immolated, there was counsel among the Jews concerning the killing of him, and he himself draws near to the place of the passion.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- Venit in Bethaniam. Ideo prius Bethaniam venit, ut resuscitatio Lazari altius memoriae omnium traderetur, et inexcusabiles Judaei confunderentur, tractantes de morte tanti suscitatoris.

VERS. 3.-- Maria ergo. Pretiosum unguentum fit de nardo maxime de spicis, hic autem dicitur pistici a loco unde erat. Et interpretatur fidelis, non alia admixtione corrupti. Dixit ergo unus. Judas etsi malus 401Ctamen inter apostolos numeratur, ut doceamur malos tolerare, ne corpus Christi dividamus, et malos bonis non obesse.

VERS. 6.-- Non quia, etc. Quantum ad suam intentionem ad eum non pertinebat de egenis, quia non curabat de ipsis, non quia commissum esset ei dispensare pauperibus.

Loculos habens, etc. Cui ministrant angeli, loculos habet in sumptus pauperum, condescendens infirmis.

VERS. 7.-- Sinite illam. Novit furem, sed non prodit, docens tolerare malos. Tantum obsequium Mariae commendat, ostendens quod esset moriturus, et ad sepeliendum aromatibus ungendus. Ideo Mariae cui non licuit mortuum ungere, datum est viventem officio funeris donare, unde secundum Marcum dicitur, 401Dquod habuit, id est potuit, hoc fecit, praevenit ungere corpus meum in sepulturam.

VERS. 8.-- Me autem non semper habetis. Corporali praesentia. Vel non habebitis, tu Juda et qui sunt hujusmodi, quia si mali jam communicant sacramentis, postea tamen non habent Dominum ut boni.

VERS. 9.-- Et venerunt. Curiositas hos, non charitas adduxit. Nos econtra, si cognoscimus Jesum obedientis animae domum in qua habitet invenisse, veniamus illuc contemplatione, non propter hominem tantum quem in anima suscitatum videmus, sed ut bonam vitam imitemur, et per hoc ad visionem Jesu perveniamus.

VERS. 10.-- Cogitaverunt. Quia non poterant occultare 402Avel negare miraculum, stulte volunt occidere, quasi non possit suscitare occisum qui suscitavit defunctum.

VERS. 12.-- In crastinum. Ecce oves ex Israel veniunt ad pastorem. Rami sunt laudes significantes victoriam superaturi diabolum et mortem.

VERS. 13.-- Clamabant. (AUG.) Osanna vox obsecrantis magis indicans affectum quam rem aliquam significans, sicut interjectiones.

Rex Israel. Rex non terreno more, quod parum est, sed quia regit, et ad mentes, et ad regna coelorum ducit, et est miserationis non indignationis.

VERS. 15.-- Noli timere, filia. In reprobo populo erat filia Sion, id est, Jerusalem, scilicet oves quae devote occurrunt venienti, quibus divinitus dicitur: 402BAgnoscite quem laudatis, et ne timeatis cum patitur qui est vestra redemptio. Pullus in quo nemo sederat, populus est gentium qui legem Dei non acceperat.

VERS. 19.-- Ecce totus. Per passionem et resurrectionem totum mundum redemit, et secutus est eum. Et quid mirum si mundus post eum vadit per quem factus est mundus?

VERS. 21.-- Hi ergo accesserunt. Ostendit gentes per praedicationem apostolorum conversas.

VERS. 23.-- Jesus autem. De Judaeis erant, qui clamabant: Benedictus qui venit in nomine Domini; modo gentiles quaerunt videre, ecce duo parietes concurrentes. Dicit itaque angularis lapis. Venit hora ut clarificetur. Haec non est clarificatio de istis volentibus 402Cvidere, sed occasione horum annuntiat venturam plenitudinem gentium eo in coelis glorificato. Sed hanc altitudinem glorificationis praecedit humilitas passionis, unde: Amen, amen dico vobis, etc. Ubi se dicit granum mortificandum et multiplicandum, ad quam passionem jam exhortatus subdit: Qui amat animam suam, etc.

Nisi granum frumenti. (AUG.) Ipse est frumentum quo vivere debet fidelis anima. Granum, de quo spiritualis seges debet surgere. Cum sint multa grana seminum, nulli se comparat nisi frumento, unde mos coepit Ecclesiae de hoc solo grano confici corpus Domini.

VERS. 25.-- Qui amat. (AUG.) Qui amat in hac vita, perdit in futuro quod magis congruit, unde 402Dsubdit: Qui odit animam, etc. Non amat, sed odit animam in hoc mundo, qui diligendo Deum, potius quam in eum peccet eligit occidi.

VERS. 26.-- Si quis mihi ministrat. Pretium operis quo illi ministratur est esse cum illo, sine quo nusquam bene est esse, et cum quo nusquam male est esse, unde et apertius subdit, si quis mihi ministrat.

VERS. 27.-- Nunc anima mea turbata est. Et quid dicam? Praedicta passione et ad eam supposita exhortatione quasi data voce fortitudinis suae subdit affectum infirmitatis nostrae quam vere suscepit, sed ex voluntate pro nobis ne de hac infirmitate desperemus: in qua etiam docet quid dicere debeamus, et quo 403Aconfugere. Quasi dicat: Conturbatur anima mea, quid dicam? quo confugiam? quem invocem? in quem sperem? in Patrem qui salvat, cui voluntatem meam propono, quia propterea ut haec patiar veni in hanc horam, unde et clarificatio mihi sic fit et in membris meis.

VERS. 28.-- Pater, clarifica. Salvationem quaero, sed passionem non recuso, propter quam passionem clarifica me quod est gloria tui nominis.

VERS. 30.-- Non propter me vox. Ostendit hanc vocem non sibi indicasse quod sciebat, sed eis quibus indicari oportebat.

VERS. 31.-- Nunc judicium est. Discretionis scilicet, quod est ante ultimum damnationis, quia nunc per passionem jure diaboli destructo multi liberabuntur, 403Bunde exponendo subjungit: Patior, turbor, et per haec, etc. Ejicietur. A redemptis expelletur, praedicantibus apostolis plene expelletur qui olim a paucis etsi extra tentetur, non laedit intus.

VERS. 34.-- Et quomodo tu, etc. Quomodo in cruce morietur? Hoc enim eum dixisse putabant quod facere cogitabant. Filium hominis. Quia hoc memoriter tenebant, quod saepe dixerat se Filium hominis, non quod hic dixisset, si exaltatus fuerit Filius hominis. Quis est iste Filius hominis? Si enim Christus est, manet in aeternum. Si manet in aeternum, quomodo exaltabitur a terra, id est, morietur in cruce?

VERS. 35.-- Ambulate. Id est, totum intelligite, et moriturum Christum ut redimat victurum in aeternum 403Cin coelo quo perducat. Vel dum Christum vobiscum habetis ne tenebrae ignorantiae et mortis perpetuae vos comprehendant. Nescit quo vadat. Qui non credit mortem Christi, nescit quo vadit, sed offendit in petram scandali, quod est Christus, Judaeis contemnentibus et irridentibus mortuum.

VERS. 36.-- Dum lucem habetis. Id est, dum aliquid veri intelligitis de aeternitate Christi, credite in veritatem addendo de humilitate, ut renascamini in veritate. Ab eis. Non ab his qui credere incoeperant, sed ab his qui invidebant. Quod abscondit se, nostrae infirmitati consuluit, non suae potestati derogavit.

VERS. 38.-- Ut sermo Isaiae impleretur. Quem omnibus modis impleri necesse est, non tamen necessitate faciendum, sed voluntate, praescivit enim 403DDeus et praedixit, non credituros et peccaturos, non tamen coegit ad hoc, sed ut fit sic praescivit et praedixit.

VERS. 39.-- Non poterant. (AUG.) Non quod non possunt homines mutari in melius, sed quandiu tales non possunt credere.

VERS. 43.-- Gloriam Dei. Ideo crux in fronte credentium, ubi est sedes verecundiae, figitur, ut de nomine Christi nullus erubescat, et magis gloriam Dei quam hominum quaerat.

VERS. 44.-- Jesus. Aliis non credentibus, aliis vero credentibus, vel palam cum palmis et laudibus, vel in occulto jam appropinquante sua passione, clamat Jesus qui latebat quandiu passio non erat complenda. 404AQui credit in me. Quia homo apparebat, et Deus in homine latebat, ne putarent eum hoc tantum esse quod videbant, vult se credi talem et tantum qualis et quantus pater est.

VERS. 45.-- Et qui me videt, videt et eum qui, etc. Usque adeo nihil distat inter me et eum, ut qui videt me videat eum. Haec visio non carnalis, sed intellectualis est mundo corde. Qui videt me. Non ait non videt me, sed eum qui me misit, sicut dixit, qui credit in me, non credit in me, sed in eum qui misit me, quia illud dixit, ne sicut videbatur crederetur tantum Filius hominis; hoc autem dixit, ut crederetur aequalis Patri. Neutrum horum diceret alius missus, id est apostolus, quia tantum servus. Potest autem dicere alia Filio communia, ut qui audit vel 404Brecipit me audit eum et recipit qui me misit, quia Dominus honoratur in servo et Pater in Filio.

VERS. 46.-- Ego lux. De alio non dicitur: Lux venit in mundum, nisi de eo qui illuminat, caeteri ut illuminati sunt lux, quia vita eorum lucet hominibus.

Sermo quem locutus sum. Manifeste dicit se judicaturum in novissimo die, quia ipse est sermo Patris qui seipsum locutus est, seipsum annuntiavit.

VERS. 49.-- Mandatum. Non dedit Pater Filio mandatum per angelum vel aliam creaturam. Nec mandatum quod aliquando nescisset: sed dum verbum et sapientiam in qua sunt omnia mandata, genuit, dedit: sicut Filio qui est vita, eo dedit vitam quo genuit vitam, quia gignendo vitam, dedit esse 404Cvitam, quae sine vitio fuit.

VERS. 50.-- Et scio. Cum dicat mandatum esse vitam aeternam, et ipse sit vita aeterna, aperte dicit se mandatum Dei. Quae ergo ego loquor sicut dixit mihi Pater, sic loquor. Intelligentibus mentibus, ut est nata veritas ex Pater, sed sic loquitur veritas intus sine sono, sicut dixit ei Pater ut crederent homines quod nondum possunt intelligere, ex ore carnis verba sonuerunt, quae prolata et litteris signata, nos ad ipsam veritatem aliquatenus erigunt.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- He came into Bethania. Therefore he came first to Bethania, that the raising of Lazarus might be handed down more deeply to the memory of all, and that the Jews might be confounded as inexcusable, plotting the death of so great a raiser of the dead.

VERS. 3.-- Mary therefore. Precious ointment is made of nard, chiefly of the spikes; but here it is called pistic from the place whence it came. And it is interpreted "faithful," not corrupted by any other admixture. Then said one. Judas, although evil, 401Cis nevertheless numbered among the apostles, that we may be taught to bear with the evil, lest we divide the body of Christ, and that the evil do not harm the good.

VERS. 6.-- Not because, etc. As far as his own intention went, it did not pertain to him concerning the needy, because he did not care for them; not because it had been committed to him to dispense to the poor.

Having the purse, etc. He to whom the angels minister has purses for the expenses of the poor, condescending to the weak.

VERS. 7.-- Let her alone. He knows the thief, but does not betray him, teaching us to bear with the evil. He commends so great a service of Mary, showing that he was to die and to be anointed with spices for burial. Therefore to Mary, to whom it was not permitted to anoint him dead, it was given to bestow the office of a funeral upon him living; whence according to Mark it is said 401Dthat what she had, that is, what she could, this she did, she is come beforehand to anoint my body for the burial.

VERS. 8.-- But me you have not always. By bodily presence. Or you shall not have me, thou Judas and those who are of this sort, because if the evil now communicate in the sacraments, yet afterwards they do not have the Lord as the good do.

VERS. 9.-- And they came. Curiosity brought these, not charity. We on the contrary, if we know that Jesus has found the house of an obedient soul in which to dwell, let us come thither by contemplation, not only for the sake of the man whom we see raised in the soul, but that we may imitate the good life, and through this come to the vision of Jesus.

VERS. 10.-- They thought. Because they could not hide 402Aor deny the miracle, they foolishly wish to kill him, as though he who raised the dead man could not raise the slain.

VERS. 12.-- On the next day. Behold, the sheep out of Israel come to the shepherd. The branches are praises signifying the victory of him who was to overcome the devil and death.

VERS. 13.-- They cried out. (AUG.) Hosanna is the voice of one beseeching, indicating an affection rather than signifying any thing, like the interjections.

King of Israel. King, not after an earthly manner, which is a small thing, but because he rules, and leads both unto minds and unto the kingdoms of heaven, and is of mercy, not of indignation.

VERS. 15.-- Fear not, daughter. In the reprobate people there was a daughter of Sion, that is, Jerusalem, namely the sheep who devoutly run to meet him as he comes, to whom it is said by God: 402BAcknowledge him whom you praise, and fear not when he suffers who is your redemption. The colt on which no man had sat is the people of the Gentiles, which had not received the law of God.

VERS. 19.-- Behold, the whole. Through his passion and resurrection he redeemed the whole world, and it followed him. And what wonder if the world goes after him through whom the world was made?

VERS. 21.-- These therefore came. He shows the Gentiles converted through the preaching of the apostles.

VERS. 23.-- But Jesus. They were of the Jews who cried out: Blessed is he that cometh in the name of the Lord; now the Gentiles seek to see him: behold, two walls meeting. And so the corner stone speaks. The hour is come that should be glorified. This is not a glorification concerning those who wish 402Cto see him, but on their occasion he announces the coming fulness of the Gentiles, when he has been glorified in heaven. But this height of glorification is preceded by the lowliness of the passion; whence: Amen, amen I say to you, etc. Where he calls himself the grain which must be mortified and multiplied; to which passion, having now exhorted them, he adds: He that loveth his life, etc.

Unless the grain of wheat. (AUG.) He himself is the wheat by which the faithful soul ought to live. The grain, from which the spiritual harvest ought to rise. Though there be many grains of seeds, he compares himself to none but wheat; whence the custom began in the Church that of this grain alone the body of the Lord is confected.

VERS. 25.-- He that loveth. (AUG.) He who loves in this life loses in the life to come what is more fitting; whence 402Dhe adds: He that hateth his life, etc. He does not love but hates his life in this world who, loving God, chooses rather to be slain than to sin against him.

VERS. 26.-- If any man minister to me. The price of the work by which he is ministered to is to be with him, without whom it is nowhere good to be, and with whom it is nowhere ill to be; whence he adds more openly, if any man minister to me.

VERS. 27.-- Now my soul is troubled. And what shall I say? The passion having been foretold, and the exhortation to it set beneath it, as though the voice of his own fortitude had been given, he adds the affection of our infirmity, which he truly took upon himself, but of his will, for us, lest we despair of this infirmity: in which also he teaches what we ought to say, and whither 403Ato flee. As if he should say: My soul is troubled; what shall I say? whither shall I flee? whom shall I call upon? in whom shall I hope? In the Father who saves, to whom I set forth my will, because for this cause, that I might suffer these things, I came unto this hour; whence also my glorification comes about thus, and in my members.

VERS. 28.-- Father, glorify. I seek salvation, but I do not refuse the passion; for which passion glorify me, which is the glory of thy name.

VERS. 30.-- This voice came not because of me. He shows that this voice did not declare to him what he knew, but to those to whom it had to be declared.

VERS. 31.-- Now is the judgment. Of discernment, namely, which is before the last judgment of damnation, because now, through the passion, the right of the devil being destroyed, many shall be set free; 403Bwhence, expounding it, he subjoins: I suffer, I am troubled, and through these things, etc. He shall be cast out. He shall be driven out from the redeemed; when the apostles preach he shall be fully driven out, who of old by few, even though a man be tempted without, does not hurt within.

VERS. 34.-- And how sayest thou, etc. How shall he die upon the cross? For they thought that he had said this which they were plotting to do. The Son of man. Because they held this in memory, that he had often said that he was the Son of man, not that he had said it here, if the Son of man shall be lifted up. Who is this Son of man? For if he is Christ, he abides for ever. If he abides for ever, how shall he be lifted up from the earth, that is, die upon the cross?

VERS. 35.-- Walk. That is, understand the whole: both that Christ was to die that he might redeem, and that he was to live for ever 403Cin heaven, whither he may lead you. Or, while you have Christ with you, lest the darkness of ignorance and of everlasting death seize upon you. He knoweth not whither he goeth. He who does not believe the death of Christ knows not whither he goes, but stumbles upon the rock of scandal, which is Christ, while the Jews despise and mock him as dead.

VERS. 36.-- Whilst you have the light. That is, while you understand something of the truth concerning the eternity of Christ, believe in the truth, adding what belongs to his lowliness, that you may be born again in the truth. From them. Not from those who had begun to believe, but from those who envied him. That he hid himself, he took counsel for our infirmity; he did not derogate from his own power.

VERS. 38.-- That the saying of Isaias might be fulfilled. Which must in all ways be fulfilled, yet not by a necessity of doing but by will; for God foreknew 403Dand foretold that they would not believe and would sin, yet he did not compel them to this, but foreknew and foretold that it would so come to pass.

VERS. 39.-- They could not. (AUG.) Not that men cannot be changed for the better, but that so long as they are such they cannot believe.

VERS. 43.-- The glory of God. Therefore the cross is fixed upon the forehead of believers, where the seat of shame is, that none may blush at the name of Christ, and may seek the glory of God rather than that of men.

VERS. 44.-- Jesus. Some not believing and others believing, whether openly with palms and praises, or in secret now that his passion drew near, Jesus cries out, who lay hid so long as the passion was not to be accomplished. 404AHe that believeth in me. Because he appeared as man, and God lay hid in the man, lest they should think him to be only that which they saw, he wills himself to be believed such and so great as the Father is.

VERS. 45.-- And he that seeth me seeth him also who, etc. So far is there no distance between me and him, that he who sees me sees him. This vision is not carnal but intellectual, with a clean heart. He that seeth me. He does not say, seeth not me, but him who sent me, as he said, he that believeth in me believeth not in me, but in him that sent me, because he said that, lest as he was seen he should be believed only the Son of man; but he said this, that he might be believed equal to the Father. Neither of these would another sent one say, that is, an apostle, because he is only a servant. But he can say other things common to the Son, as, he who hears or 404Breceives me hears and receives him who sent me, because the Lord is honoured in the servant, and the Father in the Son.

VERS. 46.-- I am the light. Of no other is it said: The light is come into the world, save of him who enlightens; the rest, as being enlightened, are light, because their life shines before men.

The word that I have spoken. He plainly says that he will judge on the last day, because he himself is the Word of the Father, who has spoken himself, has announced himself.

VERS. 49.-- A commandment. The Father did not give the commandment to the Son through an angel or any other creature. Nor a commandment which he had at some time not known: but while he begot the Word and the wisdom in which are all commandments, he gave it: as to the Son who is life, he gave life by that whereby he begot life, because by begetting life he gave 404Clife to be, which was without fault.

VERS. 50.-- And I know. Since he says that the commandment is life everlasting, and he himself is life everlasting, he openly says that he is the commandment of God. The things therefore that I speak, as the Father said to me, so I speak. To understanding minds, as the truth is born of the Father; but the truth so speaks within, without sound, as the Father said to him: that men might believe what they cannot yet understand, words sounded from the mouth of flesh, which, uttered and marked in letters, raise us in some measure to the truth itself.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- Ut transeat. Interpretatur nobis evangelista nomen paschae, id est transitus et significationem, quia sicut illi de Aegypto, sic Christus de 404Dmundo. Sic et nos, sanguine Agni insigniti, de vitiis ad virtutes, de mundo ad coelum. In finem dilexit. In finem, id est in Christum qui est finis consummans, id est, ut ejus dilectione transeant quo ipse transit. Vel in finem, id est in mortem, non quod morte terminetur ejus dilectio, sed in tantum dilexit quod usque ad mortem dilectio eum perduxit, in quo exemplum dedit ut omnes in Dei veritate permaneant usque in finem.

VERS. 2.-- Et coena facta. Coena significat passionem. Sicut enim post coenam non fit prandium, sic et Christus postquam semel passus est, non iterum patitur. Surgit a coena, post passionem ponit vestimenta, secundum priorem infirmitatem linteo 405Apraecinxit se, eadem carne jam immortali, mittit aquam in pelvim, id est gratiam baptismi, lavat pedes et tergit a peccatis in baptismo, accepit vestimenta, id est Judaeos in fine saeculi, quos prius excaecatos deposuit.

VERS. 4.-- Surgit a coena. Ab illa coelesti refectione, qua cum angelis reficiebatur, deponit vestimenta, gloriam aeternae beatitudinis. Et ponit vestimenta. Praetermisit altitudinem, suppositurus ejus humilitatem, ut perfecte commendetur humilitas homini cum tam altus sic se humiliaverit, ut et pedes traditoris lavaret.

VERS. 5.-- Deinde mittit. Sanguinem in terram fudit, ut vestigia credentium mundaret, quae terrenis peccatis erant sordida.

405BVERS. 8.-- Dicit ei Petrus, etc. Pro altitudine promissi mysterii nondum permittit fieri, nondum potest sustinere Deum ad pedes humiliari, pavore perterritus. Respondit. Qui non lavatur per baptismum et confessionem poenitentiae, non habet partem cum Jesu.

VERS. 9.-- Dicit ei Simon. Error Petri hic nobis profuit. Erravit enim ne nos erraremus. Erravit, cum se totum lavandum Deo obtulit, quem Deus correxit, ostendens semel baptizatum non esse baptizandum.

VERS. 10.-- Qui lotus. Ex his verbis intelligimus baptizatos apostolos, etsi non legimus ubi vel quando. Qui lotus. Baptismo lotus est mundus. Sed quia iterum pulvere terrenae habitationis inquinatur, 405Citerum opus est pedes lavare, quos lavat Dominus interpellans pro nobis, et nos ipsi orando. Ille etiam qui in otio studet veritati, dum in aliis pulsanti sponso aperire pergit, pedes inquinat, quia veritas humiliter auditur, sed sine periculo non potest praedicari, sed hos pedes Dominus discipulis lavat.

VERS. 12.-- Et cum recubuisset, iterum dixit eis: Scitis quid fecerim vobis? Vos vocatis me, etc. Sicut prius exemplo, sic etiam nunc verbo instruit eos magister veritatis. Et quod dicit magna de se non est arrogantia, sed veritas quae prodest auditoribus, sicut facit et Paulus.

(GREG.) Incauti sunt humiles qui se mendacio illaqueant dum arrogantiam vitant, quia contra veritatem se erigunt quam relinquunt. Qui enim necessitate 405Dcogente vera de se et bona loquitur, tanto magis humilitati jungitur, quanto veritati sociatur.

VERS. 18.-- Non de omnibus. Non omnes facietis hoc unde beati sitis, sed qui manducat meum panem, non ut proficiat, sed ut lateat, levabit contra me calcaneum suum, id est me calcabit, ut impleatur Scriptura quae hoc praedixit, non quod cogat, sed quia quod futurum erat non tacuit.

VERS. 19.-- Amodo dico vobis. Quasi dicat: Hucusque toleravi et tacui, sed jam noto proditorem antequam fiat quod mox faciendum est, uti cum factum fuerit credatis quia ego sum ille de quo Scriptura illa praedixit.

VERS. 20.-- Amen, amen dico vobis. Postquam 406Aexemplo suo eos docuit humiliationem et calcanei passionem, supponit quo consoletur eos honore, quod ipse et Pater in eis recipitur.

VERS. 21.-- Cum haec dixisset Jesus Nostra infirmitas in eo vere turbata est, non sua potestas, sicut et vere passa. Voluntarie autem accepit turbationem, ne desperet servus turbatus necessitate. Quod autem turbatur significat turbandos spirituales ex charitate, cum urgens causa et zizania a tritico ante messem cogitur separare.

VERS. 26.-- Et cum intinxisset panem. Panis intinctio significat fictionem Judae, qui fictus amicus venit ad coenam. Quae enim tinguntur, non mundantur, sed inficiuntur. Si autem bonum aliquid significat intinctio, eidem bono ingratum, non immerito 406Bsecuta est damnatio. Dedit Judae Simonis Scariothis, etc. Non, ut quidam putant, nunc Judas corpus Christi accepit. Sciendum est enim, quod jam omnibus Dominus distribuerat sacramentum corporis et sanguinis, inter quos et Judas fuit, deinde per buccellam intinctam, exprimitur ipse traditor, etc.

VERS. 27.-- Quod facis. Non praecepit, sed praedicit Judae malum, nobis bonum, quia hoc vult cito fieri, non tam festinando in illius poenam quam in salutem fidelium.

VERS. 29.-- Quia loculos habebat. In quibus oblata scrutabantur suorum necessitatibus et aliis indigentibus. In quo datur forma Ecclesiae servandi necessaria, quae jubetur non cogitare de crastino, ne pro terrenis serviat, vel timore inopiae justitiam 406Cdeserat.

VERS. 30.-- Erat autem. Nox congruit sacramento. Erat enim qui exivit filius tenebrarum faciens opera tenebrarum.

Nunc clarificatus est Filius hominis. (AUG.) Exeunte Juda pro quo dicebatur: Non estis mundi omnes, etc., usque ad quasi sit factum quod mox est futurum.

VERS. 33.-- Filioli. (ID.) Quia dixerat quod Deus est cito clarificaturus eum, etc., usque ad non potest hoc accipi; quia non conveniret quod sequitur.

Quo ego vado vos non potestis venire. Nunc non poterant apostoli nondum spiritu confirmati sequi Dominum, id est mori pro Domino et justitia, vel venire ad vitam immortalem ad quam ipse ibat, secuturi autem erant postea. Quomodo autem idonei 406Dpossint esse et pergere quo ille antecedit, docet.

Mandatum novum do. (AUG.) Non quod hoc non sit in antiqua lege, Diliges proximum tuum sicut teipsum, etc., usque ad quae dilectio facit mori saeculo.

VERS. 34.-- Ut diligatis invicem sicut dilexi vos. Non putetur haec praetermissa dilectio Dei quae est in dilectione proximi, in proximo enim non nisi Deus diligitur, sicut et Christus in nobis diligit Deum, et non quod hoc habeamus, sed ut haberemus sicut medicus in aegro, non morbum, sed salutem quam venit dare.

VERS. 36.-- Domine, quo, etc. Audito, quo ego vado, et intellecta via qua sequi potest, id est dilectione, 407Adicit Petrus ut sequi paratus. Et quia hunc ejus animum novit Dominus, dixit non modo eum posse, in quo non abstulit spem futuri, sed confirmavit. Sequeris autem postea.

VERS. 37.-- Animam meam. Multum praesumit qui animam pro eo ponere promittit, qui nondum pro Petro animam dedit, quasi praeire possit.

(AUG.) Voluntatem suam, sed non vires suas cognoscens, ut infirmus voluntatem suam jactat, sed inspiciebat invalitudinem medicus. Ille promittebat, ille praenoscebat, et qui nesciebat audiebat, qui praesciebat docebat.

VERS. 38.-- Non cantabit gallus, etc. Petrus ante primum galli cantum trinam negationem incipit, totam autem perficit ante secundum galli cantum.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- That he should pass. The evangelist interprets for us the name of the pasch, that is, a passing over, and its signification: because as they passed out of Egypt, so Christ out of 404Dthe world. So we also, marked with the blood of the Lamb, from vices to virtues, from the world to heaven. He loved them unto the end. Unto the end, that is, unto Christ who is the consummating end, that is, that by love of him they may pass whither he himself passes. Or unto the end, that is, unto death: not that his love is ended by death, but that he loved so far that love led him even unto death, wherein he gave an example that all should remain in the truth of God unto the end.

VERS. 2.-- And when supper was done. The supper signifies the passion. For as after supper there is no dinner, so also Christ, after he has once suffered, does not suffer again. He rises from supper; after the passion he lays aside his garments; according to his former infirmity he girded himself with a linen cloth, 405Ahis flesh being now immortal; he puts water into a basin, that is, the grace of baptism; he washes the feet and wipes them from sins in baptism; he took his garments, that is, the Jews at the end of the world, whom he had first laid aside as blinded.

VERS. 4.-- He riseth from supper. From that heavenly refreshment with which he was refreshed among the angels he lays aside his garments, the glory of eternal blessedness. And layeth aside his garments. He passed over his loftiness, being about to place his lowliness beneath it, that lowliness might be perfectly commended to man, since one so lofty humbled himself so far that he washed even the feet of the traitor.

VERS. 5.-- After that he putteth. He poured his blood upon the earth, that he might cleanse the steps of believers, which were soiled with earthly sins.

405BVERS. 8.-- Peter saith to him, etc. Because of the loftiness of the promised mystery he does not yet permit it to be done; he cannot yet endure that God be humbled at his feet, being struck through with dread. He answered. He who is not washed through baptism and the confession of penance has no part with Jesus.

VERS. 9.-- Simon saith to him. Peter's error here profited us. For he erred that we might not err. He erred when he offered his whole self to God to be washed, whom God corrected, showing that one once baptized is not to be baptized again.

VERS. 10.-- He that is washed. From these words we understand that the apostles were baptized, although we do not read where or when. He that is washed. He that is washed by baptism is clean. But because he is again soiled by the dust of his earthly dwelling, 405Cthere is again need to wash the feet, which the Lord washes by interceding for us, and we ourselves by praying. He also who in leisure studies the truth, while he goes to open to the bridegroom knocking in others, soils his feet, because the truth is heard humbly, but cannot be preached without danger; but these feet the Lord washes for his disciples.

VERS. 12.-- And when he had sat down, he said to them again: Know you what I have done to you? You call me, etc. As before by example, so now also by word the master of truth instructs them. And that he says great things of himself is not arrogance, but truth which profits the hearers, as Paul also does.

(GREG.) Incautious are the humble who ensnare themselves in a lie while they shun arrogance, because they set themselves against the truth which they forsake. For he who under compelling 405Dnecessity speaks true and good things of himself is joined so much the more to humility as he is allied to truth.

VERS. 18.-- I speak not of you all. You shall not all do that whereby you may be blessed; but he that eateth my bread, not that he may profit but that he may lie hid, shall lift up his heel against me, that is, shall tread upon me, that the Scripture may be fulfilled which foretold this, not that it compels, but because it did not keep silence concerning what was to be.

VERS. 19.-- From henceforth I tell you. As if he should say: Hitherto I have borne with him and kept silence, but now I mark the traitor before that is done which is soon to be done, so that when it has been done you may believe that I am he of whom that Scripture foretold.

VERS. 20.-- Amen, amen I say to you. After 406Ahe had by his own example taught them humiliation and the suffering of the heel, he subjoins an honour wherewith he may console them, that he himself and the Father are received in them.

VERS. 21.-- When Jesus had said these things Our infirmity was truly troubled in him, not his own power, as it also truly suffered. But he willingly took the trouble upon himself, lest the servant troubled by necessity should despair. And that he is troubled signifies that spiritual men are to be troubled out of charity, when an urgent cause is compelled to separate even the cockle from the wheat before the harvest.

VERS. 26.-- And when he had dipped the bread. The dipping of the bread signifies the feigning of Judas, who came to the supper a feigned friend. For the things that are dipped are not cleansed but stained. But if the dipping signifies something good, damnation not undeservedly 406Bfollowed one ungrateful to that same good. He gave it to Judas, son of Simon the Iscariot, etc. Not, as some think, did Judas now receive the body of Christ. For it must be known that the Lord had already distributed to all the sacrament of his body and blood, among whom Judas also was; then by the dipped morsel the traitor himself is pointed out, etc.

VERS. 27.-- That which thou dost. He did not command, but he foretells evil to Judas, good to us, because he wills this to be done quickly, not so much hastening to that man's punishment as to the salvation of the faithful.

VERS. 29.-- Because he had the purse. In which the offerings were examined for the needs of their own and of others in want. Wherein a form is given to the Church of keeping what is necessary, which is bidden not to think of the morrow, lest it serve for earthly things, or through fear of want forsake 406Cjustice.

VERS. 30.-- And it was. The night befits the sacrament. For he who went out was a son of darkness, doing the works of darkness.

Now is the Son of man glorified. (AUG.) Judas going out, for whom it was said: You are not all clean, etc., as far as as though that were already done which is soon to be.

VERS. 33.-- Little children. (ID.) Because he had said that God would quickly glorify him, etc., as far as this cannot be taken so, because what follows would not agree.

Whither I go you cannot come. The apostles, not yet confirmed in spirit, could not now follow the Lord, that is, die for the Lord and for justice, or come to the immortal life to which he himself was going; but they were to follow afterwards. And how they might 406Dbe fit and go where he goes before, he teaches.

A new commandment I give. (AUG.) Not that this is not in the old law, Thou shalt love thy neighbour as thyself, etc., as far as which love makes a man die to the world.

VERS. 34.-- That you love one another, as I have loved you. Let not that love of God be thought passed over which is in the love of the neighbour; for in the neighbour nothing is loved but God, as Christ also loves God in us; and not that we have this, but that we might have it, as a physician in a sick man loves not the disease but the health which he comes to give.

VERS. 36.-- Lord, whither, etc. Having heard "whither I go," and having understood the way by which he can follow, that is, love, 407APeter says that he is ready to follow. And because the Lord knew this mind of his, he said that he could not now, wherein he did not take away the hope of the future, but confirmed it. But thou shalt follow hereafter.

VERS. 37.-- My life. He presumes much who promises to lay down his life for him who had not yet given his life for Peter, as though he could go before.

(AUG.) Knowing his own will, but not his own strength, as a weak man he boasts of his will, but the physician looked upon his infirmity. The one promised, the other foreknew; and he who knew not was hearing, he who foreknew was teaching.

VERS. 38.-- The cock shall not crow, etc. Peter begins the threefold denial before the first cockcrow, but completes the whole of it before the second cockcrow.

CAPUT XIV.

  • 407B

VERS. 1.-- Non turbetur cor vestrum, etc. (AUG.) Ne autem mortem tanquam hominis timerent, et ideo turbarentur, etc., usque ad alia claritas lunae, alia claritas stellarum.

VERS. 2.-- In domo, etc. Domus Dei, templum Dei, regnum Dei. Regnum coelorum, sunt homines justi, in quibus sunt multae differentiae inter se. Et hae sunt mansiones ipsius domus.

(AUG.) Modo justi sunt regnum Dei et regnum coelorum, etc., usque ad quod eratis in spe, sitis in re.
