Si quo minus, etc. Id est, si non praedestinassem mansionem, quod est inconveniens quantum ad Deum qui omnia bona ante omnia tempora praedestinavit, tunc modo praepararem, id est, praedestinarem, 407Cquod iterum est inconveniens, quantum ad Deum praesentialiter aliquid praedestinare.

(ID.) Confirmat esse, quia si non essent dixisset, ibo et parabo, etc., usque ad et haec multitudo mansionum est.

VERS. 3.-- Et si abiero. (ID.) Abiit latendo, venit apparendo, sed manet regendo, ut proficiamus bene vivendo, et sic paratur locus.

(ID.) Fides quae non videt credit, nam si videret, etc., usque ad desiderium dilectionis, praeparatio est mansionis.

VERS. 4.-- Et quo ego vado. Hoc dictum est secundum quosdam qui sciebant et Filium et Patrem. Thomas vero etsi putaret se nescire, convincitur per hoc quod ipsum novit, et viam scire et vitam, 407Did est, Filium et Patrem. De istis scientibus subditur: Amodo cognoscetis eum per me cognitum, et vidistis eum in corde, dum me per omnia similem ei vidistis.

VERS. 5.-- Dicit ei Thomas, etc. Quo et qua itur, dixit eos scire qui non mentitur, sed Thomas dicit eos utrumque nescire, nesciens eos scire, sed Christus convincit eos scire, quia si eum sciebant, et ipse est via, viam sciebant, et si ipsum sciebant, et ipse est veritas et vita, tunc sciebant veritatem et vitam ad quam vadit: ipse ergo per se viam, ad se veritatem et vitam et ad Patrem, quia cum Patre unum est, vadit.

VERS. 7.-- Si cognovissetis me. Quia dixerat, nullum 408Avenire ad Patrem, nisi per se, qui est inseparabiliter idem cum Patre, ne quaererent quis est Pater, ostendit ex se noto nosci Patrem, quod nondum quidam cognoverant, quos arguit dicens: Si cognovissetis, etc. Et vidistis eum. Ut de duobus omnino similibus dicitur: Si istum vidisti, illum vidisti; viderant ergo quam simillimum Filium, sed monendi erant, ut talem etiam Patrem intelligerent, et non dissimilem.

VERS. 9.-- Quomodo tu dicis. Et cum omnino similis. Quomodo tu dicis, etc. Quasi diceret: Video quo animo dicas, non quaeris alterum similem, sed putas meliorem. A meipso. Quia non sum a meipso qui loquor: ei tribuit quod facit, de quo est ipse qui facit.

408BVERS. 12.-- Qui credit in me. Ego et Pater indifferenter operamur. Et quicunque credit in me (ne de solis apostolis hoc acciperetur) opera quae ego facio per me, ipse faciet, id est, ego facio in eo vel per eum, et etiam majora. Opera quae ego facio. Non tamen ex se, sed ex me. Non est servus major domino. Ipse enim sine eis multa, illi sine eo nihil, nisi peccatum.

Majora horum. (AUG.) Quia si ipse fimbria, Petrus umbra sanavit. Vel opus ejus, etc., usque ad quia si utrumque aequalis potentiae, hoc est majoris misericordiae.

VERS. 15.-- Si diligitis me. Quomodo monet diligere et servare mandata, ut accipiant spiritum, quem nisi habeant non possunt diligere et servare? Sed 408Cquod habebant minus occulte, accepturi erant amplius manifeste, ut eis innotesceret quod habebant.

VERS. 16.-- Et ego rogabo Patrem. (THEOPH.) Rogabo, hoc est, obsecrabo Patrem et alium Paracletum mittet vobis, etc., usque ad quod omnino superveniat eis Spiritus paracletus ejusque gratia condescendens, illis dicit: Rogabo Patrem.

VERS. 17.-- Quia non videt, etc. Non habent spirituales oculos mundi amatores quibus Spiritum sanctum videant. Nec vero carnalibus oculis possunt videre, quia Spiritus sanctus non apparuit indutus humanitate. Et in vobis. Erit in vobis, non manebit ut sit, quia prius est esse alicubi quam manere. Ne quis ergo putet sic manere, ut hospes apud alium visibiliter, sed in eis cognosci potest, non ab aliis.

408DVERS. 18.-- Non relinquam. Non ita dabitur Spiritus pro me, ut ego etiam non sim vobiscum. Orphani Graece, pupilli Latine est.

VERS. 19.-- Adhuc modicum et mundus. Sed mundus qui modo videt oculis carnis, post modicum non videbit, quia resuscitatus se eis non ostendit, sed totum tempus modicum est, quo finito auferetur impius ne videat gloriam Dei, quam justi videbunt. Quia ego vivo, et vos vivetis. Praesenti utitur quia mox futura est ejus resurrectio. Ad illos utitur futuro quorum in fine futura resurrectio.

VERS. 20.-- Et vos in me et ego in vobis. Hoc modo est, et cognoscitur quodammodo per fidem; 409Ased tunc cognoscetur per speciem, quando haec conjunctio perficietur quae modo inchoatur.

VERS. 21.-- Qui habet, etc. Non solum vos apostoli vivetis et cognoscetis, sed et quicunque habet in memoria et servat in vita. Vel, habet faciendo, servat perseverando. Vel qui habet in sermonibus servat in moribus. Vel, habet audiendo, servat faciendo. Et ego diligam eum. Ad hoc diligam, ut manifestem, quia et nunc diligit, sed non ad hoc, sed potius ut credamus et mandata servemus, ut mereamur quod post capiamus. Et manifestabo ei. Qui habet meum mandatum, ille me cognoscit, et hoc est ibi: Manifestabo ei meipsum. Sed praemittit causam mandati, quia diligit me, et qui me diligit, Pater meus diliget eum, et quia Pater, tunc ego diligam 409Beum, et inde est manifestatio.

VERS. 22.-- Dicit ei Judas, etc. Audito quod mundus modo videbit eum, sed ipsi videbunt, pro omnibus quaesiturus quaerit Judas causam hujus discretionis. Haec autem causa est secundum responsionem Domini, dilectio, per quam solam et non per aliam discernitur gens sancta a non sancta.

VERS. 23.-- Ad eum veniemus. Venit Deus ad hominem, dum homo venit ad Deum. Venit homo credendo, obediendo, intuendo, capiendo; Deus venit subveniendo, illuminando, implendo. Et mansionem, etc. Charitas facit habitare in domo in qua Pater et Filius facit mansionem, unde et dabit manifestationem de qua ille quaesierat.

VERS. 25.-- Haec locutus sum vobis. Haec de mansione 409Cin dilectoribus suis in quo significatur Ecclesia futura spiritualis, in qua beati, a quibus non recedit. Apud vos manens. Hic Ecclesia praesens corporalis in qua visitat liberando, et redit.

VERS. 26.-- Paracletus. Consolari cum sit opus Trinitatis, ad personam tamen maxime Spiritus sancti pertinet, quia in Scriptura nomine bonitatis Spiritus sanctus designatur: per bonitatem ergo Deus nos consolatur, per eam omnia bona sua nobis tribuit.

Ille vos. Quia etsi Filius doceat nos et revelet Patrem, hoc totum facit per suam bonitatem. Ille vos docebit omnia. Docebit quae dixero, non quod dicat Filius et Spiritus doceat, quia quod dicit et docet, et ipsa Trinitas dicit et docet. Sed quia Trinitas est, 409Doportet singulas personas insinuari, quos nos distincte audire, inseparabiliter intelligere debemus. Cum ergo dicimus, Pater et Filius et Spiritus sanctus, non eos simul dicimus, cum non possint esse non simul.

VERS. 27.-- Pacem relinquo vobis. Iturus ad Patrem. Pacem, etc. Et repetens de eodem. Pacem meam do. Et hoc est quod Propheta dicit: Pacem super pacem (Isa. XXVII). Pax est in hoc saeculo, in qua manentes hostem vincimus, invicem diligimus, et de occultis invicem non judicamus. Pax erit in futuro cum sine hoste regnabimus, ubi alter ab altero non poterit dissentire, ubi omnia aperta et laus unicuique (I Cor. IV). In illo autem et ab illo est utraque pax. Ipse enim est pax nostra (Ephes. II), et eum 410Acredimus, quia est, et eum videbimus sicuti est. Unde quod dicit meam, a posteriori, etiam ad prius subaudiendum est. Vel pax in hoc saeculo est nostra, quae est aliqua et non plena, et ab illo in futuro sua est pax ubi nihil repugnat qualiter ipse habet.

Non turbetur. Quia in pace vos relinquo, non turbetur cor vestrum de eo quod dixi, Vado, quasi pastoris absentia lupus invadit, quia sicuti homo vado, ut Deus praesens sum.

VERS. 28.-- Si diligeretis. (AUG.) Hoc erat ire ad eum, et recedere a nobis, etc., usque ad ut incorruptibilis pulvis sederet ad dexteram Patris.

Gauderetis, etc. Quia humanae naturae congratulandum est, quae levatur ad dexteram Patris, in quo idem sibi sperant caeteri.

410BVERS. 28.-- Credatis. Non quod factum videritis quia non est laus vel meritum fidei, imo nec fides est de eo quod, videtur, sed de eo quod non videtur. Sed credatis me Filium Dei, non nova fide, quia etiam prius crediderant, sed aucta fide et refecta, quia et modo cum haec diceret parva erat, et cum moreretur, nulla.

VERS. 30, 31.-- Et in me, etc. Noluit Dominus habere quod perderet; pauper venit ne haberet diabolus quod auferret.

CHAPTER XIV.

  • 407B

VERS. 1.-- Let not your heart be troubled, etc. (AUG.) But lest they should fear his death as a man's, and on that account be troubled, etc., as far as one glory of the moon, another glory of the stars.

VERS. 2.-- In the house, etc. The house of God, the temple of God, the kingdom of God. The kingdom of heaven is just men, among whom there are many differences one from another. And these are the mansions of that same house.

(AUG.) Just men are now the kingdom of God and the kingdom of heaven, etc., as far as that what you were in hope, you may be in reality.

If otherwise, etc. That is, if I had not predestined a mansion, which is unfitting as regards God, who predestined all good things before all times, then I should only now be preparing, that is, predestining, 407Cwhich again is unfitting, as regards God's predestining anything in the present.

(ID.) He confirms that they are, because if they were not he would have said, I will go and will prepare, etc., as far as and this is the multitude of mansions.

VERS. 3.-- And if I shall go. (ID.) He went away by hiding, he came by appearing, but he remains by governing, that we may make progress by living well, and thus a place is prepared.

(ID.) The faith which does not see believes, for if it saw, etc., as far as the desire of love is the preparation of a mansion.

VERS. 4.-- And whither I go. This was said with respect to certain ones who knew both the Son and the Father. But Thomas, although he supposed himself not to know, is convicted by this, that he knew him, and knew both the way and the life, 407Dthat is, the Son and the Father. Of these who knew, it is subjoined: From henceforth you shall know him, known through me, and you have seen him in the heart, while you have seen me like to him in all things.

VERS. 5.-- Thomas saith to him, etc. He who does not lie said that they knew whither and by what way one goes; but Thomas says that they know neither, not knowing that they know; yet Christ convicts them of knowing, because if they knew him, and he is the way, they knew the way; and if they knew him, and he is the truth and the life, then they knew the truth and the life to which he goes: he therefore goes by himself as the way, to himself as truth and life, and to the Father, because he is one with the Father.

VERS. 7.-- If you had known me. Because he had said that none 408Acomes to the Father except through himself, who is inseparably the same with the Father, lest they should ask who the Father is, he shows that from himself, once known, the Father is known, which certain ones had not yet known, whom he reproves, saying: If you had known, etc. And you have seen him. As it is said of two things wholly alike: If you have seen this one, you have seen that one; they had therefore seen the Son as most like [him], but they had to be admonished to understand the Father also to be such a one, and not unlike.

VERS. 9.-- How sayest thou. And that, when he is wholly like. How sayest thou, etc. As if he should say: I see with what mind thou sayest it; thou dost not seek another like me, but thou thinkest [him] better. Of myself. Because I who speak am not of myself: he attributes to him what he does, of whom is he himself who does it.

408BVERS. 12.-- He that believeth in me. I and the Father work without difference. And whosoever believes in me (lest this should be taken of the apostles alone), the works that I do through myself, he also shall do, that is, I do in him or through him, and even greater ones. The works that I do. Not however of himself, but of me. The servant is not greater than his lord. For he himself [does] many things without them, they without him nothing, save sin.

Greater than these. (AUG.) Because if he himself [healed] by the hem of his garment, Peter healed by a shadow. Or his work, etc., as far as because if both are of equal power, this is of greater mercy.

VERS. 15.-- If you love me. How does he admonish them to love and to keep the commandments, that they may receive the Spirit, whom unless they have they cannot love and keep? But 408Cwhat they had in less measure, secretly, they were to receive more abundantly, openly, so that what they had might become known to them.

VERS. 16.-- And I will ask the Father. (THEOPH.) I will ask, that is, I will beseech the Father, and he will send you another Paraclete, etc., as far as that the Paraclete Spirit and his condescending grace may wholly come upon them, he says to them: I will ask the Father.

VERS. 17.-- Because it seeth him not, etc. The lovers of the world have not spiritual eyes wherewith they might see the Holy Spirit. Nor indeed can they see him with carnal eyes, because the Holy Spirit did not appear clothed in humanity. And in you. He shall be in you; he shall not abide in order to be, because to be somewhere is prior to abiding. Let no one therefore think that he so abides as a guest visibly with another; but in them he can be known, not by others.

408DVERS. 18.-- I will not leave. The Spirit shall not be given in my stead in such a way that I too am not with you. Orphani is Greek, pupilli is Latin.

VERS. 19.-- Yet a little while, and the world. But the world, which now sees with the eyes of the flesh, after a little while shall not see, because when raised again he did not show himself to them; but the whole time is little, and when it is ended the impious man shall be taken away, that he may not see the glory of God, which the just shall see. Because I live, and you shall live. He uses the present, because his resurrection is soon to be. Of those he uses the future, whose resurrection is to be at the end.

VERS. 20.-- And you in me, and I in you. This is so now, and is known after a fashion through faith; 409Abut then it shall be known through sight, when this conjunction shall be perfected which is now begun.

VERS. 21.-- He that hath, etc. Not only you apostles shall live and shall know, but also whosoever has [them] in memory and keeps [them] in life. Or, he has by doing, keeps by persevering. Or he who has [them] in his discourses keeps [them] in his manners. Or, he has by hearing, keeps by doing. And I will love him. To this end will I love him, that I may manifest [myself], because even now he loves, but not to this end, but rather that we may believe and keep the commandments, that we may deserve what we may afterwards receive. And I will manifest to him. He who has my commandment, he knows me, and this is [what stands] there: I will manifest myself to him. But he sets first the cause of the commandment, because he loves me; and he who loves me, my Father will love him, and because the Father [loves him], then I too will love 409Bhim, and thence comes the manifestation.

VERS. 22.-- Judas saith to him, etc. Having heard that the world will only see him, but that they themselves shall see, Judas, about to ask on behalf of all, asks the cause of this distinction. But this cause is, according to the Lord's answer, love, through which alone and through no other is the holy nation distinguished from the unholy.

VERS. 23.-- We will come to him. God comes to man, while man comes to God. Man comes by believing, obeying, beholding, receiving; God comes by succouring, enlightening, filling. And a mansion, etc. Charity makes [a man] dwell in the house in which the Father and the Son make a mansion, whence he will also give the manifestation concerning which that man had asked.

VERS. 25.-- These things have I spoken to you. These things concerning the mansion 409Cin those who love him, in which is signified the future spiritual Church, in which are the blessed, from whom he does not withdraw. Abiding with you. Here [is] the present bodily Church, in which he visits by delivering, and returns.

VERS. 26.-- The Paraclete. Although to console is the work of the Trinity, yet it pertains most of all to the person of the Holy Spirit, because in Scripture the Holy Spirit is designated by the name of goodness: through goodness therefore God consoles us, through it he bestows on us all his good things.

He you. Because even though the Son teaches us and reveals the Father, he does all this through his goodness. He shall teach you all things. He shall teach the things that I shall have said, not that the Son says and the Spirit teaches, because what he says and teaches, the Trinity itself also says and teaches. But because it is a Trinity, 409Dit behoves the single persons to be intimated, whom we ought to hear distinctly, to understand inseparably. When therefore we say, Father and Son and Holy Spirit, we do not say them at the same time, since they cannot be not at the same time.

VERS. 27.-- Peace I leave with you. Being about to go to the Father. Peace, etc. And repeating concerning the same. My peace I give. And this is what the Prophet says: Peace upon peace (Isa. XXVII). There is a peace in this world, abiding in which we conquer the enemy, love one another, and do not judge one another concerning hidden things. There will be a peace in the world to come when we shall reign without an enemy, where one will not be able to dissent from another, where all things are open and praise is to each one (I Cor. IV). But in him and from him is each peace. For he himself is our peace (Ephes. II), and we 410Abelieve him, because he is, and we shall see him as he is. Whence what he says, my, from the latter is to be understood also of the former. Or, the peace in this world is ours, which is some [peace] and not full; and from him in the world to come is his own peace, where nothing is repugnant, in the manner in which he himself has it.

Let it not be troubled. Because I leave you in peace, let not your heart be troubled at that which I said, I go, as though at the shepherd's absence the wolf invades; because as man I go, as God I am present.

VERS. 28.-- If you loved. (AUG.) This was to go to him, and to withdraw from us, etc., as far as that incorruptible dust might sit at the right hand of the Father.

You would rejoice, etc. Because human nature is to be congratulated, which is raised to the right hand of the Father, in whom the rest hope for the same for themselves.

410BVERS. 28.-- That you may believe. Not that you should have seen it done, because there is no praise or merit of faith; nay, faith is not even of that which, is seen, but of that which is not seen. But that you may believe me to be the Son of God, not with a new faith, because they had believed before also, but with a faith increased and restored, because even now, when he was saying these things, it was small, and when he was dying, none at all.

VERS. 30, 31.-- And in me, etc. The Lord would not have anything that he might lose; he came poor, lest the devil should have anything that he might take away.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- Ego sum vitis vera. Et me praecedentem ad passionem debetis sequi, quia ego sum vitis, id est caput vestrum ejusdem naturae vobiscum, ut in me possit fructificare humana natura. 410CEt Pater, etc. Ego etiam agricola cum Patre, quia sum mundator palmitum, quod est agricolae, non vitis officium, qui etiam palmites operarios facit, licet non de suo, nec per ipsos, sed per meipsum.

VERS. 3.-- Propter sermonem, etc. Non ait: propter aquam baptismi, quia et ipsa sine verbo est solum aqua, per verbum fit sacramentum.

VERS. 5.-- Ego sum, etc. Qui se a se ferre fructum existimat, non est in vite. Unde cum repetitione et superiorum explanatione supponit: Ego sum vitis, vos palmites. Mihi quasi viti adhaerentes. Qui manet in me. Et ideo quicunque (non solum vos) manet in me credendo, obediendo, perseverando. Et ego in eo. Illuminando, subveniendo, perseverantiam dando. Hic fert. Et si secundum hominem Christus 410Dest vitis, tamen hanc gratiam palmitibus non daret, nisi etiam Deus esset.

VERS. 6.-- Si quis. Sarmenta praecisa caeteris lignis sunt viliora, quia nullis usibus deputantur, sed igni, vel in vite sunt vel in igne.

VERS. 7.-- Et verba. Verba Dei tunc dicuntur manere in nobis, quando facimus quae praecipit et diligimus quae promittit. Qui autem hoc non facit, tangit, non cohaeret, et ideo non sunt ei in beneficium sed in testimonium damnationis. Petetis. Si quid petit fidelis et non fit, non petit hoc quod habet mansio in eo, nec quod habet verba ejus quae sunt in eo, sed quod habet cupidites et infirmitas carnis.

411AVERS. 8.-- In hoc clarificatus, etc. Qui manet in me fructum multum fert, quod est ex gratia Dei, et ita est gloria ejus non hominis, et hoc est: In hoc, etc. Ut fructum plurimum. Sicut non aequalitatem ostendit nostrae naturae et suae, sed gratiam qua Mediator est, nam Pater diligit nos, sed in Filio. Efficiamini. Quomodo? Si me dilexeritis. Ex qua dilectione? ex praeceptorum observatione. Si me dilexeritis sicut dilexit me Pater, non quantum. Non enim notatur aequalitas, sed similitudo dans eis aeternam aeternitatem.

VERS. 11.-- Ut gaudium. Gaudium Dei de nobis qui praescivit et praedestinavit semper est plenum, in nobis incipit ex fide, crescit imo et perficietur in resurrectione.

VERS. 12.-- Hoc est praeceptum, etc. Et supra de 411Bdilectione proximo, sed repetitio est confirmatio: ibi tamen dicit: Mandatum do, quasi non ante fuerit; hic autem, hoc est quasi non aliud sit. Ibi Novum, ne in vetustate remaneamus, hic, Meum, ne contemnatur.

VERS. 13.-- Majorem hac. Diffinit plenitudinem mutuae dilectionis quam habere praecipit.

VERS. 15.-- Jam non dicam. Commendata dilectione addit quod dignationis est, ut bonos servos (qui sciunt faciendo quae praecipit) dicat amicos, et eis tollit nomen servi, qui est ex timore foras mittendo, qui nescit quid faciat Dominus. Quia cum aliquid facit, sic extollitur quasi ipse faciat, non dominus.

411CVERS. 17.-- Haec mando, etc. Hic ostendit quis sit fructus quem dixerat, pro quo et dabit Pater quod petimus, qui et hoc dedit, ut diligamus.

Ut diligatis. (AUG.) Merito saepe dilectionem commendat, quasi sola praecipienda sit sine qua non possunt caetera, quae sine caeteris non potest haberi. Pro hac autem patienter debemus sustinere etiam omnia adversa mundi. Unde subdit:

VERS. 18.-- Si mundus. Nomine mundi aliquando boni, aliquando mali designantur, quia una massa est quae tota in Adam periit, de qua sunt alia vasa in honorem, alia in contumeliam.

VERS. 19.-- Mundus quod suum. Si malus punit scelus mali, ex parte odit mundus quod suum est; dum punit ex parte amat, quia malus favet 411Dmalis.

VERS. 20.-- Sermonis mei. Hoc scilicet: Non est. Et illius superioris: Scitote, quia me priorem vobis odio habuit. Et subdit causam: Non est servus major, etc. Quia exemplo sui (quo aliud melius non est) monuit ad ferenda odia mundi, subdit quare debeant, eum in hoc imitari, quia ipse Dominus, illi servi.

VERS. 22.-- Si non venissem. Hic expressius Judaeos ostendit, ad quos venit, de quibus sub nomine mundi superiora dixerat. Et quia ad eos venit, hic inexcusabiles de omnibus malis ostendit. Excusationem, etc. Illi quibus non venit nec locutus est, habent excusationem non de omni peccato, sed de 412Ahoc quod in Christum non crediderint. Hi vero quibus in apostolis venit et locutus est, non excusantur. Priores vero qui sine lege peccaverunt, sine lege peribunt (Rom. II). De peccato. Quod non credunt me sed oderunt, et persequentur in meipso et in meis, sed hoc peccatum in me, etiam redundat in Patrem, quia qui me odit et Patrem meum odit. Et quod est gravissimum, non solum veni et locutus sum, sed opera feci, quae videntes oderunt et me et Patrem.

VERS. 24.-- Quae nemo, etc. (AUG.) Quod pavit tot millia de paucis, quod super aquas ivit, etc., usque ad et quia ipse per seipsum fecit, nemo sine eo.

Oderunt, etc. Judaei dicunt se diligere Deum et 412Btamen nescientes oderunt, quia qui volunt esse mali nolunt esse veritatem qua damnantur. Et sicut oderunt suam poenam, oderunt veritatem quae irrogat poenam, nescientes eum esse veritatem. Et si oderunt veritatem, oderunt eum de quo nata est veritas, et ita Patrem nescientes de eo natam veritatem oderunt.

VERS. 25.-- Quia odio. Gratis odit, qui nullum ex odio commodum quaerit vel incommodum fugit. Sic impii oderunt Christum. Sic justi diligunt gratis, non propter aliud sed propter ipsum.

VERS. 26.-- Cum autem. Nunc videntes opera oderunt gratis et me et Patrem. Sed cum venerit Paracletus, manifestiore testimonio convincet eos qui viderunt et oderunt, sed et quosdam eorum compunctos 412Cde tanto scelere convertet. Quem ego mittam. Die Pentecostes venit Spiritus sanctus in centum viginti homines congregatos, cum quibus erant omnes apostoli in domo ubi coena fuerat. Qui a Patre. Spiritus procedit a Patre et Filio, sed Filius dicitur a Patre procedere, quia ad Patrem solet referre, et quod ipsius est de quo ipse est. A quo ergo habet ut sit, ab illo habet ut de illo procedat Spiritus.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- I am the true vine. And you ought to follow me as I go before to the passion, because I am the vine, that is, your head, of the same nature with you, that in me human nature may be able to bear fruit. 410CAnd the Father, etc. I too am the husbandman together with the Father, because I am the cleanser of the branches, which is the office of the husbandman, not of the vine, who also makes the branches workmen, though not out of what is their own, nor through them, but through myself.

VERS. 3.-- Because of the word, etc. He does not say: because of the water of baptism, because that also, without the word, is water only; through the word it becomes a sacrament.

VERS. 5.-- I am, etc. He who reckons that he bears fruit of himself is not in the vine. Whence, with a repetition and an explanation of what went before, he adds: I am the vine, you the branches. Cleaving to me as to a vine. He that abideth in me. And therefore whosoever (not you only) abides in me by believing, by obeying, by persevering. And I in him. By enlightening, by succouring, by giving perseverance. He beareth. And although according to the man Christ 410Dis the vine. yet he would not give this grace to the branches unless he were also God.

VERS. 6.-- If any one. Shoots that have been cut off are of less worth than other woods, because they are appointed to no uses but the fire; they are either in the vine or in the fire.

VERS. 7.-- And the words. The words of God are then said to abide in us, when we do the things he commands and love the things he promises. But he who does not do this touches, he does not cleave; and therefore they are not to him for a benefit but for a testimony of damnation. You shall ask. If a faithful man asks anything and it is not done, he does not ask that which the mansion in him has, nor that which his words which are in him have, but that which cupidity and the infirmity of the flesh have.

411AVERS. 8.-- In this is glorified, etc. He who abides in me bears much fruit, which is from the grace of God, and so it is his glory, not man's; and this is: In this, etc. That you bring forth very much fruit. Just as he shows not an equality of our nature and his, but the grace by which he is Mediator; for the Father loves us, but in the Son. That you may become. How? If you shall have loved me. From what love? From the observance of the precepts. If you shall have loved me as the Father loved me, not as much. For an equality is not noted, but a likeness giving them eternal eternity.

VERS. 11.-- That joy. The joy of God concerning us, whom he foreknew and predestined, is always full; in us it begins from faith, it grows, nay and shall be perfected in the resurrection.

VERS. 12.-- This is the commandment, etc. And above concerning 411Blove toward the neighbour; but repetition is confirmation: there, however, he says, I give a commandment, as though it had not been before; but here, this is, as though it were not another. There, New, lest we remain in oldness; here, Mine, lest it be contemned.

VERS. 13.-- Greater than this. He defines the fulness of mutual love which he commands them to have.

VERS. 15.-- Now I will not call. Love having been commended, he adds what belongs to condescension, that he should call good servants (who know by doing the things he commands) friends, and takes from them the name of servant, which is from fear, by casting it out, [the servant] who does not know what his Lord does. Because when he does anything, he is so exalted as though he himself did it, and not the lord.

411CVERS. 17.-- These things I command, etc. Here he shows what is the fruit of which he had spoken, for which the Father will also give what we ask, who gave this also, that we should love.

That you love one another. (AUG.) Deservedly does he often commend love, as though it alone were to be commanded, without which the rest cannot be, and which without the rest cannot be had. And for its sake we ought patiently to endure even all the adversities of the world. Whence he subjoins:

VERS. 18.-- If the world. By the name of the world sometimes the good, sometimes the evil are designated, because there is one mass which wholly perished in Adam, of which some are vessels unto honour, others unto contumely.

VERS. 19.-- The world what was its own. If an evil man punishes the crime of an evil man, in part the world hates what is its own; while it punishes, in part it loves, because an evil man favours 411Dthe evil.

VERS. 20.-- Of my word. This, namely: It is not. And of that above: Know ye, that it hath hated me before you. And he subjoins the cause: The servant is not greater, etc. Because by his own example (than which nothing else is better) he admonished them to bear the hatreds of the world, he subjoins why they ought, to imitate him in this, because he himself [is] Lord, they servants.

VERS. 22.-- If I had not come. Here he shows more expressly the Jews, to whom he came, of whom he had spoken the things above under the name of the world. And because he came to them, he here shows them inexcusable concerning all evils. An excuse, etc. Those to whom he did not come nor speak have an excuse, not concerning every sin, but concerning 412Athis, that they have not believed in Christ. But these, to whom he came and spoke in the apostles, are not excused. But the former, who have sinned without the law, shall perish without the law (Rom. II). Of sin. Because they do not believe me but hate me, and will persecute me in myself and in my own; but this sin against me redounds also upon the Father, because he who hates me hates my Father also. And what is most grievous, not only did I come and speak, but I did works, which, seeing, they hated both me and the Father.

VERS. 24.-- Which no man, etc. (AUG.) That he fed so many thousands from a few [loaves], that he walked upon the waters, etc., as far as and because he did it through himself, no one without him.

They hated, etc. The Jews say that they love God, and 412Byet, not knowing, they hate him, because those who wish to be evil do not wish there to be a truth by which they are condemned. And as they hate their own punishment, they hate the truth which inflicts the punishment, not knowing that he is the truth. And if they hate the truth, they hate him of whom the truth was born; and so, they hate the Father, not knowing that the truth was born of him.

VERS. 25.-- Because with hatred. He hates gratis who seeks no advantage from his hatred nor flees any disadvantage. So do the impious hate Christ. So do the just love gratis, not for the sake of anything else but for his own sake.

VERS. 26.-- But when. Now, seeing the works, they hate gratis both me and the Father. But when the Paraclete shall have come, he will convict by a more manifest testimony those who saw and hated; but some of them also, pricked 412Cfor so great a crime, he will convert. Whom I will send. On the day of Pentecost the Holy Spirit came upon a hundred and twenty men gathered together, with whom were all the apostles in the house where the supper had been. Who from the Father. The Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son, but the Son is said to proceed from the Father, because he is wont to refer to the Father even what is his own, from whom he himself is. From whom therefore he has that he is, from him he has that the Spirit proceeds from him.

CAPUT XVI.

VERS 1.-- Haec locutus sum, etc. Postquam exemplo suo docuit ferre odia in quo sciunt quid debeant facere, sed non possunt postquam etiam promisit Spiritum quo poterunt resistere, etiam addit: Haec locutus sum, etc. Quod est exemplum de Spiritu qui 412Dconfirmabit, ut, ita charitate diffusa in cordibus vestris, sit vobis multa pax et non scandalum.

VERS. 2.-- Sed venit hora. Quasi dicat: Si pulsi ab eis tot acquiretis mihi (unde vobis gaudendum) ut non satis sit eis vos pellere. Sed non sinant vivere, ne omnes acquiratis, ut a Judaismo avertatis, in quo Deum Israel credunt deseri et sancta sua.

VERS. 4.-- Haec autem. Postquam dixit futuras eorum passiones et consolatorem Spiritum eis esse dandum, subdit: Haec non dixi, etc. Quod si de passionibus accipimus, non solummodo post coenam sed et ante proximum Pascha, et ab initio missionis duodecim apostolorum praedixerat; sed de consolatione Spiritus eis ventura non praedixerat, quia ipse 413Acum eis corporali praesentia advocatus vel consolator erat; sed quia jam iturus est, opus erat ut illum diceret venturum qui confirmaret eos contra abjicientes et interficientes.

VERS. 5.-- Vado ad eum. Ita palam ut nemo interroget quod visu corporali cernat, viderunt eum in nube ascendentem certi de gloria qui prius praedicta passione quaesierant: Domine, quo vadis?

VERS. 7.-- Si enim, etc. Non quod positus in terra non posset dare, sed quia illi non sunt idonei accipere, nisi eum secundum carnem desistant nosse.

Si autem. Mentibus vestris sic raptis ad coelestia aderit Spiritus simul Pater et Filius. Spirituales enim ex carnalibus facti, capacius fuerant Trinitatem habituri, sicut promissum erat.

413BVERS. 8.-- Ille arguet. Non quod Filius non arguat, sed quia Spiritus dabit eis charitatem et pellet timorem, ut sit eis libertas arguendi.

(AUG.) Arguitur de peccato qui in Christum non credit. Arguitur, etc., usque ad ne cum mundi principe judicentur quem judicatum imitantur.

Et de justitia. Non de sua, sed de credentium justitia mundus arguitur, quorum comparatione damnatur. Unde non ait: Non videbit me mundus, sed Non videbitis me, vos apostoli. Contra infideles qui dicunt, Quomodo credemus quod non videmus? Est vera justitia credere quod non videtur, ut qui viderunt hominem crediderunt Deum. Et cum per ascensionem forma hominis subtracta est visibus, tunc ex omni parte impletum est: Justus ex fide vivit.

413CVERS. 10.-- Et jam, etc. Id est, nunquam videbitis me talem ut modo, id est mortalem judicandum, sed glorificatum judicem. Et hoc est justitia fidei, de qua Apostolus: Et si novimus Christum secundum carnem, sed jam non novimus (II Cor. V), quam quia mundus ab istis non vult accipere arguitur.

VERS. 11.-- De judicio. Quia si damnatum illum sequuntur cum eo damnantur, et ne sibi credant parci terrentur supplicio illius superbi.

VERS. 13.-- Omnem veritatem, etc. Promittit quod totum facturus est Spiritus, non quod in hac vita totum compleatur.

A semetipso, etc. (AUG.) Quia non est a seipso, quia solus Pater, etc., usque ad semper scivit, scit 413Det sciet.

VERS. 14.-- Ille me clarificabit. Quia spirituales faciendo vos declarabit quomodo Patri Filius est aequalis quem secundum carnem ut hominem noverant, vel timorem tollendo faciet vos gloriam meam annuntiare toti mundo, quae non mihi, sed hominibus prosit.

De meo. Non quod Spiritus sit minor Filio. Cur autem hoc dixit, explanat: Omnia quaecunque habet Pater mea sunt. Propterea dixi quia de meo accipiet. Ergo de Patre accipit Spiritus, unde accipit Filius, quia in hac Trinitate de Patre natus est Filius, de Patre procedit Spiritus. Qui autem de nullo natus est de nullo procedit, Pater est solus.

414AVERS. 15.-- Omnia, etc. (AUG.) De Spiritu dicit Christus. Spiritus Patris, etc., usque ad ita aperte ostenditur Patris et Filii esse Spiritus.

VERS. 16.-- Modicum. Id est, parvum tempus restat usque ad hoc quod patiar et claudar sepulcro, et iterum parvum tempus usquequo me videatis resuscitatum. Vel parvum tempus est, id est triduum quo clausus non videbo. Et iterum parvum, id est quadraginta dies, quo resuscitatus videbor. Quia vado. Hoc est quia tempus est ut, deposita mortalitate, humanam naturam coelis inferam.

VERS. 20.-- Quia plorabitis. Me occiso, de quo inimici gaudebunt. Similiter plorant omnes boni in aerumnis hujus saeculi; sed mundi amatores gaudent. Boni autem comparantur parienti quae genuit in 414Bpartu; sed laetatur de fructu, et plus gaudet de adventu futurae prolis quam tristis est de praesenti dolore.

VERS. 23.-- Si quid petieritis. (AUG.) Ita sane intelligendum est, quod ait, dabit vobis, ut ea beneficia significata sciantur quae ad eos qui petunt proprie pertinent. Exaudiuntur enim sancti pro seipsis, non autem pro omnibus aliis amicis vel inimicis.

Dabit vobis. In futuro non rogabitis, sed modo petite et dabitur vobis quod ad vos pertinet, et si non omnibus illis quibus petitis, et ideo dicitur vobis.

VERS. 25.-- Haec in proverbiis. Quae hucusque dixi quasi in proverbiis (quae solent homines audire sed non intelligere) dixi, quia animales estis. Animalis 414Cautem qui non percipit quae sunt Spiritus Dei (I Joan. I). Quaecunque dicuntur de incorporea et immutabili substantia, habet quasi proverbia. Sed venit hora. Qua dato Spiritu faciam vos spirituales, ut certissima intelligentia mentis (etsi non ut in futuro) Deum videatis, et tunc palam de Patre nuntiabo, ut ipsum et me ejusdem substantiae intelligatis, et tunc, In nomine meo petite. Quia tunc vere cognoscetis et scietis quod si ut homo rogo Patrem, ut Deus exaudio cum Patre; unde dicit: Et non dico vobis, quoniam ego rogabo Patrem.

VERS. 27.-- Ipse enim. Amat nos Pater quia amamus Filium, cum a Patre et Filio accepimus ut amemus Patrem et Filium: data charitate per Spiritum, 414Dquem et ipsum amamus cum Patre et Filio, prior amans facit in nobis quod ametur.

VERS. 28.-- Iterum relinquo mundum. Reliquit mundum corporali praesentia. Vadit ad Patrem, quia humanitatem ad invisibilia paternae majestatis induxit. Reliquit mundum, quia ab aspectu amatorum mundi quod viderant abstulit. Redit sd patriam, quia amatoribus suis se aequalem credendum docuit Patri.

VERS. 29.-- Dicunt ei discipuli ejus. Cum nondum venisset hora quam promisit: Ecce nunc palam loquitur. Illa quae Dominus scit non intelligentibus esse proverbia: illi usque adeo nobis intelligunt, ut nec saltem se non intelligere intelligant. De qua 415Aeorum infirmitate admonens respondit: Modo creditis, etc.

VERS. 30.-- Nunc scimus quia scis omnia. Aperte ostendunt, quia loquens de eis, ad eos maxime agebat quae illos delectabant audire: et quae ipsi interrogare volebant, haec ipse praeveniens ultro proferebat, et ideo illum, quia cogitationes noverat, confitentur scire omnia quasi Deum et Dei Filium. Et non opus est tibi, etc. Cum scias omnia, non est opus ut interroges aliquem a quo aliquid scias. Vel etiam interrogeris ab aliquo praesciens quid ille velit. Si autem aliquando interrogavit vel interrogatus est, non eis, sed aliis opus fuit.

VERS. 31.-- Modo creditis. Ut parvuli, quibus quaecunque alta adhuc proverbia sunt, creditis, sed venit 415Bhora passionis, et jam prope est, in qua ita perturbemini, ut quod modo creditis, relinquatis, non modo corpore, sed etiam mentis fide; unde: Sperabamus quod redempturus esset Israel.

VERS. 32.-- Et non sum, etc. Ecce profecerunt, sed ad majus vult eos extendi, ut cum dixerint: A Deo existi, dicat: Pater mecum est, nec sic putent a Deo exisse, ut putent etiam recessisse. Quia Pater mecum est. Dixerat Relinquo mundum et vado ad Patrem, nunc dicit: Pater mecum est, quod intelligenti est verbum, non intelligenti proverbium. Cui si non est cibus, tamen lactis est alimentum. Ex quo est, quod sciebant eum nosse omnia.

CHAPTER XVI.

VERS 1.-- These things have I spoken, etc. After he had taught by his own example to bear hatreds, wherein they know what they ought to do but cannot, after he had also promised the Spirit by whom they will be able to resist, he adds also: These things have I spoken, etc. Which is an example concerning the Spirit who 412Dwill confirm them, so that, charity being thus poured forth in your hearts, there may be to you much peace and no stumbling-block.

VERS. 2.-- But the hour cometh. As if he should say: If, driven out by them, you gain so many for me (whereat you ought to rejoice), so that it is not enough for them to drive you out. But let them not suffer [you] to live, lest you gain all, so that you turn [them] away from Judaism, in which they believe the God of Israel and his holy things to be forsaken.

VERS. 4.-- But these things. After he had spoken of their sufferings to come and of the Spirit the comforter to be given them, he subjoins: These things I said not, etc. Which, if we take it of the sufferings, he had foretold them not only after the supper but also before the next Passover, and from the beginning of the sending of the twelve apostles; but concerning the consolation of the Spirit to come to them he had not foretold, because he himself 413Awith his bodily presence was their advocate or comforter; but because he is now about to go, it was needful that he should say that he would come who should confirm them against those who cast them out and killed them.

VERS. 5.-- I go to him. So openly that no one asks what he discerns by bodily sight; they saw him ascending in a cloud, certain of his glory, they who before, when the passion had been foretold, had asked: Lord, whither goest thou?

VERS. 7.-- For if, etc. Not that, placed on earth, he could not give him, but because they are not fit to receive him, unless they cease to know him according to the flesh.

But if. To your minds thus caught up to heavenly things the Spirit will be present, and together with him the Father and the Son. For, made spiritual out of carnal, they were to possess the Trinity more capaciously, as had been promised.

413BVERS. 8.-- He shall convince. Not that the Son does not convince, but because the Spirit will give them charity and will drive out fear, so that there may be to them a liberty of convincing.

(AUG.) He is convicted of sin who does not believe in Christ. He is convicted, etc., as far as lest they be judged along with the prince of the world whom, judged, they imitate.

And of justice. Not of its own, but of the justice of believers is the world convicted, by comparison with whom it is condemned. Whence he does not say: The world shall not see me, but You shall not see me, you apostles. Against the unfaithful who say, How shall we believe what we do not see? It is true justice to believe what is not seen, so that they who saw the man believed him God. And when through the ascension the form of the man was withdrawn from their sight, then in every part was fulfilled: The just liveth by faith.

413CVERS. 10.-- And now, etc. That is, never shall you see me such as now, that is, mortal, to be judged, but glorified, a judge. And this is the justice of faith, concerning which the Apostle: And if we have known Christ according to the flesh, yet now we know him no longer (II Cor. V), which, because the world will not receive it from these men, it is convicted.

VERS. 11.-- Of judgment. Because if they follow him who is condemned they are condemned with him; and lest they should believe themselves to be spared, they are terrified by the punishment of that proud one.

VERS. 13.-- All truth, etc. He promises what the Spirit is to do wholly, not that in this life the whole be accomplished.

Of himself, etc. (AUG.) Because he is not of himself, because the Father alone, etc., as far as he always knew, knows 413Dand will know.

VERS. 14.-- He shall glorify me. Because by making you spiritual he will declare how the Son is equal to the Father, whom according to the flesh, as a man, they had known; or, by taking away fear, he will make you announce my glory to the whole world, which glory profits not me, but men.

Of mine. Not that the Spirit is less than the Son. But why he said this, he explains: All things whatsoever the Father hath are mine. Therefore I said that he shall receive of mine. Therefore the Spirit receives from the Father, whence the Son receives, because in this Trinity the Son is born of the Father, the Spirit proceeds from the Father. But he who is born of none and proceeds from none is the Father alone.

414AVERS. 15.-- All things, etc. (AUG.) Christ speaks concerning the Spirit. The Spirit of the Father, etc., as far as so openly is it shown that the Spirit is of the Father and of the Son.

VERS. 16.-- A little while. That is, a small time remains until this, that I suffer and be shut in the sepulchre, and again a small time until you see me raised up. Or it is a small time, that is, the three days in which, shut in, I shall not see. And again a small [time], that is, forty days, in which, raised up, I shall be seen. Because I go. This is because it is time that, mortality laid aside, I should bring human nature into the heavens.

VERS. 20.-- Because you shall lament. When I have been slain, over which the enemies will rejoice. In like manner all good men lament amid the hardships of this world; but the lovers of the world rejoice. But the good are compared to a woman in travail who has brought forth in 414Bchildbirth; but she is glad over the fruit, and rejoices more over the coming of the offspring to be than she is sad over the present pain.

VERS. 23.-- If you shall ask anything. (AUG.) Thus indeed is that to be understood which he says, he will give you: that those benefits are to be known as signified which properly pertain to those who ask. For the saints are heard for themselves, but not for all others, whether friends or enemies.

He will give you. In the world to come you shall not ask, but now ask, and there shall be given you what pertains to you, even if not to all those for whom you ask; and therefore it is said, to you.

VERS. 25.-- These things in proverbs. The things I have said thus far I have said as it were in proverbs (which men are wont to hear but not to understand), because you are natural men. But the natural 414Cman who perceiveth not the things that are of the Spirit of God (I Joan. I) [Vulg. 1 Cor 2:14 reads Animalis autem homo non percipit ea quae sunt Spiritus Dei, "the natural man perceiveth not the things that are of the Spirit of God"; Migne prints a relative where the received text has the subject noun and the finite verb]. Whatsoever things are said of an incorporeal and unchangeable substance, he holds as it were for proverbs. But the hour cometh. In which, the Spirit being given, I will make you spiritual, so that with the most certain understanding of the mind (though not as in the world to come) you may see God; and then I will announce openly concerning the Father, so that you may understand him and me to be of the same substance; and then, Ask in my name. Because then you will truly know and understand that if as man I ask the Father, as God I hear together with the Father; whence he says: And I say not to you, that I will ask the Father.

VERS. 27.-- For he himself. The Father loves us because we love the Son, since from the Father and the Son we have received that we should love the Father and the Son: charity having been given through the Spirit, 414Dwhom we also ourselves love together with the Father and the Son, he who loves first works in us that he be loved.

VERS. 28.-- Again I leave the world. He left the world by bodily presence. He goes to the Father, because he led manhood into the invisible things of the paternal majesty. He left the world, because he took away from the sight of the lovers of the world what they had seen. He returns to the fatherland, because he taught his lovers that he is to be believed equal to the Father.

VERS. 29.-- His disciples say to him. Since the hour which he promised had not yet come: Behold, now he speaketh plainly [Vulg. Jo 16:29 has loqueris, "now thou speakest plainly"; the disciples' address in the second person]. Those things which the Lord knows to be proverbs to men who do not understand: they to such a degree do not understand [nobis, "for us"; read non -- Augustine, Tract. in Ioh. 103, illi usque adeo non intelligunt], that they do not even understand that they do not understand. Admonishing them concerning which 415Ainfirmity of theirs, he answered: Now you believe, etc.

VERS. 30.-- Now we know that thou knowest all things. They show openly that, speaking of them, he was chiefly treating the things which it delighted them to hear; and the things which they themselves wished to ask, these he himself, anticipating, brought forward of his own accord; and therefore, because he knew their thoughts, they confess that he knows all things, as God and the Son of God. And thou needest not, etc. Since thou knowest all things, there is no need that thou shouldst question anyone from whom thou mightest know anything. Or even that thou shouldst be questioned by anyone, foreknowing what that man wishes. But if he ever questioned or was questioned, there was need not for them, but for others.

VERS. 31.-- Now you believe. As little ones, to whom whatsoever lofty things are still proverbs, you believe; but there comes 415Bthe hour of the passion, and now it is near, in which you will be so troubled that what you now believe you will forsake, not only in body, but even in the faith of the mind; whence: We hoped that he would have redeemed Israel.

VERS. 32.-- And I am not, etc. Behold, they have made progress, but he wishes them to be stretched out to something greater, so that when they shall have said: Thou camest forth from God, he may say: The Father is with me, lest they should so think him to have gone forth from God as to think that he had also withdrawn. Because the Father is with me. He had said, I leave the world and I go to the Father, now he says: The Father is with me, which to one who understands is a word, to one who does not understand a proverb. To whom, if it is not food, it is nevertheless the nourishment of milk. From which it comes that they knew him to know all things.

CAPUT XVII.

415CVERS. 1.-- Haec locutus est. Non solum quae in coena, sed etiam quae ab initio. Sed maxime quae in coena hac de causa dicit dicta, ut in illo pacem haberent. Haec pax est pro qua Christiani sumus, pro qua sacramentis imbuimur, pro qua omnimodis erudimur, pro qua Spiritum in pignus accepimus, pro qua in eum credimus et speramus et amore ejus accendimur. Haec est pressuris consolatio et liberatio. Pater, venit hora. Potuit orare silentio, sed in orando est etiam doctor, et notam facit orationem quae et praesentium et futurorum aedificatio. Venit hora. Non urgente fato, sed disponente Deo, qui sine tempore ordinavit omnia quae fiant per tempora ordine congruo. Clarifica Filium. Humilitas in passione, claritas in resurrectione coepit. Venit hora 415Dpassionis, seminandae humilitatis, non differas gloriam resurrectionis, ut Filius tuus clarificet te. Et sic claritas Patris nec augeri nec minui potest in se. Apud homines minor erat dum in Judaea sola notus, sed per resurrectionem Filii ubique innotuit. Ut Filius tuus clarificet te. In cognitione hominum, sicut dedisti, usque ut cognoscant te, et quem misisti. Non enim potest cognitione hominem clarificari Pater, nisi ille cognoscatur per quem clarificatur, id est populus innotescat, et haec est clarificatio Patris, quae non circa solos apostolos sed etiam circa omnes credentes sit.

VERS. 3.-- Haec est autem, etc. Ordo, Ut cognoscant te, et quem misisti, id est Christum cum Spiritu, 416Aqui est amborum, esse unum et solum verum Deum. Si ergo eo modo te clarificat Filius sicut dedisti ei potestatem omnis creaturae, et sic dedisti, ut omne quod ei dedisti ei det eis vitam aeternam: Et haec est vita aeterna, ut cognoscant te. Sic te ergo Filius clarificat, ut omnibus quos dedisti ei te cognitum faciat. Ut cognoscant te. Quod hic est ex Patre, in futuro erit plene. Qui ergo proficit in cognitione, magis vivere tendit. Quando autem erit plena cognitio, erit sine fine laudatio. Et haec est illa clarificatio, sed prius hic clarificatur dum innotescit fidelibus; unde: Ego te clarificavi super terram.

VERS. 4.-- Opus consummavi. Id est, si aperte futurum diceret, clarificabo, gentibus praedicando. Opus consummabo, quia jam factum praedestinatione; 416Bet pro jam facto est habendum, quod certissime est futurum. Hanc praedestinationem in sua clarificatione manifestius aperit, dicens: Quam habui apud te priusquam mundus esset; id est, sicut tunc in praedestinatione habui apud te ante tempora, ita nunc in re fac in mundo in tempore. Dedisti mihi, etc. In quo notatur gratia. Nihil enim habet in homine quod non accepit.

VERS. 5.-- Hominibus. Possunt haec omnia dici de omnibus fidelibus futuris. Et est haec clarificatio qua clarificatus Filius clarificat Patrem super terram, secundum quod est praeteritum pro futuro.

VERS. 6.-- Tui erant. (AUG.) Patris erant hi sicut omnia, etc., usque ad quia nondum erat homo.

VERS. 7.-- Cognoverunt. Ne putaretur cognitio 416Cjam facta per speciem, addit: Crediderunt vere; non eo modo quo cum dixit: Modo creditis, venit hora ut dispergamini. Sed nec adhuc tales erant, sed praenuntiat quales sint futuri accepto Spiritu, sine quo nec servaverunt sermonem quem dixerat.

VERS. 9.-- Ego pro eis rogo. Prima oratio fuit: Clarifica Filium, ut Filius clarificet te, et de his exsecutus est et conclusit in fide credentium. Jam pro eis rogat ut Pater servet eos quos ipse corpore reliquit.

VERS. 10.-- Et mea omnia. Non enim quia Filio dedit Pater amisit, cum Filius adhuc addat: Mea omnia tua et tua mea sunt. Unigeniti Filii sunt omnia quae Patris, per hoc quod etiam Deus est ipse, etiam de Patre natus, Patri est aequalis.

416DVERS. 11.-- Serva eos. Ut sint unum in natura sua, sicut et nos in nostra sumus unum; et ideo non ait, ut sint unum nobiscum, aut ut simus unum ipsi et nos sicut nos unum sumus, quia non sunt ejusdem naturae cum Deo, sicut Filius, secundum quod Deus, unum est cum Patre.

VERS. 12.-- Cum essem. Non debemus intelligere, quod Pater et Filius alternatim servent quasi alter post alterum. Simul enim servant Pater et Filius et Spiritus sanctus qui est unus Deus. Sed Scriptura nos non levat, nisi ad nos descendat. Cum ita loquitur, intelligamus distinguere personas, non separare naturam. Gaudium quod supra expressit, ut sint unum sicut et nos, haec est pax illa et beatitudo in 417Afuturo saeculo, propter quam omnia fiunt, propter quam se esse locutum dicit in mundo.

VERS. 17.-- Sanctifica. Sicut ex gratia tua incaepta est sanctificatio per quam jam non sunt de mundo, eadem gratia fac sanctiores, ut perseverent in veritate, cujus umbra fuerunt veteres sanctificationes; id est, in me qui sum veritas, quod subdendo aperit: Sermo tuus veritas est, quod est, ego sum veritas. Graece λόγος, Latine verbum vel sermo, qui est unigenitus Patris.

VERS. 19.-- Et pro eis ego sanctifico. Id est, ut pro eis prosit, ego me hominem sanctificabo in me Verbo quod est ab initio Incarnationis suae quando Verbum factum est caro. Ita autem illis prodest, quia et ipsos, qui sunt ego, quia caput et corpus sunt unus Christus, 417Bsanctifico in meipso, unde addit: ut sint et ipsi sicut ego, sanctificati iu veritate et in me.

VERS. 20.-- Non pro eis. Jam non de solis apostolis, vel de caeteris membris incipit: Non autem orat pro his qui jam sunt in fide ejus defuncti, sed pro his qui sunt in tentationibus. Sed et pro eis qui. Usque in finem mundi, vel jam credunt ubicunque sunt, non tamen tam perfecte, ut post credituri sunt post resurrectionem. Per verbum. Per verbum Evangelii, quod est eorum, non quod per eos tantum sit praedicatum, sed et per Paulum et alios quoslibet, sed quia eis primo et principaliter commissum sit ad praedicandum. Hoc est verbum fidei, verbum Dei, per quod credidit latro, credidit Paulus, et quicunque credit.

417CVERS. 21.-- Ut omnes unum sint. Non ait, Omnes unum sumus, sed ut illi omnes unum sint, sicut tu Pater in me et ego in te, subaudi unum sumus: Ita et Pater in Filio et Filius in Patre, ut unum sint, quia unius substantiae. Nos vero possumus esse in Patre et Filio et Spiritu sancto. Vel Pater et Filius et Spiritus sanctus in nobis esse possunt, unum tamen cum Deo esse non possumus, quia non unius substantiae. Deus vero in nobis est ut in templo, nos in illo ut creatura in Creatore. Ut et ipsi. In nobis dicit, ut quod unum sunt fide et charitate, gratiae Dei tribuant, ut nunc autem lux in Domino.

Ut mundus. Mundus hic accipitur pro omnibus illis qui unum sunt. Nec hoc quod sunt facit causam ut credant, quia potius credendo unum sunt quamvis 417Dunum essent natura, dissentiendo tamen ab uno non erant unum, sed orando dixit ut mundus credat, sicut orando dixit ut omnes unum sint, acsi tertio subaudiatur, rogo ut mundus credat.

VERS. 24.-- Quos dedisti. Id est, traxisti ad me, sic et ego elegi eos de mundo: alii non sunt dati ei, non sunt sui, etsi potestas sit ei omnium. Volo. Non potest non fieri quod vult Omnipotens, sed illa voluntas est hominis, de qua dicit: Non sicut ego volo (Matth. XXVI). Videant. Cum in forma servi clarificata viderint judicantem bonum et malum, tolletur impius ne videat claritatem Dei qua Deus est, quam soli mundi corde videbunt quod erit eis vita aeterna.

418AVERS. 26.-- Ut dilectio. Dilectio qua Pater diligit Filium est in omnibus justis, quia membra ejus sunt et diliguntur in illo qui totus diligitur, ideo subdit: Et ego in ipsis sum. Quasi dicat: Quia ego sum in ipsis, aliter est in nobis ut in templo, nos in illo aliter; quia nos sumus ille, cum secundum hominem ejus capitis sumus corpus.

CHAPTER XVII.

415CVERS. 1.-- These things spoke. Not only the things [said] at the supper, but also those from the beginning. But chiefly he says that the things at the supper were said for this cause, that in him they might have peace. This is the peace for which we are Christians, for which we are steeped in the sacraments, for which we are instructed in every way, for which we have received the Spirit as a pledge, for which we believe in him and hope, and are kindled with love of him. This is consolation in afflictions and deliverance. Father, the hour is come. He could have prayed in silence, but in praying he is also a teacher, and makes his prayer known, which is the edification both of those present and of those to come. The hour is come. Not with fate urging, but with God disposing, who without time ordained all the things that come to pass through times in a fitting order. Glorify thy Son. Humility began in the passion, glory in the resurrection. The hour of the passion is come, 415Dthe hour of sowing humility; do not defer the glory of the resurrection, that thy Son may glorify thee. And thus the glory of the Father can neither be increased nor diminished in itself. Among men it was less while he was known in Judaea alone, but through the resurrection of the Son he became known everywhere. That thy Son may glorify thee. In the knowledge of men, as thou hast given, as far as that they may know thee, and him whom thou hast sent. For the Father cannot be glorified in the knowledge of man unless he be known through whom he is glorified, that is, unless the people become known; and this is the glorification of the Father, which is not about the apostles alone but also about all believers.

VERS. 3.-- And this is, etc. The order [is], That they may know thee, and him whom thou hast sent, that is, Christ with the Spirit, 416Awho is of both, to be the one and only true God. If therefore the Son glorifies thee in that manner in which thou hast given him power over every creature, and hast so given it, that all that thou hast given him, he may give them life everlasting: And this is life everlasting, that they may know thee. So therefore does the Son glorify thee, that he may make thee known to all whom thou hast given him. That they may know thee. What is here from the Father, in the world to come will be fully. He therefore who advances in knowledge tends the more to live. But when knowledge shall be full, there will be praise without end. And this is that glorification; but first he is glorified here, while he becomes known to the faithful; whence: I have glorified thee upon earth.

VERS. 4.-- I have finished the work. That is, as if he said openly of what is to come, I will glorify [thee], by preaching to the Gentiles. I will finish the work, because it is already done by predestination; 416Band what most certainly is to be is to be held as already done. This predestination he opens more manifestly in his own glorification, saying: Which I had with thee before the world was; that is, as I then had it with thee in predestination before the times, so now in reality make it in the world in time. Thou hast given me, etc. Wherein grace is noted. For he has nothing in the man which he has not received.

VERS. 5.-- To men. All these things can be said of all the faithful who are to be. And this is that glorification by which the Son, being glorified, glorifies the Father upon earth, according to which the past stands for the future.

VERS. 6.-- They were thine. (AUG.) These were the Father's, as all things are, etc., as far as because he was not yet man.

VERS. 7.-- They have known. Lest the knowledge 416Cshould be thought already made through sight, he adds: They have believed truly; not in that manner in which it was when he said, Now you believe, the hour cometh that you be scattered. But not even yet were they such; rather he foretells what they will be when the Spirit has been received, without whom they did not even keep the word which he had spoken.

VERS. 9.-- I pray for them. The first prayer was: Glorify the Son, that the Son may glorify thee, and he pursued these things and concluded in the faith of believers. Now he prays for them, that the Father may keep those whom he himself has left in body.

VERS. 10.-- And all mine. For the Father did not lose because he gave to the Son, since the Son further adds: All mine thine and thine mine are. Of the only-begotten Son are all the things that are the Father's, through this, that he himself is also God, and, being also born of the Father, is equal to the Father.

416DVERS. 11.-- Keep them. That they may be one in their own nature, as we also in ours are one; and therefore he does not say, that they may be one with us, or that we and they may be one as we are one, because they are not of the same nature with God, as the Son, according to that which he is God, is one with the Father.

VERS. 12.-- When I was. We ought not to understand that the Father and the Son keep alternately, as it were the one after the other. For the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, who is one God, keep together. But Scripture does not lift us up unless it comes down to us. When it speaks thus, let us understand that we are to distinguish the persons, not to separate the nature. The joy which he expressed above, that they may be one as we also, this is that peace and blessedness in 417Athe world to come, for the sake of which all things are done, for the sake of which he says that he has spoken in the world.

VERS. 17.-- Sanctify. As from thy grace the sanctification was begun through which they are now not of the world, by the same grace make them holier, that they may persevere in the truth, of which the ancient sanctifications were a shadow; that is, in me who am the truth, which he opens by subjoining: Thy word is truth, which is, I am the truth. In Greek λόγος, in Latin verbum or sermo, who is the only-begotten of the Father.

VERS. 19.-- And for them I sanctify. [Vulg. Jo 17:19 has the future sanctificabo, "I will sanctify"; the gloss that follows expounds the future.] That is, that it may profit for them, I will sanctify myself as man in myself the Word, which is from the beginning of his Incarnation, when the Word was made flesh. And it profits them in this way, because I also sanctify in myself those who are I, since head and body are one Christ, 417Bwhence he adds: that they also may be, as I am, sanctified in truth and in me.

VERS. 20.-- Not for them. He now begins not concerning the apostles alone, or concerning the other members: but he does not pray for those who have already died in his faith, but for those who are in temptations. But also for those who. Even unto the end of the world, or [those who] already believe wherever they are, yet not so perfectly as they are to believe afterwards after the resurrection. Through the word. Through the word of the Gospel, which is theirs, not that it was preached through them only, but also through Paul and any others whatever, but because it was committed to them first and principally to be preached. This is the word of faith, the word of God, through which the thief believed, Paul believed, and whosoever believes.

417CVERS. 21.-- That all may be one. He does not say, We all are one, but that they all may be one, as thou, Father, in me and I in thee, understand, we are one: so also the Father [is] in the Son and the Son in the Father, that they may be one, because of one substance. But we can be in the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit. Or the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit can be in us; yet we cannot be one with God, because we are not of one substance. But God is in us as in a temple, we in him as a creature in the Creator. That they also. He says, in us, that what they are one by faith and charity they may ascribe to the grace of God, but that now light in the Lord.

That the world. The world is here taken for all those who are one. Nor does this, that they are, make a cause that they should believe, because rather by believing they are one; although 417Dthey were one by nature, yet by dissenting from the One they were not one; but by praying he said that the world may believe, as by praying he said that all may be one, as if a third time it should be understood, I pray that the world may believe.

VERS. 24.-- Whom thou hast given. That is, thou hast drawn them to me, so I also have chosen them out of the world: others are not given to him, are not his, although the power over all be his. I will. What the Almighty wills cannot fail to be done; but that is the will of the man, concerning which he says: Not as I will (Matth. XXVI). That they may see. When they shall have seen him, in the form of a servant made glorious, judging the good and the evil, the impious shall be taken away, that he may not see the glory of God whereby God is, which the clean of heart alone shall see, which will be to them life everlasting.

418AVERS. 26.-- That the love. The love with which the Father loves the Son is in all the just, because they are his members and are loved in him who is wholly loved; therefore he subjoins: And I am in them. As if he should say: Because I am in them, he is in us in one way, as in a temple, we in him in another way; because we are he, since according to the man we are the body of his head.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Ubi erat. In horto capitur ut deleret peccatum commissum in horto deliciarum, in quem hujus saeculi torrentem transiens discipulos introduxit.

VERS. 3.-- Cohortem. Non Judaeorum, sed militum a praeside servato ordine legitimae potestatis, ut 418Billis tenentibus nullus auderet resistere; vel etiam si quis auderet, contra tot non posset. Venit illuc. Nocte traditur, ut sine turbis, quae in die frequentes aderant, inveniretur.

VERS. 5.-- Ego sum. Hac una voce tot feroces et fortes moriturus prostravit virtute latentis Deitatis: quia volebat quidem comprehendi, sed non nisi quando voluit.

VERS. 6.-- Abierunt retrorsum et ceciderunt in terram. Sic modo, per Evangelium ubique dicit Christus, Ego sum, et Judaei exspectantes Antichristum retro abeunt et in terrena, quae amant, cadunt.

VERS. 8.-- Sinite hos abire. (AUG.) Faciunt quod jubet, sinunt eos abire quos non vult perire, non quin post sint morituri, sed quia sic nondum credebant 418Cquomodo credunt qui non pereunt.

VERS. 11.-- Mitte. Acsi diceret: Quiescat vindicta. Aliter: Converte gladium in locum suum, id est verbum praedicationis converte ad gentes, ubi secundum Paulum ostium apertum et ingens inveniatur. Aliter, postquam plenitudo subintraverit, dicit Petro, per quem totus ordo praedicatorum designatur: Mitte verbum praedicationis ad Israel. Omnes enim qui gladium acceperint gladio peribunt (Matth. XXVI). Quo gladio? Illo qui igneus vertitur ante paradisum: et gladio spiritus, qui in Dei scribitur armatura.

VERS. 12.-- Cohors autem. Mos erat Judaeis, ut 418Dquem morte dignum judicarent vinctum praesidi traderent.

VERS. 13.-- Et adduxerunt eum. Erant eo tempore duo sacerdotum principes (ut Lucas ait), Annas et Caiphas, qui vicissim annos suos agebant. Sed tunc erat annus Caiphae, et voluntate ejus primum ductus est Jesus ad Annam, non quia collega sed quia socer ejus erat. Vel domus sic erant positae, ut non deberet Annas a transeuntibus praeteriri.

VERS. 15.-- Et alius discipulus. Quis sit iste, quia hic tacetur, non temere definiatur. Solet tamen iste Joannes sic se significare.

VERS. 17.-- Dicit ille: Non sum. (AUG.) Non solum negat Christum qui dicit eum non esse Christum, sed qui etiam negat se esse Christianum, et 419Aita hoc impletur quod Dominus dixit: Ter me negabis (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 21.-- Quid me interrogas? Id est, a quo veritatem audire non desideras. Interroga magis eos de quorum dictis non habeas invidiam. Ita temperat responsionem, ut nec veritatem taceret, nec defendere se videretur.

VERS. 22.-- Haec autem. Objicitur, cur non fecerit quod docuit. Debuit non sic respondere, sed alteram maxillam praebere. Sed veraciter et mansuete et juste respondit, et non modo maxillam, sed totum corpus ferienti paravit. Et hinc potius ostendit praecepta patientiae, non ostentatione corporis, sed cordis praeparatione facienda, potest enim quis maxillam visibiliter praebere iratus. Melius ergo respondet 419Bvere placatus, et ad ferenda graviora tranquillo animo paratus.

VERS. 23.-- Si male locutus. Male loquitur qui contra veritatem loquitur. Si male locutus sum, da testimonium quo probes mendacem.

VERS. 25.-- Erat autem. (AUG.) Dicto, quod Annas misit Jesum ad Caipham, redit ad locum narrationis ubi reliquerat Petrum, ut explicet quid in domo Annae de trina Petri negatione contigerat.

VERS. 28.-- In praetorium. Id est, ubi praeses Pilatus habitabat. Sed si ad Caipham, quomodo in praetorium? forsitan, aliqua causa urgente, de domo Annae ad audiendum Jesum ambo convenerant. 419CCaiphas perrexit ad praetorium praesidis, et socero suo Jesum reliquit ad audiendum; vel in domo Caiphae Pilatus acceperat praetorium, et tanta erat domus, ut seorsum habitaret dominus, seorsum Pilatus. Ut non contaminarentur. Quia diebus azymorum, contaminatio erat illis in alienigenae habitaculum intrare.

VERS. 31.-- Nobis non, etc. Hoc dicunt propter diei festi sanctitatem. Lex enim praecipit, ne parcant malefactoribus, praesertim seducentibus a Deo suo, qualem istum dicebant esse; et contra seipsos dicunt, quia interficiebant quem interficiendum offerebant, sicut et Pilatus interfecit, non tamen manibus suis. Cum Pilatus vellet Jesum tradere Judaeis, ut secundum legem suam judicarent eum, 419Dnoluerunt recipere, dicentes: Nobis non licet interficere quemquam; et sic impletur sermo Jesu quem de sua morte praedixit, ut eum a Judaeis traditum gentes interficerent, quasi sic essent alieni a scelere qui magis peccant, in quo non eorum innocentia, sed dementia monstratur.

VERS. 34.-- A temetipso hoc dicis, etc. Scit Jesus quid ipse interrogat et quid ille responsurus est, sed tamen dici voluit non ut sciret, sed ut scriberetur quod scire voluit.

VERS. 35.-- Quid fecisti. Quasi: Si haec culpa non est, quod te regem dixeris, quid aliud fecisti? Quasi non est mirum si judici traderis puniendus qui te regem dicis. Sed si haec causa non esset, tunc 420Aforte alia quaerenda esset; quasi: confirma si haec causa est quae objicitur, an alia.

VERS. 36.-- Regnum. Hoc est quod nos scire voluit, sed prius vicissim interrogando voluit ostendere quae esset de regno suo hominum opinio, Judaeorum vel gentilium, qua ostensa utrisque aptius respondet: Regnum meum non est de hoc mundo. Quasi, decepti estis; non enim impedio dominationem vestram in hoc mundo, ne vane timeatis et serviatis, sed ad regnum coeleste credendo venire.

VERS. 37.-- Tu dicis. Non se regem timet confiteri, sed ita liberatum est, ut neque se regem neget, nec de mundo se fateatur esse, ut ille sentiebat qui dixit: Ergo rex es tu? Cui respondit: 420BTu dicis, quia rex sum ego. Quasi dicat, carnalis carnaliter dicis.

VERS. 38.-- Iterum. Non exspectat responsum, ne cum vellet dimittere mora fieret, subito enim venit in mentem consuetudo qua posset per Pascha dimitti.

VERS. 39.-- Est autem. Quia in Pascha a servitute liberati sunt, in Pascha haec consuetudo dimittendi eis fuit.

VERS. 40.-- Erat autem Barabbas latro. Id est filius magistri, id est diaboli, qui latroni in suo scelere Judaeis in perfidia magister fuit. Vel Barabbas, id est filius patris, id est diaboli.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Where there was. He is taken in a garden, that he might blot out the sin committed in the garden of delights; and into that garden, passing over the torrent of this world, he led his disciples in.

VERS. 3.-- A cohort. Not of the Jews, but of soldiers from the governor, the order of lawful power being preserved, so that 418Bwhile they held him none might dare to resist; or even if any should dare, he could not prevail against so many. He came thither. He is betrayed by night, that he might be found without the crowds, which in the daytime were often present.

VERS. 5.-- I am he. By this one word he who was to die laid low so many fierce and strong men, by the power of the Godhead lying hidden: because he willed indeed to be seized, but not save when he willed.

VERS. 6.-- They went backward and fell to the ground. So now Christ says everywhere through the Gospel, I am he, and the Jews, awaiting Antichrist, go backward and fall into the earthly things which they love.

VERS. 8.-- Let these go their way. (AUG.) They do what he commands; they let those go whom he wills not to perish: not but that they should die afterwards, but because they did not yet believe 418Cas those believe who do not perish.

VERS. 11.-- Put up. As if he said: Let vengeance rest. Otherwise: Turn thy sword into its own place, that is, turn the word of preaching to the Gentiles, where, according to Paul, a door open and great may be found. Otherwise, after the fulness shall have come in, he says to Peter, by whom the whole order of preachers is designated: Send the word of preaching to Israel. For all that shall take the sword shall perish by the sword (Matth. XXVI). By what sword? By that fiery one which is turned before paradise: and by the sword of the spirit, which is written of in the armour of God.

VERS. 12.-- And the cohort. It was the custom with the Jews that 418Dwhomsoever they judged worthy of death they delivered bound to the governor.

VERS. 13.-- And they led him away. There were at that time two chief priests (as Luke says), Annas and Caiphas, who held their years by turns. But then it was the year of Caiphas, and by his will Jesus was first led to Annas, not because he was his colleague but because he was his father-in-law. Or the houses were so placed that Annas ought not to be passed by by those going by.

VERS. 15.-- And another disciple. Who this may be, since it is here kept silent, let it not be rashly determined. Yet this John is accustomed so to signify himself.

VERS. 17.-- He saith: I am not. (AUG.) Not only does he deny Christ who says that he is not the Christ, but he also who denies that he is a Christian; and 419Aso is fulfilled that which the Lord said: Thou shalt deny me thrice (Matth. XXVI).

VERS. 21.-- Why askest thou me? That is, from whom thou desirest not to hear the truth. Ask rather those of whose sayings thou mayest have no ill-will. So he tempers his answer that he neither kept the truth silent, nor seemed to be defending himself.

VERS. 22.-- And when he had said these things. It is objected, why he did not do what he taught. He ought not to have answered thus, but to have offered the other cheek. But he answered truthfully and meekly and justly, and made ready for the smiter not his cheek only, but his whole body. And hence he rather showed that the precepts of patience are to be fulfilled not by an ostentation of the body, but by a preparation of the heart; for a man can offer his cheek visibly and be angry. Better, therefore, does he answer 419Bwho is truly appeased, and with a tranquil mind ready to bear heavier things.

VERS. 23.-- If I have spoken ill. He speaks ill who speaks against the truth. If I have spoken ill, give testimony whereby thou mayest prove me a liar.

VERS. 25.-- And there was. (AUG.) Having said that Annas sent Jesus to Caiphas, he returns to the place of the narrative where he had left Peter, that he may unfold what had befallen in the house of Annas concerning Peter's threefold denial.

VERS. 28.-- Into the hall. That is, where Pilate the governor dwelt. But if to Caiphas, how into the hall? perhaps, some cause pressing, both had come together from the house of Annas to hear Jesus. 419CCaiphas went on to the governor's hall, and left Jesus to his father-in-law to be heard; or in the house of Caiphas Pilate had taken his hall, and the house was so great that the master dwelt apart and Pilate apart. That they might not be defiled. Because in the days of unleavened bread it was a defilement to them to enter into the dwelling of a stranger.

VERS. 31.-- It is not lawful for us, etc. This they say because of the holiness of the feast day. For the law commands that they spare not evildoers, especially such as seduce men from their God, which they said this man was; and they speak against themselves, because they were killing him whom they were offering up to be killed, as Pilate also killed, yet not with his own hands. When Pilate wished to deliver Jesus to the Jews, that according to their own law they might judge him, 419Dthey refused to receive him, saying: It is not lawful for us to put any man to death; and so is fulfilled the word of Jesus which he foretold concerning his own death, that, being delivered up by the Jews, the Gentiles should kill him, as though they were thus strangers to the crime who sin the more; wherein not their innocence but their madness is shown.

VERS. 34.-- Sayest thou this of thyself, etc. Jesus knows what he himself asks and what the other will answer, but yet he willed it to be said, not that he might know, but that what he willed to know might be written.

VERS. 35.-- What hast thou done. As if: If this be no fault, that thou hast called thyself king, what else hast thou done? As if: it is no wonder if thou art delivered to a judge to be punished, thou who callest thyself king. But if this were not the cause, then 420Aperhaps another must be sought; as if: confirm whether this be the cause which is objected, or another.

VERS. 36.-- A kingdom. This is what he willed us to know; but first, by asking in his turn, he willed to show what was the opinion of men concerning his kingdom, of Jews or of Gentiles; and this being shown, he answers more fitly to both: My kingdom is not of this world.
