As if: ye are deceived; for I hinder not your dominion in this world, that ye may not vainly fear and be in servitude, but to come by believing to the heavenly kingdom.

VERS. 37.-- Thou sayest. He fears not to confess himself a king, but it is so set free that he neither denies himself a king, nor confesses himself to be of the world, as he supposed who said: Art thou a king then? To whom he answered: 420BThou sayest that I am a king. As if he should say, being carnal thou speakest carnally.

VERS. 38.-- Again. He waits not for an answer, lest, when he wished to release him, there should be delay; for suddenly there came into his mind the custom whereby one might be released at the Pasch.

VERS. 39.-- But there is. Because at the Pasch they were freed from servitude, at the Pasch this custom of releasing was theirs.

VERS. 40.-- Now Barabbas was a robber. That is, son of a master, that is, of the devil, who was a master to the robber in his crime, to the Jews in their treachery. Or Barabbas, that is, son of the father, that is, of the devil.

CAPUT XIX.

420CVERS. 1.-- Tunc ergo. Hoc fecit Pilatus, id est, milites facere permisit, vel etiam jussit ut Judaei satiati de poenis a morte desisterent.

VERS. 2.-- Coronam de spinis imposuerunt. In spinea corona nostrorum notatur susceptio peccatorum, pro qua mortalis fierit voluit. Unde praecursor: Ecce Agnus Dei, ecce qui tollit peccata mundi. Spinae namque in peccatorum significatione ponuntur. Unde Dominus: Terra tua spinas et tribulos germinabit (Gen. III). Id est, conscientia tua punctiones, et aculeos vitiorum procreare non desistet.

VERS. 4.-- Exivit ergo. Ecce implentur quae de se praedixerat Jesus occulta potentia, commendatur patientia qua informantur martyres.

420DVERS. 5.-- Et dicit eis. Hic apparet non ignorante Pilato haec a militibus esse facta, sive jusserit, sive permiserit, ut satiatis Judaeis haec sufficerent, sed magis inardescunt et clamant: Crucifige, crucifige.

VERS. 7.-- Filium Dei. Ecce altera major invidia, quia parva videbatur illicita affectatio regni, et tamen utrumque vere erat Christus, et rex et Filius Dei.

VERS. 9.-- Jesus autem. Hic et in aliis locis legitur siluisse Christus ut prophetia impleatur: Sicut agnus coram tondente sine voce, sic non aperuit os suum (Isa. LIII). Quae similitudo de agno data est, ut in suo silentio non reus sed innocens habeatur. 421ANon enim ut conscius convincitur, sed ut mansuetus pro aliis immolatur.

VERS. 12.-- Et exinde. Id est, ex hac causa, ne haberet peccatum occidendi innocentem (et si minus illis invidis) quaerebat dimittere, non quod tunc primum quaereret, sed si prius volebat modo quaerebat.

Si hunc dimittis, non es amicus. Supra legem proposuerunt, secundum quam deberet mori, quia Filium Dei se fecit: sed quia ille legem gentis alienae non timet, sed magis timet occidere Filium Dei, terrent eum a Caesare, quem non potest ut auctorem potestatis suae contemnere.

VERS. 14.-- Erat autem parasceve. (AUG.) Parasceve quod interpretatur praeparatio, etc., usque 421Bad et eadem est sexta praeparationis.

VERS. 15.-- Regem vestrum crucifigam? Adhuc terror quem de Caesare ingerunt superare conatur, dicens: Regem vestrum crucifigam? De ignominia eorum volens eos frangere, quos de ignominia Christi non poterat mitigare.

VERS. 16.-- Tunc ergo. Quia profitentur Caesarem aperte contra eum esse si alium regem velit eis ingerere, dimittendo impunitum quem propter hos ausus tradiderant occidendum. Victus ergo timore, tradidit eis Jesum ut crucifigatur. Tradidit eis Jesum ut crucifigeretur. Eis, non quod ipsi manibus eum acciperent, qui supra hoc excusaverant, sed ut crimine impliciti videantur a quo alieni esse conantur, cum non nisi pro eis hoc faceret Pilatus, 421Cunde nec ait ut crucifigerent, sed ut crucifigeretur judicio et potestate Pilati.

VERS. 17.-- Bajulans. Portabat crucem suam Jesus. Grande ludibrium impiis, grande mysterium piis gloriantibus, quasi in sceptro regni, et est crux candelabrum lucernae quae non erat sub modio ponenda. Primo Dominus crucem portat, quia prior passus est; postea imposita est Simoni Cyrenaeo, quia debemus sequi vestigia.

VERS. 18.-- Et cum eo. Latro qui permansit in perfidia significat Judaeos; qui confitetur, gentes significat.

VERS. 19.-- Et erat scriptum. Hae tres linguae caeteris eminebant: Hebraea propter Judaeos in lege 421DDei gloriantes; Graeca propter gentium sapientes; Latina propter Romanos tunc pene omnibus gentibus imperantes.

VERS. 23.-- Milites ergo cum crucifixissent eum. Ecce quae gesta sunt juxta crucem, cum jam crucifixus esset.

Quatuor. Quadripertita vestis signat Ecclesiam toto orbe, qui quatuor partibus constat diffusam omnibus partibus aequaliter, id est concorditer distributam. Tunica sortita omnium partium significat unitatem, quae charitatis vinculo continetur. Desuper. Quia charitas supereminet omnibus per totum, quia nemo est ejus expers qui pertinere videatur ad totum, a quo toto, sicut Graeca indicat 422Alingua, catholica vocatur Ecclesia, quae in sorte Dei gratia commendatur.

VERS. 26.-- Cum vidisset ergo Jesus matrem et discipulum stantem quem diligebat. Qui divina facturus, quasi repulit ignotam matrem; jam ostendit venisse horam, quam tunc praedixit, in qua agnoscit de qua fuerat mortaliter natus. Et docet ut a piis filiis impendatur cura parentibus, unde et Apostolus: Si quis suorum curam non habet, fidem negat (I Tim. V), etc.

VERS. 27.-- Et ex illa hora accepit eam discipulus. Qui etsi jam non haberet propria, in illa societate centupliciter acceperat, ubi erant omnia communia, nihil habens et omnia possidens.

CHAPTER XIX.

420CVERS. 1.-- Then therefore. This Pilate did, that is, he permitted the soldiers to do it, or even commanded it, that the Jews, being sated with the punishments, might desist from his death.

VERS. 2.-- They put on a crown of thorns. In the crown of thorns is marked the taking up of our sins, for which he willed to become mortal. Whence the forerunner: Behold the Lamb of God, behold him who taketh away the sins of the world. [Vulg. Jo. 1:29 has the singular, "the sin of the world"] For thorns are set down in the signification of sins. Whence the Lord: Thy earth shall bring forth thorns and thistles (Gen. III). [Vulg. Gen. 3:18 has no "thy earth," and reads "shall bring forth thorns and thistles to thee"] That is, thy conscience shall not cease to bring forth prickings and the stings of vices.

VERS. 4.-- He went forth therefore. Behold, there are fulfilled the things which Jesus had foretold of himself by his hidden power; his patience is commended, by which the martyrs are formed.

420DVERS. 5.-- And he saith to them. Here it appears that these things were done by the soldiers not without Pilate's knowledge, whether he commanded or permitted them, that, the Jews being sated, these things might suffice; but they burn the more and cry out: Crucify, crucify.

VERS. 7.-- The Son of God. Behold another and greater ill-will, because the unlawful aspiring to a kingdom seemed a small thing; and yet Christ was truly both, both king and Son of God.

VERS. 9.-- But Jesus. Here and in other places Christ is read to have kept silence, that the prophecy might be fulfilled: As a lamb before his shearer, without voice, so he opened not his mouth (Isa. LIII). [Vulg. Isa. 53:7 reads "and as a lamb before his shearer shall be dumb, and he shall not open his mouth"; the wording printed here is that of Act. 8:32] Which likeness is taken from the lamb, that in his silence he may be held not guilty but innocent. 421AFor he is not convicted as one conscious of guilt, but as a meek one he is sacrificed for others.

VERS. 12.-- And from thenceforth. That is, from this cause, lest he should have the sin of slaying an innocent man (and if less so to those envious men), he sought to release him: not that he then first sought it, but if before he wished it, now he sought it.

If thou release this man, thou art not a friend. Above they set forth the law, according to which he ought to die, because he made himself the Son of God: but because that man fears not the law of a foreign nation, and rather fears to slay the Son of God, they frighten him with Caesar, whom he cannot despise as the author of his own power.

VERS. 14.-- And it was the parasceve. (AUG.) Parasceve, which is interpreted preparation, etc., down 421Bto and the same is the sixth of the preparation.

VERS. 15.-- Shall I crucify your king? Still he tries to overcome the terror which they press upon him concerning Caesar, saying: Shall I crucify your king? Wishing to break them by their own shame, whom by the shame of Christ he could not soften.

VERS. 16.-- Then therefore. Because they openly declare that Caesar is against him if he wish to press another king upon them, by releasing unpunished the man whom on account of these presumptions they had delivered to be slain. Overcome therefore by fear, he delivered Jesus to them that he might be crucified. He delivered Jesus to them to be crucified. To them: not that they should themselves take him with their hands, who above had excused themselves from this, but that they may be seen implicated in the crime from which they try to be strangers, since Pilate did this for none but them; 421Cwhence he does not say that they should crucify him, but that he should be crucified by the judgment and power of Pilate.

VERS. 17.-- Bearing. Jesus was bearing his own cross. A great mockery to the impious, a great mystery to the pious who glory in it, as in the sceptre of a kingdom; and the cross is the candlestick of the lamp which was not to be placed under a bushel. First the Lord bears the cross, because he suffered first; afterwards it was laid upon Simon of Cyrene, because we ought to follow in his steps.

VERS. 18.-- And with him. The robber who remained in unbelief signifies the Jews; he who confesses signifies the Gentiles.

VERS. 19.-- And it was written. These three tongues were eminent above the rest: Hebrew, because of the Jews glorying in the law 421Dof God; Greek, because of the wise men of the nations; Latin, because of the Romans, who then ruled over almost all nations.

VERS. 23.-- The soldiers therefore, when they had crucified him. Behold what things were done beside the cross, when he was now crucified.

Four. The fourfold garment signifies the Church spread through the whole world, which consists of four parts, distributed equally to all its parts, that is, harmoniously. The tunic assigned by lot signifies the unity of all the parts, which is held together by the bond of charity. From above. Because charity is eminent above all things throughout the whole, because no one is without it who is seen to belong to the whole; from which whole, as the Greek 422Atongue indicates, the Church is called catholic, which in the lot is commended by the grace of God.

VERS. 26.-- When Jesus therefore had seen his mother and the disciple standing whom he loved. He who, being about to do divine things, as it were repelled his mother as one unknown, now shows that the hour has come which he then foretold, in which he acknowledges her of whom he had been mortally born. And he teaches that care is to be bestowed by dutiful sons upon their parents; whence the Apostle also: If any man have not care of his own, he denieth the faith (I Tim. V), etc.

VERS. 27.-- And from that hour the disciple took her. Who, although he had now nothing of his own, had received a hundredfold in that fellowship where all things were common, having nothing and possessing all things.

CAPUT XX.

  • 422B

VERS. 4.-- Currebant autem. Joannes significat Synagogam, quae prior venit ad monumentum sed non intravit, quia prophetias de incarnatione et passione audivit, sed et mortuum credere noluit. Petrus Ecclesiam, quae cognovit carne mortuum, viventem credidit Deum, post quem et in Judaea in fine intrabit.

VERS. 7.-- Separatur. Quia sacramenta Divinitatis incomprehensibilia a nostrae infirmitatis cognitione remota sunt. In involuto, nec finis nec initium aspicitur. Sic celsitudo Divinitatis nec coepit esse, nec desinit. In unum locum. Quia in scissura mentium Deus non est, sed in unitate. Vel, sudarium capitis passio Christi, cujus sacramenta infidelibus 422Csunt occultata seorsum, quia longe a nostra, quia ille sponte et sine culpa passus, nos inviti cum culpa.

VERS. 9.-- Nondum enim. Scilicet, usque adeo, ut cum ab ipso Domino aperte diceretur propter consuetudinem ab eo audiendi parabolas, non intelligerent.

VERS. 11.-- Plorans. Jam plus de sublato quam de occiso plorat, quia tanti magistri cujus vita subtracta fuerat, nec memoria remanebat et iste dolor ibi eam tenebat. Dum ergo, etc. Cum eum sciret ibi non esse, sed quia nimis dolet, nec suis oculis, nec oculis illorum duorum credentium putavit. Vel potius divino instinctu hoc factum est.

VERS. 12.-- Vidit duos. Angeli, id est nuntii, significant 422DEvangelium Christi a capite usque ad pedes, id est ab initio usque ad finem esse nuntiandum. Et ille ex passione sua erat nuntiandus, quia Deus est ante saecula, et homo in fine saeculi. Sedet ergo angelus ad caput, cum dicit: In principio erat Verbum. Ad pedes, cum dicit: Verbum caro factum est. Vel duo angeli duo Testamenta, unum prius, aliud sequens. Conjuncta dum pari sensu incarnatum, mortuum, resuscitatum nuntiant.

VERS. 13.-- Quid ploras? Quasi dicat: Noli peccare. Prohibent lacrymas quodammodo nuntiantes gaudium. At illa putans eos interrogare nescientes, prodit causam lacrymarum: Quia tulerunt Dominum meum, id est corpus Domini mei.

423AVERS. 14.-- Et non sciebat quia Jesus, etc. Quia tergum ad eum habebat quem non resurrexisse credebat; quia amabat et dubitabat, videbat et non cognoscebat.

VERS. 15.-- Quem quaeris? Illa existimans quia hortulanus esset, dicit ei. Non quod dubitet quem requireret, sed quia illa quem quaerit ignoret, non enim quaerit Christum, sed quem putat raptum. Domine. Quem putat hortulanum dicit Dominum, ut honorificaret hominem a quo rogabat beneficium. Nec sic dicit eum Dominum, sicut illum quem quaerit sublatum. Jesum vero agnoscens vocat magistrum, quia recolit eum a quo discernere humana et divina discebat. Si tu sustulisti eum. Non dicit quem, quia hoc in animo agit vis amoris, ut quem semper cogitat 423Bnullum alium ignorare credat.

VERS. 16.-- Maria. Conversa illa dicit ei: Rabboni, quod dicitur magister. Dicit ei Jesus. Postquam communi nomine vocavit, et non est agnitus, vocat ex nomine, ac si dicat, Recognosce eum a quo recognosceris.

VERS. 18.-- Venit Maria Magdalenae annuntians discipulis, quia vidit Dominum, etc. Ibi culpa abscinditur undet processit. Mulier mortem viro propinavit, modo mulier vitam nuntiat viris, et quae tunc verba serpentis modo narrat verba vivificatoris, ac si ipsis rebus dicat Deus: De qua manu illatus est potus mortis, de ipsa suscipite potum vitae.

VERS. 19.-- Cum ergo, etc. Hoc ipso in die resurrectionis factum est, in quo quinquies Jesus apparuit. 423CPrimo Mariae Magdalenae, secundo eidem cum aliis currenti nuntiare discipulis volentibus, tertio Petro, quarto Cleophae et socio ejus, quinto discipulis januis clausis praedictis duobus jam reversis.

VERS. 20.-- Ostendit eis manus et latus. Post resurrectionem in corpore suo Dominus duo contraria ostendit, et palpabile ejusdem naturae ut formet ad fidem, et incorruptibile alterius gloriae ut invitet ad praemium.

VERS. 21.-- Dixit ergo eis iterum. Pax vobis, etc. Pacem offert, qui propter pacem venit, iterat, ut monstret per suum sanguinem esse pacificata quae in coelis sunt, et quae in terra.

VERS. 22.-- Accipite Spiritum sanctum. (AUG.) In terra datur spiritus ut diligatur proximus, e coelo 423Ddatur Spiritus, ut diligatur Deus.

VERS. 23.-- Quorum remiseritis peccata. Ecce charitas, quae per Spiritum sanctum infunditur cordibus eorum. Qui participes sui sunt, peccata dimittit; eorum qui non sunt, tenet. Et ideo postquam dixit: Accipite Spiritum sanctum, continuo subjecit: Quorum remiseritis peccata.

VERS. 24.-- Thomas autem unus. Non casu, sed divina dispositione defuit Thomas et audita non credidit; unde palpandum se ei Dominus praebuit, quia ejus dubitatione et facta sibi probatione nulli relinquitur jam dubitare. Qui dicitur Didymus. Didymus, id est geminus, quia dubius Thomas, id est abyssus: quia dubitando profundius novit.

424AVERS. 28.-- Respondit. Videbat et tangebat hominem, et super hoc Deum credebat jam remota dubitatione, et confitebatur quem non videbat.

VERS. 29.-- Quia vidisti me, Thoma, credidisti. Non ait tetigisti, sed vidisti: quia visus quodam modo generalis sensus est qui de aliis quatuor dici solet. Forsitan non fuit ausus tangere; sed aspiciendo tantum, seu etiam tangendo credit.

Beati. Commendat fidem gentium, sed praeterito utitur, ut qui quod erat futurum, in sua praedestinatione noverat jam factum.

CHAPTER XX.

  • 422B

VERS. 4.-- And they ran. John signifies the Synagogue, which came first to the tomb but did not enter, because she heard the prophecies concerning the incarnation and the passion, but refused to believe him even dead. Peter signifies the Church, which knew him dead in the flesh and believed him living God; after whom she too, in Judaea, will enter at the end.

VERS. 7.-- It is separated. Because the sacraments of the Godhead are incomprehensible and set far from the knowledge of our weakness. In a thing wrapped up, neither end nor beginning is seen. So the loftiness of the Godhead neither began to be, nor ceases. Into one place. Because God is not in the sundering of minds, but in unity. Or, the napkin of the head is the passion of Christ, whose sacraments are hidden 422Capart from unbelievers, because it is far from ours, because he suffered willingly and without fault, we unwillingly and with fault.

VERS. 9.-- For as yet. That is, to such a degree that, although it was openly said by the Lord himself, because of their custom of hearing parables from him, they did not understand.

VERS. 11.-- Weeping. She now weeps more for him taken away than for him slain, because of so great a master, whose life had been taken from her, not even the memory remained, and this grief held her there. While therefore, etc. Although she knew that he was not there, yet because she grieves too much, she gave no thought either to her own eyes or to the eyes of those two who believed. Or rather this was done by divine prompting.

VERS. 12.-- She saw two. The angels, that is, messengers, signify 422Dthat the Gospel of Christ is to be announced from the head even to the feet, that is, from the beginning even to the end. And he was to be announced from his passion, because he is God before the ages, and man at the end of the age. The angel therefore sits at the head when he says: In the beginning was the Word. At the feet, when he says: The Word was made flesh. Or the two angels are the two Testaments, the one former, the other following. Joined together, while with equal sense they announce him incarnate, dead, raised again.

VERS. 13.-- Why weepest thou? As if he should say: Sin not [peccare; read plorare, "Do not weep" -- the next sentence says what is forbidden is her tears]. They forbid her tears, in a manner announcing joy. But she, thinking that they asked as not knowing, brings forth the cause of her tears: Because they have taken away my Lord, that is, the body of my Lord.

423AVERS. 14.-- And she knew not that it was Jesus, etc. Because she had her back toward him whom she believed not to have risen; because she loved and doubted, she saw and did not know him.

VERS. 15.-- Whom seekest thou? She, thinking that it was the gardener, saith to him. Not that he doubted whom she was seeking, but because she knows not whom she seeks; for she seeks not Christ, but him whom she thinks carried off. Lord. Him whom she thinks a gardener she calls Lord, that she might honour the man of whom she was asking a kindness. Nor does she call him Lord in the same way as she calls him whom she seeks as taken away. But recognizing Jesus she calls him master, because she calls to mind him from whom she was learning to distinguish things human and divine. If thou hast taken him away. She does not say whom, because the force of love works this in the mind, that of him whom she always thinks she believes 423Bno other to be ignorant.

VERS. 16.-- Mary. She turning saith to him: Rabboni, which is to say master. Jesus saith to her. After he had called her by the common name, and was not recognized, he calls her by her name, as if he should say, Know again him by whom thou art known again.

VERS. 18.-- Mary of Magdalene came announcing to the disciples that she had seen the Lord, etc. There the fault is cut off undet it proceeded. A woman gave death to a man to drink; now a woman announces life to men; and she who then [told] the words of the serpent now relates the words of the Life-giver; as if God should say by the very facts: From what hand the draught of death was brought in, from that same hand receive ye the draught of life.

VERS. 19.-- When therefore, etc. This was done on the very day of the resurrection, on which Jesus appeared five times. 423CFirst to Mary Magdalene, secondly to the same woman running with the others who wished to announce it to the disciples, thirdly to Peter, fourthly to Cleophas and his companion, fifthly to the disciples, the doors being shut, the two aforesaid having now returned.

VERS. 20.-- He shewed them his hands and his side. After the resurrection the Lord showed in his body two contrary things: both what was palpable, of the same nature, that he might form them to faith, and what was incorruptible, of another glory, that he might invite them to the reward.

VERS. 21.-- He said therefore to them again. Peace be to you, etc. He offers peace, who came for the sake of peace; he repeats it, that he may show that through his blood the things that are in heaven and the things that are on earth have been made peaceful.

VERS. 22.-- Receive ye the Holy Ghost. (AUG.) On earth the spirit is given that the neighbour may be loved; from heaven 423Dthe Spirit is given that God may be loved.

VERS. 23.-- Whose sins you shall forgive. Behold charity, which is poured into their hearts through the Holy Ghost. He forgives the sins of those who are partakers of him; of those who are not, he retains them. And therefore after he had said: Receive ye the Holy Ghost, he immediately subjoined: Whose sins you shall forgive.

VERS. 24.-- Now Thomas, one. Not by chance, but by divine disposition was Thomas absent, and he did not believe what he heard; whence the Lord offered himself to him to be handled, because by his doubting, and by the proof made to him, it is now left to no one to doubt. Who is called Didymus. Didymus, that is, twin, because Thomas is doubtful; that is, an abyss: because by doubting he knew more deeply.

424AVERS. 28.-- He answered. He was seeing and touching the man, and above this he believed him God, doubt being now removed, and he confessed him whom he did not see.

VERS. 29.-- Because thou hast seen me, Thomas, thou hast believed. He does not say, thou hast touched, but, thou hast seen: because sight is in a manner the general sense, which is wont to be said of the other four. Perhaps he did not dare to touch; but by looking only, or even by touching, he believes.

Blessed. He commends the faith of the Gentiles, but he uses the past tense, as one who knew that what was to come was already done in his own predestination.

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Postea manifestavit. Relictis omnibus secuti fuerunt Jesum. Quod si post mortem Jesu antequam 424Bredderetur eis vivus, repetissent priorem artem, possent videri ex desperatione hoc fecisse; sed post redditum vivum, post acceptum ejus insufflationis spiritum, post verba ejus: Sicut misit me Pater, et ego mitto vos, subito fiunt quod fuerunt, piscatores, non hominum sed piscium. Sed sciendum est non prohibitos fuisse sua arte licita victum quaerere, apostolatus integritate servata, cum unde viverent non haberent, et sic illis laborantibus adjecit Deus necessaria quae promisit, hic autem penuria eorum disposita est, ut in piscatione tanta exhiberentur sacramenta.

VERS. 14.-- Hoc jam. Non quod ter tantum manifestatus sit, sed ad dies, refert, primo die quo resurrexit, post octo dies, hoc die quando hoc fecit de 424Cpiscibus. Secundum dies ergo manifestationes numerantur. Prima, quotiescunque sit facta, uno die resurrectionis. Secunda, post octo dies, et haec est tertia. In hac tertia manifestatione hoc Evangelium terminatur.

VERS. 15.-- Cum ergo prandissent. Jesus scit quod interrogat an plus diligat, Petrus quod de se novit, dicit, id est quod amat. Et quia de aliis quantum diligant, nescit, ideo utrum plus illis diligat, tacet. Ecce docet, non temere definire de occultis, et priori periculo negationis cautius de se respondit.

Pasce agnos meos. Si me diligis, pasce agnos meos. Non ut tuos gloriam meam in eis quaere, non tuam; mea lucra, non tua, quia dilectio Dei probatur in dilectione proximi. Qui vero opus 424Dpietatis negat proximis, minus Deum diligit. Haec autem charitas ex divina datur gratia. Quod tacite innuit Joannes dum interrogans de amore dicit hic quod non alibi: Simon Joannis, id est obediens Dei gratia, quia quod ardentius caeteris diligit et obedit, non est nisi Dei gratia.

VERS. 17.-- Dicit ei tertio. Interrogat quod sciebat non semel, sed iterum et tertio; et tertio audit a Petro se diligi, et tertio jubet pascere oves. Trinae negationi redditur trina confessio, ne minus amori lingua serviat quam timori. Et sicut fuit judicium timoris negare pastorem, sic officium amoris pascere Domini gregem. Contristatus est Petrus. (AUG.) Etiam remissis peccatis tolerat homo miseriam, in quam tamen 425Aex peccato venit. Productior enim poena est quam culpa, nec parva videretur culpa, si cum illa auferretur poena. Est ideo vel ad demonstrationem debitae miseriae, vel ad emendationem labilis vitae, vel ad exercitationem necessariae patientiae temporaliter tenet hominem poena, etiam quem jam reum non tenet culpa, et haec est horum dierum flenda, sed non reprehendenda conditio. Pasce. Pascere oves est credentes ne deficiant confortare, terrena subsidia, si necesse est, subditis providere, exempla virtutum praebere, adversariis obsistere, peccantes corrigere.

VERS. 18.-- Cum autem senueris. Passurum praedicit qui negaturum praedixerat, hoc jam poterat resurrectione firmatus, quod immature pollicebatur 425Binfirmus. Jam non metuit hujus vitae interitum, quia resurgente Domino vitae alterius praecessit exemplum.

VERS. 19.-- Et cum hoc dixisset. His dictis, coepit Jesus abire, et nondum intellecto quod audivit, Sequere me, coepit Petrus incessu pedum sequi, secutus est et Joannes qui sciebat se diligi.

VERS. 20.-- Quem diligebat Jesus. Diligebat et alios, sed familiarius istum donavit potiore dulcedine sui amoris, quia virgo electus ab ipso virgo permansit, unde et matrem ei commendavit.

(AUG.) Tacito nomine discernitur Joannes a caeteris, etc., usque ad qui virgo est Joannes convenit futurae vitae, ubi neque nubent neque nubentur.

426ASuper pectus. In pectore Jesu sunt omnes thesauri sapientiae et scientiae absconditi, super quod recubuit quem majori caeteris sapientiae et scientiae singularis munere donat, in quo figurabatur quanta arcana divinitate prae caeteris esset scripturus.

VERS. 21.-- Domine, hic autem quid? Quia audierat Petrus se crucifigendum, voluit etiam fratris cognoscere exitum.

VERS. 22.-- Sic eum volo manere, donec veniam. Nolo eum per martyrium consummare, sed exspectare eum placidam carnis suae absolutionem, quando ego veniens recipiam eum in aeternam beatitudinem.

VERS. 23.-- Et non dixit ei. Non est intelligendum quod Joannes non sit mortuus in carne, sed quidquid ante passus, quod ultimum in pace senium 426Bfiniret. Non enim magnum erat dare dilecto non mori, cum dissolvi et esse cum Christo sit multo melius.

VERS. 24.-- Hic est, etc. Hic aperte suam designat personam ex officio, quod noluit designare ex vocabulo.

VERS. 25.-- Nec ipsum arbitror mundum. (AUG.) Non spatio locorum, credendum est mundum capere non posse, etc., usque ad sicut alii tropi, hoc est locutionum modi.

Nec ipsum. Non dicit spatio non posse capi, sed capacitate legentium, quamvis salva fide rerum plerumque verba videantur excedere fidem per hyperbolen.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Afterwards he manifested. Having left all things they had followed Jesus. But if after the death of Jesus, before 424Bhe was restored to them alive, they had taken up their former craft again, they might seem to have done this out of despair; but after he was restored alive, after they had received the spirit of his breathing, after his words: As the Father hath sent me, I also send you, suddenly they become what they had been, fishers, not of men but of fishes. But it is to be known that they were not forbidden to seek their living by their own lawful craft, the integrity of the apostolate being preserved, since they had not whereof to live; and so, as they laboured, God added the necessary things which he had promised; but here their want was disposed, that in the fishing such great sacraments might be displayed.

VERS. 14.-- This now. Not that he was manifested only three times, but he refers it to the days: on the first day, on which he rose again; after eight days; on this day, when he did this concerning 424Cthe fishes. The manifestations therefore are numbered according to the days. The first, however often it was made, on the one day of the resurrection. The second, after eight days; and this is the third. In this third manifestation this Gospel is ended.

VERS. 15.-- When therefore they had dined. Jesus knows what he asks, whether he loves more; Peter says what he knows of himself, that is, that he loves. And because he does not know concerning the others how much they love, therefore he is silent as to whether he loves more than they. Behold, he teaches not to determine rashly about hidden things; and by the earlier peril of his denial he answers of himself more cautiously.

Feed my lambs. If thou love me, feed my lambs. Not as thine own: seek my glory in them, not thine own; my gains, not thine, because the love of God is proved in the love of the neighbour. But he who denies the work 424Dof piety to his neighbours loves God less. And this charity is given out of divine grace. Which John silently hints at when, asking about love, he says here what he does not say elsewhere: Simon, son of John, that is, obedient by the grace of God, because that he loves and obeys more ardently than the rest is nothing save the grace of God.

VERS. 17.-- He saith to him the third time. He asks what he knew, not once, but again and a third time; and a third time he hears from Peter that he is loved, and a third time he bids him feed the sheep. To the threefold denial a threefold confession is rendered, lest the tongue serve love less than fear. And as it was the judgment of fear to deny the shepherd, so it is the office of love to feed the Lord's flock. Peter was grieved. (AUG.) Even when his sins are forgiven a man endures the misery into which nevertheless 425Ahe came out of sin. For the punishment is drawn out longer than the fault, and the fault would seem no small thing if the punishment were taken away along with it. It is therefore either for the showing of the misery that is owed, or for the amending of a slipping life, or for the exercise of necessary patience, that punishment holds a man for a time, even him whom guilt no longer holds as guilty; and this is the condition of these days, to be wept over, but not to be blamed. Feed. To feed the sheep is to comfort believers lest they fail, to provide earthly supports, if it be necessary, for those subject to one, to offer examples of the virtues, to withstand adversaries, to correct those who sin.

VERS. 18.-- But when thou shalt be old. He foretells that he will suffer, who had foretold that he would deny; strengthened by the resurrection he could now do that which, while weak, he promised 425Bbefore its time. Now he does not fear the ending of this life, because, the Lord rising again, the example of another life went before.

VERS. 19.-- And when he had said this. These things being said, Jesus began to go away; and, not yet understanding what he had heard, Follow me, Peter began to follow with the going of his feet; and John also followed, who knew that he was loved.

VERS. 20.-- Whom Jesus loved. He loved others also, but more familiarly he endowed this one with a greater sweetness of his love, because, chosen a virgin by him, he remained a virgin; whence he also commended his mother to him.

(AUG.) By the withholding of his name John is distinguished from the rest, etc., as far as John, who is a virgin, is suited to the life to come, where they shall neither marry nor be given in marriage.

426AUpon the breast. In the breast of Jesus are all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge hidden, upon which he reclined whom he endows with a singular gift of wisdom and knowledge greater than that of the rest; in which it was figured how great secret things concerning the divinity he was to write beyond the rest.

VERS. 21.-- Lord, but what of this man? Because Peter had heard that he was to be crucified, he wished to know his brother's end also.

VERS. 22.-- So I will have him to remain till I come. I will not have him consummated through martyrdom, but that he await the peaceful loosing of his flesh, when I, coming, shall receive him into eternal blessedness.

VERS. 23.-- And he did not say to him. It is not to be understood that John did not die in the flesh, but that, whatever he suffered before, he ended his last old age 426Bin peace. For it was no great thing to grant the beloved not to die, since to be dissolved and to be with Christ is far better.

VERS. 24.-- This is, etc. Here he openly designates his own person from his office, which he was unwilling to designate from his name.

VERS. 25.-- Neither do I think the world itself. (AUG.) Not by the space of places is it to be believed that the world could not contain them, etc., as far as like other tropes, that is, modes of speech.

Neither itself. He does not say that they cannot be contained by space, but by the capacity of readers; although, the faith of the facts being safe, words very often seem to exceed belief through hyperbole.

The Latin is Migne's printing of Evangelium secundum Joannem (PL 114, coll. 355B–426B), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/114/355b resolves to the first.
